# tutor — architecture

> Status: detailing finished 2026-11-01 (R59); the skeleton is from `spec`'s template, 2026-10-19.
>
> What the component does, what it does not do and what is still to decide about it. This is the detail of its card, architecture 3.6 spec. The card stays the summary: if the detail changes what the card says, the card is edited in `spec` by the same route as questions go into its registers (RULES.md, Across repositories).
>
> Why it was decided so and not otherwise is in [decisions.md](decisions.md); the work plan is in [RUN.md](../RUN.md). The concepts are concepts spec. The documents and decisions of `spec` lie in `spec/docs/` and are cited with the suffix "spec".

## 1. Purpose and boundaries

**Does:**
- AI capabilities — everything for which the platform turns to a model (concepts 10, 15 spec; architecture 3.6 spec):
  - handwriting reading and text reading: the learner's work becomes an observation — what is written, what value, whether it is legible, where on the canvas. When reading, the model does not see the key (concepts 9.1 spec; privacy 5.1 spec). Reading gives the value of an answer slot as it is written: its check kind in `module-kit` normalizes it (R56 spec). Reading gives a fraction written stacked inline, `3/4` (the 2026-10-28 amendment to R56 spec). Reading does not give a mirrored digit as a digit: the box is ambiguous, the verdict is "unreadable", and the learner rewrites the digit. So until the measurement on children's handwriting. A false alarm on it is within the goal of the first slice — no more than 1 % (the 2026-10-29 amendment to R56 spec; product 10 spec). The prompt of the workbook measurement still holds the former rule, version 1, and T99 makes version 2 for the new one (R51);
  - analysis of the course of solution: the model also sees the key and the task's solution and looks for the place of the first error in the learner's solution (privacy 5.1 spec; concepts 9.1 spec; R55 spec). The task's traps that AI may suppose, and the matched trap, the model receives as a marked data block, with the descriptions from the trap catalog (R02 spec; R57 spec);
  - the hint: words for the rung of the hint ladder that the program chose, by the rules of the hint — do not name the answer, one short question, about a place in the learner's work (concepts 10 spec; R05 spec). The words go to the device whole, after the leak check, not as a stream: a stream is only for reading, whose answer the program reads (R54 spec). What the learner's request gives before the first submission, R57 spec decided: "what is known and what to find" and the task's technique, without AI and without reading the work, and `tutor` is not called. What it gives after a wrong answer is the open О-21, and the request does not send unsubmitted work for reading between submissions either (R69 spec). If it is decided by the recommendation, "explain differently" is the same rung in other words: the hint prompt receives the past questions of the same task (privacy 4 spec), does not repeat them and does not raise the rung (R03 spec; R10);
  - assessment by the rubric — from the first slice, for the essay module. Its result is feedback, not a verdict (R41 spec; concepts 9.1 spec; R04 spec). These are two calls: reading the essay into text, then assessing the read text by the rubric's criteria, without the picture (R53; section 3.5). Later, from stage 3, voice: recognition of the learner's speech and assessment of pronunciation, and voicing — if an AI provider is to do it rather than the device's speech synthesis (Т-13; R56; section 3.8).

  Reading and analysis are two capabilities and two calls: the model sees the key and the task's solution only in analysis. One call is only for a model that passes the threshold of R03 on the handwriting of adult volunteers, by a new decision of `spec` (R67 spec; R46). Handwriting is read by two readers of one provider, by two calls without the key, and an answer slot that they read differently is "unreadable" (R68 spec; R47). An essay under a rubric is read by one reader: the second safeguards the verdict, and a rubric has none (R53). The second 2026-11-01 amendment to R68 spec decided so too: two readers are for the answer slots whose check gives a verdict. Work is read only on the learner's action — "Check" or "Step done" —, and AI is not called on a pause (R69 spec);
- holds the platform's prompts: one per capability, each with its own eval set, and for analysis and the hint, one prompt for one call, which both eval sets measure (section 3.10; row В65), and in the prompt of a capability that writes words to the learner there is a clause on gender: the tutor's words do not give away the learner's sex (R70 spec). There too is a clause on safety, and in the prompts the safety rules for children, for all learners (R54; section 3.6). A module's free text reaches the model only as a separate marked data block (R02 spec; concepts 10 spec);
- holds the providers' adapters: OpenAI and Anthropic. The terms of a provider beyond them are checked in full when the platform needs it — first Cerebras, then Groq —, and until then there is no route to it (R65 spec; section 3.12). The adapter sends handwriting to the model as a picture or a crop, without the writing history (R52 spec), and translates the place that the model named into fractions 0–1000 of the crop (R53 spec). It divides by the size of the picture sent, not of the one padded to a multiple of 28, and first clamps the box to the picture (Д33 RUN.md `spec`). Google's models are closed on the platform — both the Gemini API and Vertex AI, for all learners on both AI routes and for the other parts of the platform: Google Cloud's terms forbid generative AI in a service that people under 18 probably come to (R47 spec; architecture 3.6 spec; privacy 5.3 spec; R07 spec; sources 1.1). Whether they may be used in a separate deployment only for adults, a lawyer will decide (П-3). The reading measurement does not take Google: it does not measure models that cannot be given to children (measurements 1.7; R06, the 2026-10-21 amendment); *2026-11-10 (T129; R90): the adapters are the packages `provider/claude` and `provider/gpt`, which the service connects itself, by the field `Adapters` of `tutor.Config`, and the root package `tutor` does not build them in: a service with the models of one provider does not carry the SDK of another (Д185 RUN.md `spec`). They call only the paths that zero data retention covers: at OpenAI, Responses with `store: false` and the picture in the request body, without `/v1/files`; at Anthropic, Messages, without the Files API and Batch (R121 spec; Д150 RUN.md `spec`);*
- holds the code of the form check of the hint and of the rules of feedback by the rubric. `api` calls it before the text goes to the device, beside the leak check, and `tutor`'s eval must measure models with the same code: so admission measures exactly what the platform will check (R70 spec). A hint is exactly one question, no more than two sentences, the letters of the explanation language and no more than 30 words in Russian and 35 in English, and in a language that the measurement did not measure, without a word limit. Feedback is no more than three sentences and 80 words in English, and a stretch in quotation marks of four words or more is only the learner's words. How words and sentences are counted, this code sets. Now it holds the checks of the measurement E4, with its limits: 30 words of a hint in both languages and 60 words of feedback (R39; R45), and beside them the count by the limits of R70 spec for the reports of the extended measurement (R50). The code that `api` calls is the input of T06 and of the epic after it (R49; section 3.5); *2026-11-08 (T116; R83): this code is the package `words`: `words.Hint` and `words.Feedback` with the limits of R70 spec, and the measurement E4 calls the same code with its own limits;*
- holds the reading model on the device, if such reading comes: weights with a digest in a release of `tutor`, eval and admission. The verdict on what was read is given by `module-kit` on the server, as for an answer typed in. The code, the weights and the training data of such a model are category A of the ASF: it learns on `tutor`'s synthetic sheets and on the samples of adult volunteers, and a sample goes into training only if the volunteer's consent names it (R66 spec; the 2026-10-28 amendment to R12 spec; П-16). In the first slice there is none, and the Prompt API in Chrome is closed, like Google's models (the 2026-10-28 amendment to R47 spec);
- holds the list of admitted models — exact IDs for each capability (R07 spec). A new model is admitted when it passes the capability's eval set and the eval sets of all the published modules with this capability; an own key does not open a model without eval (architecture 4.3, 4.6 spec; concepts 14 spec);
- admits a request: an AI route — platform AI or own key — and a provider are open to the learner only if the provider's terms allow the learner's age category, and the model is among the admitted. This is one pure function: `api` calls it before an attempt, and `tutor` before each call of a model with the learner's work. It takes the rules from `spec`'s admission table, and the deployment facts from `api`'s configuration file; how it is built is section 3.9 (R57; Т-20; architecture 3.6, 4.1 spec; concepts 14 spec; R07 spec);
- reports the usage of each call — in one message for both AI routes: the model, the tokens and an estimate in dollars by its own price table. By it `api` counts the platform AI quotas (architecture 3.5, 3.6 spec). The platform does not accumulate the usage of an own key: the owner sets its spending limit at the provider, and a refusal of the provider by the limit `tutor` gives as the outcome "limit reached" (R55; section 3.7);
- holds the eval runner — a CLI on the same code (R15 spec). It counts false alarms by verdict, through `module-kit`, as in the platform's work (repositories 4 spec). From stage 2 the CI of `modules` takes it and runs it on the eval set of each module (architecture 3.10 spec). *2026-11-10 (T129; R90): the order of admission — a model from the list of admitted models of a tag of `tutor` is admitted only when a pull request of `modules` is merged that moves to this tag and runs the eval sets of all modules, and `api` takes such a tag after this merge (Д80 RUN.md `spec`; architecture 4.6 spec). While the runner is not in the CI of `modules` and there are no eval sets of modules, the list holds only the rung "author" — the first application, where only the author learns (section 3.9).*

**Does not:**
- does not give a verdict and does not choose a lesson or a task: `module-kit` gives the verdict, `engine` chooses the next lesson or task, and neither of these libraries calls a model (R03 spec; architecture 1 spec);
- does not choose the rung of the hint ladder, does not run the leak check and does not substitute a fallback text — the trap's text or advice, the module's rung template or the simpler case: `api` does this before the tutor's text goes to the device (architecture 3.5 spec; concepts 10 spec; R57 spec). The form of the hint and the rule of feedback `api` checks there too, with `tutor`'s code (R70 spec);
- stores nothing: the picture or crop of the work, sound and the own key live only in the memory of the request (architecture 3.6, 5 spec; R09 spec);
- does not receive the identifiers of the learner and of the account, the pseudonym and the adult's data — but for the own key. Its request types have no fields for them (architecture 1, 3.6 spec; R15 spec; privacy 5.1 spec);
- takes no instructions to the model from a module: the prompts belong to the platform, and a module gives only data — traps, techniques, the solution, the rubric's criteria (R02 spec; concepts 10 spec);
- does not disclose to the learner that they are talking to AI: `app` does that (architecture 3.1 spec), by the recommendation of П-15 — to all learners;
- does not voice the tutor's words while voicing is the device's speech synthesis (architecture 3.1 spec): `app` voices them, the same text that passed `api`'s checks, and `tutor` writes no separate text for speech. The tutor's words do not go speech to speech (R54 spec; R56; section 3.8);
- does not flag or track what is alarming in children's work: monitoring is only at the AI provider, and the providers' other measures for minors are section 3.6 (R54);
- in the first slice it is not a service but a library in `api`'s process (R15 spec).

**Data and links:**
- only what is needed for one call goes to the model (privacy 5.1 spec; concepts 10 spec): the work — a picture, a crop, text or sound; the task's data — the task statement, and for analysis also the key and the task's solution (R55 spec) and the task's traps with the descriptions from the catalog (R57 spec), and for assessment by the rubric the rubric's criteria and the read text of the essay, without the picture (R53; R75 spec; privacy 5.1 spec); the observations needed for the words; the presentation style. Identifiers, the pseudonym and the adult's data never go. With an own key, the key itself also goes to its provider, and the provider links each request to the billing account of the key's owner (privacy 4, 5.2 spec). And the picture may show anything that the learner wrote, a name included (privacy 1 spec);
- from `api` come (architecture 4.1 spec): for reading, the work, the open view, the age category and the AI route; for analysis and the hint, the work, the key and the task's solution, the trap, the rung, the presentation style, the explanation language, the age category and the AI route. The diagram does not name the task statement and the observations in this call, though by privacy 5.1 spec they go to the model, and by privacy 4 spec the past questions of the same task also go to the hint: the fields of the requests are `tutor`'s own types (R15 spec), and the result of detailing, T06, brought them together — section 3.10 (R59). The measurement E4 gives the call the statement, but not the observations of reading, as on the diagram (R38). The server does not store the picture, so for analysis the app sends the work again (R09 spec). To assessment by the rubric `api` gives the read text of the essay, the task statement, the rubric's criteria, the presentation style, the explanation language, the age category and the AI route, and the diagram does not draw this route (R53; section 3.5);
- an own key comes from the device with the request and in `api` and `tutor` lives only in the memory of the request — not in the database and not in logs (architecture 4.3 spec; privacy 5.2 spec; Т-9). Whom an own key is for is О-4 and П-4; the recommendation of О-4 is only for 18+. The platform's keys at the AI providers lie in Secret Manager, and `api`'s service account reads them (R15 spec; architecture 3.12 spec);
- an AI failure, an exhausted quota, an exhausted own key limit or a refusal of admission is not the learner's error: on reading the learner sees a neutral phrase, not "not yet", and on a hint the rung's fallback text (R04 spec; architecture 4.1 spec; concepts 10 spec; section 3.7);
- logs are without direct identifiers, IP addresses and content: no work, nothing read, no text of hints (R09 spec; architecture 1 spec; privacy 4 spec);
- in the first slice only `api` calls the library, in its own process; the eval runner is a CLI, and from stage 2 the CI of `modules` runs it (architecture 3.6, 3.10 spec). From stage 4 `module-pipeline` calls the library too, and then it is decided whether `tutor` is a library or a service for it (architecture 3.6, 3.13 spec; R15 spec);
- the library itself calls the AI providers — this is its only kind of input and output (repositories 1, 2.1 spec). The eval sets of modules in the CI of `modules` and the samples of adult volunteers in the measurement of stage 2 are run with a separate platform key at the AI provider — only for eval, not the key of `api`'s service account and not the author's own key. The platform pays; the provider's terms for this key are checked as in privacy 5.3 spec, and the cost of a run is named before the first start (R24 spec). A job with this key in CI, like any job with a secret, runs only on a push to `main` and on a pull request from a branch of the repository itself. On a pull request from a fork it is not skipped but fails and names the reason, and `pull_request_target` is not used (R24 spec, the 2026-10-20 amendment). Where the key lies is Т-28. Its recommendation: `infra`'s Secret Manager through Workload Identity Federation, and while there is no code of `infra` (П-11), eval runs from the devcontainer, with the key in an environment variable, not in CI; *2026-11-10 (T129; R90): Т-28 is closed by R120 spec — the eval keys lie as the secrets `eval-key-anthropic` and `eval-key-openai` of `infra`'s Secret Manager, and only a job of the GitHub environment `eval` reads them, through the pool `github-eval`, without an intermediary service account. In `tutor` this is [eval.yml](../.github/workflows/eval.yml): for now it is started by hand and spends nothing — it reads the Anthropic key and counts with it the tokens of one word —, and the runs of eval sets and the start on a pull request will come with epic E6 (Д149 RUN.md `spec`; debt Д6);*
- at build time it depends on `module-kit` — for the eval runner, and `api` depends on `tutor`. The CI of `api` checks the full list of dependencies: the AI providers' SDKs reach the build only through `tutor` (repositories 2.1, 3 spec; architecture 1 spec);
- the observation is a shared type of `tutor`, `module-kit` and eval, and the eval protocol lies in `spec` (repositories 5 spec; R15 spec; Т-7). The eval protocol and the sample format are decided by epic E3 in RUN.md of `spec`;
- the eval sets of modules are synthetic samples and samples that adult volunteers and authors made for eval; in official modules the volunteers' samples are published under the same license as the module, Apache-2.0, and the licenses of third-party modules are О-10 (R02 spec; R20 spec; concepts 3 spec; П-16). Learners' work does not go into modules, and if it is taken into eval, it lies outside the repositories and only with a separate consent (concepts 3 spec; architecture 5 spec; R15 spec; П-10). By the recommendation of П-12, development agents get no access to it. Children's samples are made on the samples page of `canvas`, like the volunteers' samples, but without a pull request (R25 spec). They go to an AI provider only with a platform key under an agreement that admits children: while П-3 has not given such an agreement, they do not go to AI (R24 spec); *2026-11-10 (T129; R90): П-3 is closed by R121 spec, and children's samples by R125 spec: they lie in the operator's closed bucket, with a separate consent, for two years from the session, and the runner does not send the provider a sample `WRITER_CHILD` while the admission table does not open to it the work of a child under 13 (section 3.4, "Samples of the measurement by `spec` `v0.14.0`");*
- stage 2. The first code of the stage is at `tutor` and `canvas`. At `tutor` it is the check board (R43 spec; repositories 6 spec; section 3.3), and after it the measurement of the course of writing, only on Claude models (R21) and with one question: whether the course of writing helps the model (R24). The model in it, with the key, assessed a finished sheet and put the box of the first error — by `spec`'s vocabulary this is reading and analysis in one call (R52 spec). This measurement was done 2026-10-26: a benefit of the course of writing is not shown (R27). So nothing of the learner's writing history goes to AI — neither into reading nor into analysis (R52 spec). R52 spec says to come back to it with a measurement on the live handwriting of adult volunteers (П-16) and on free writing. A benefit, if one is shown, only a new decision of `spec` will accept, and with it an amendment to R09 spec and privacy 4 spec and a lawyer's answer are needed. The next measurements are epic E4: free writing, reading into an observation with Т-23, hints, the workbook and essays (R28; measurements 3). This is what both the card architecture 3.6 spec and repositories 6 spec now say (the 2026-10-26 amendment to R43 spec). E4 was done 2026-10-28 on synthetic sheets and three of the author's sheets, and by its conclusions `spec` adopted R67–R71 spec and amendments to R51–R53, R56 and R57 spec (measurements 3.9; R49). Their thresholds and goals are repeated on the samples of adult volunteers (П-16). The measurement goes by each canvas kind and each kind of answer, as E2 in canvas-api. First learn whether AI reads the work well, then build the rest (repositories 6 spec; R60 canvas-api).
  - By product 10 spec the main metric of the measurement is false alarms. The measurement of the course of writing (R24) compares input forms as its main check, and false alarms in it are a condition without which a form is not counted as useful (measurements 1.1).
  - The samples page for the first measurement on volunteers' samples, by R25 spec, is without hosting: its build from a release of `canvas` opens on the volunteer's device, and the tasks and media files are put into it at build time from a release of `modules`. The samples of the measurement of the course of writing, only the author's own, were written not on it but on `tutor`'s board (R24): so the 2026-10-26 amendment to R25 spec recorded.

  The measurement began earlier, in parallel with E1, and on 2026-10-21 E1 was postponed until its conclusions (R04, the 2026-10-21 amendment). The plan and the criterion are measurements 1.1, the code and the runs are epic E2 in [RUN.md](../RUN.md) (R04). The next measurements are measurements 3 and epic E4, and E1 waited for it too (R28). E4 was done 2026-10-29, and E1 came back as the current epic (R52), and on 2026-11-01 it was done too (R59).

## 2. To decide when detailed

**Result 2026-11-01** (T06; R59). All the items are decided but the route of voice: it waits for stage 3 — subsection 2.4, and the boundary of voice is decided (section 3.8). The subsections of the decided items stand under their former numbers — the decision log and the archives of tasks refer to them —, and their text is moved into section 3 word for word: 2.1 into 3.11, 2.6 into 3.12, and 2.2, 2.3, 2.5 and 2.7 to the end of their decisions, 3.6, 3.7, 3.9 and 3.5. The card needs no edit for 2.6: its answers are R65, R66 and R68 spec (R49), and the card already names R66 and R68 spec. Below is how the section went before the result.

*2026-11-11: subsection 2.8, words after a correct answer, is opened — beyond the card, by Д188 RUN.md `spec`.*

The items "To decide when detailed" of the card — a subsection per item. Each is decided by a task of its own of epic E1 in [RUN.md](../RUN.md). The epic was postponed until the conclusions of the measurement (R04, the 2026-10-21 amendment), and from 2026-10-26 also until E4 (R28). E4 was done 2026-10-29, with the extended measurement and T99, and on the same day the epic came back (R52): subsections 2.2–2.5 and 2.7 are decided by its tasks. A decided item names its entry of the log, and the decision itself moves into section 3. A question that goes beyond this repository goes «в реестр spec» (RULES.md, Across repositories).

Since `spec` `v0.3.2` the card has four items — subsections 2.2–2.4 and 2.7. Item 2.1 — one call or two — it no longer names: R67 spec decided it, and the subsection is closed (R49). Assessment by the rubric the card got with `spec` `v0.2.32`, as its second item (R41 spec; R22), and its subsection is the next free one. On 2026-11-01 R53 decided the item — two calls —, and the decision moved into section 3.5. `spec` recorded the same in R75 spec and from `v0.5.0` no longer names the item on the rubric in the card, so the card now has three items — 2.2–2.4 (R58). On the same day R54 decided item 2.2, measures for minors, and the decision moved into section 3.6, and R55 decided item 2.3, the spending limit of an own key, and its decision is in section 3.7. Item 2.4, voice, R56 decided in part: the boundary of voice and its rules are in section 3.8, and the route of voice waits for stage 3, and the subsection stays open, with when to come back to it. Subsection 2.5 is beyond the card: its "To decide when detailed" does not name admission to AI, though among what `tutor` does the card puts the admission function and refers to Т-20 (architecture 3.6 spec). It is named by the task that set up this repository (T09 `spec`): the admission function is code of `tutor`, it is called before each request to AI, and Т-20 blocks stage 2. The card is edited for it by the result of detailing, T06 — by a row of table 3 of RUN.md «в реестр spec», when the decision of T05 is taken, and not in advance (architecture 3 spec). On 2026-11-01 R57 decided it, and the decision moved into section 3.9.

Subsection 2.6 is beyond the card too, and it is decided not by E1 but by the research of epic E2 — the tasks T41, T42 and T44 in RUN.md: how to make a check cheaper and faster. The author assigned it on 2026-10-23, and neither the card nor `spec`'s registers name such an item. The card is edited for it when there is a decision — by a row of table 3 of RUN.md «в реестр spec», as for 2.5.

Five items of seven stood on questions of `spec`'s registers — Т-23, П-15, Т-13, Т-20 and О-22 —, and `spec` closes these questions. Т-23 and О-22 it closed in `v0.3.2` — R67 and R71 spec —, and П-15, Т-13 and Т-20 are open. Here is decided what of them falls to `tutor`, and what a task finds out about the question itself goes «в реестр spec».

### 2.1. One call or two

Closed: reading and analysis are two calls (R03, R46; R67 spec). How it was decided is section 3.11, word for word (T06; R59).

### 2.2. Measures for minors

Decided — R54, section 3.6. How it was decided is at the end of section 3.6, word for word (T06; R59).

### 2.3. The spending limit of an own key

Decided — R55, section 3.7. How it was decided is at the end of section 3.7, word for word (T06; R59).

### 2.4. Voice

- **The point:** what in voice — the tutor's voicing and the learner's voice input (concepts 10 spec) — `tutor` does and what the device does, and by which route voice goes: a realtime voice API or a chain of speech synthesis and speech recognition. This is an item of the card (architecture 3.6 spec). The route is Т-13 of `spec`'s register, and its recommendation is to decide it at stage 3. So here the boundary of `tutor` and the rules that already exist are decided, and the rest may stay postponed — with when to come back to it.
- **What is known:**
  - the recommendation of Т-13: decide at stage 3, by eval and cost; the rules are the same for platform AI and own key. The argument against speech to speech: it cannot be passed through the leak check before the learner hears it (R05 spec). Begin voicing with the device's speech synthesis — only with local voices, whose attribute `localService` is true (architecture 8 spec);
  - voicing by the device's speech synthesis is an item "To decide when detailed" of the card of `app`, not decided yet (architecture 3.1 spec);
  - voice is stage 3 (product 11 spec). There is no voice input in the first slice, and no step "say" in it either: Spanish A1, which had it, did not enter the slice and stayed a module for later (R41 spec);
  - voicing is for everyone: it is output, not data collection (privacy 6 spec). Voice input depends on the age category (privacy 3.2 spec): under COPPA a voiceprint is a biometric identifier, and whether assessment of pronunciation and passing audio to a provider fall under the exception 16 CFR 312.5(c)(9) is a question for a lawyer (privacy 6 spec; П-5). The recommendation of О-6: voice input for adults from the beta, for children after the lawyer's answer;
  - voice is not stored, it lives only in the memory of the request; it goes to AI for recognition and assessment of pronunciation (privacy 4 spec). The recommendation of П-5: if voice input is allowed to children — without storage and at a provider with zero data retention;
  - assessment of pronunciation is a rubric, its result is feedback, not a verdict (concepts 9.2 spec; R04 spec);
  - a real-time channel is not needed in the first slice; it is come back to together with voice (Т-25);
  - "listen" on the text canvas `app` voices on the event `speak` — with one speech synthesis for the whole app and with one set of rules for voices (R36 spec), and by the recommendation of Т-13 these are only local voices. There is no text canvas in the first slice (R41 spec);
  - the tutor's words go to the device whole, when the whole text has passed the leak check, not as a stream; a stream is only for reading, whose answer the program reads (R54 spec). The form of the hint and the rule of feedback `api` checks there too (R70 spec);
  - `app` does not turn to AI providers (architecture 3.1 spec), and `api` turns to them only through `tutor` (architecture 3.5 spec).
- **Options:**
  - what to decide now: the boundary of voice and its rules, and the route at stage 3; the boundary and the route, with a check of the audio APIs of OpenAI and Anthropic; nothing until stage 3;
  - speech to speech for the tutor's words: close it now — it cannot be passed through the checks of `api` (R54 spec; R70 spec); leave Т-13 open as a whole, until stage 3.
- **Decided** (R56, 2026-11-01): the boundary and the rules are section 3.8, and speech to speech for the tutor's words is closed. The route of voice — recognition, voicing by a provider, the real-time channel (Т-25), eval sets and cost — waits for stage 3. Come back to it at the start of stage 3 or earlier: if a module with voice input or with the step "say" enters the plan, or if `app` chooses voicing other than by the device's speech synthesis. What this requires of Т-13, Т-25, the cards and `spec`'s vocabulary is row В63 «в реестр spec».
- **Task:** T04 — done (R56); the route of voice is at stage 3.

### 2.5. Admission to AI

Decided — R57, section 3.9. How it was decided is at the end of section 3.9, word for word (T06; R59).

### 2.6. Cost and time of a check

Decided by the research of epic E2 and the answers of `spec` (R49) — section 3.12, word for word (T06; R59).

### 2.7. Assessment by the rubric

Decided — R53, section 3.5. How it was decided is at the end of section 3.5, word for word (T06; R59).

### 2.8. Words after a correct answer

*Opened 2026-11-11 — beyond the card, from Д188 RUN.md `spec`; debt Д8 in [DEBT.md](../DEBT.md).*

- **The point:** after a correct answer the tutor praises the step and asks which technique helped (concepts 7, 10 spec). By Д188 RUN.md `spec` this is a call of `tutor` of its own, not a rung "0" of `Words`: with the work, the key and the solution, the task's techniques and a form rule of its own beside R70 spec. `api` calls it on `GetCorrectAnswerWords` and gives the words only when they have passed the leak check and the form check (`attempt.proto` of the contracts of `spec`). Two items are not decided: by which capability of the admission table this call is admitted and what form these words have.
- **What is known** (the submodule `spec` `v0.15.0`, read 2026-11-11):
  - the admission table knows six capabilities — `handwriting_reading`, `text_reading`, `solution_analysis`, `rubric_assessment`, `hint` and `voice` (`admissionv0`) —, and neither Д188 nor the contract `GetCorrectAnswerWords` names a capability;
  - a model is admitted for a capability by the eval set of that capability (architecture 4.6 spec). For the hint these are the set of words and the admission rule of words (R85, R86);
  - the rules of the hint — one question, no more than two short sentences, begin with what has already worked, only about the task (concepts 10 spec) —, and code checks the form (R70 spec): `words.HintForm`. The words after a correct answer do not give away the learner's sex either (concepts 10 spec);
  - while there is no call, `api` answers without words, with the outcome "not admitted" (Д188 RUN.md `spec`).
- **Options for the capability:** (a) a capability of its own in the admission table, with its own eval set; (b) `hint`: the hint model says these words too, and the set of words is extended with cases after a correct answer. This is a question of `spec` — row В76 «В реестр spec». *2026-11-12 (T131; R102): `spec` chose (a) — the capability `correct_answer_words` of admission table 0.3, open to all four categories, like `hint` (the 2026-11-11 amendments to R96 spec; Д214 DEBT.md `spec`). The submodule at `v0.16.3` carries it, and the tests of `admission` run its answer vector. Now only the form of the words is not decided.*
- **Options for the form:** (1) the hint rule of R70 spec as it is; (2) a rule of its own: exactly one question, as the last sentence, and before it no more than one sentence of praise of the step; the word limit of the language the same as the hint's, `words.HintWords`; the letters those of the answer's language; (3) only the word limit.
- **Recommendation:** the capability — (a): under `hint` these words would be said by a model that was not measured on them, and a capability of its own can be closed alone, without touching the hint. The form — (2): it holds by code exactly what Д188 and concepts 10 spec name — praise of the step and a question about the technique —, while (1) expects a question about the place of an error that does not exist, and (3) does not hold the question. The call, the form rule in `words`, the eval set and its paid admission are for a task after the answer of `spec` on В76, and the run not before 2026-12-01, when the account is topped up.

## 3. How it is built

Here moves what is decided by section 2: what parts the component consists of, its types and data, how it is linked with the other repositories. At first the plan of the measurement of AI reading stood here, from which subsection 2.1 was decided (R03). Now subsections 3.1 and 3.4 are how eval is built, on which the measurements went, and the measurements themselves are in [measurements.md](measurements.md) (R102; T133). The layout of the code is subsection 3.2, and the MVP — the check board — is subsection 3.3 (R19). The board showed that Claude reads a handwritten solution (R20), and the big measurement by the plan that stood in 3.1 — now measurements 1.4–1.7 — comes back, only on Claude models, to learn whether the course of writing helps the model (R21). This measurement was done 2026-10-26 (R27), and the plan of the next ones is measurements 3 (R28). Assessment by the rubric, decided by subsection 2.7, is subsection 3.5 (R53), measures for minors by subsection 2.2 are subsection 3.6 (R54), the spending limit of an own key and the usage of a call by subsection 2.3 are subsection 3.7 (R55), the boundary of voice by subsection 2.4 is subsection 3.8 (R56), and the admission function by subsection 2.5 is subsection 3.9 (R57). The result of detailing (T06; R59) brought the rest here: the request types by AI capabilities — subsection 3.10, one call or two — 3.11, formerly 2.1, cost and time of a check — 3.12, formerly 2.6. The dossiers of items 2.2, 2.3, 2.5 and 2.7 are at the end of their decisions.

### 3.1. How eval is built: the sheet, the log, the judge, the runner, the report and the formats

Until T133 the section was called «Замер чтения ИИ». The results of the measurements that stood here are in [measurements.md](measurements.md), in English, and here is what was decided from them and what code builds (R102; T133). The decision log, the archives of `docs/plan/` and `spec` refer to the blocks of the section, and they are not rewritten. Where each block that left is now:
- the notes of 2026-10-23 and 2026-10-25, «Замер хода письма» — until R29 «Замер хода решения» —, «Критерий» and «Смета» — measurements 1.1, and «Итог замера» — measurements 1.2;
- the height estimate on synthetic sheets from "Crop", the numbers of the log of parts on the sheets of code and *Tokens* from "The log of parts, version 2", and the stub run from "The runner of free writing" — measurements 1.3;
- "Plan of 2026-10-21" — measurements 1.4–1.7. Of it, the sample format, the format of the bank of tasks and the storage of samples are in force and stayed here, below.

**Rendering a sheet** (T21; R11, R24) — `internal/render`. The runner draws the pictures of a sheet for the measurement itself, from the strokes: so all the forms have one render, and synthetic sheets and the author's sheets are drawn the same way. It draws the sheet as the board shows it: the board's pen line — #111, 3 px, with round caps and joins — on white, a pixel per unit of the sheet, the whole sheet 1000 × 600, without a crop (R24). The picture holds only the strokes, without the grid and the text of the task; the PNG is grayscale, 8 bit. The render draws the sheet at any moment of writing — the points written by that moment, without the strokes erased by then:
- **the final picture** — the sheet as it was finished: all the strokes that no erasure erased;
- **the frame before an erasure** — the sheet at the moment of the erasure, but before it: the stroke that this erasure takes away is still on the frame, and what earlier erasures erased is already gone. A stroke begun in the same millisecond as the erasure is the next one, and it is not on the frame. Which frames go into a form, T22 chooses.

The picture comes out the same on any machine: the render counts only in integers. A point is placed at the nearest sixteenth of a pixel, a pixel has 4 × 4 subsamples, and its tone is the share of the subsamples that the line covers. A stroke covers a subsample once, however many of its segments pass through it, so where it runs over itself it does not darken. Floating-point arithmetic could round differently where the compiler fuses a multiplication with an addition into one operation, as on arm64, and the picture, and with it the reference outputs of the tests, would differ between architectures. So the synthetic sheets, `internal/synth`, also round each product separately. A test checks the hashes of the reference pictures of the synthetic sheets — the final one of each scenario and the frame before each erasure.

**Crop** (T69; R35) — `render.CropOf` and `render.Cropped`, by the crop rule of the eval protocol: R25 canvas as it is (Д36 RUN.md `spec`). Reading sees it (section 3.4, "Reading into an observation"), and the judge, as before, sees the whole sheet (R24):
- **the numbers** — to the bit, as at `canvas`:
  - bundles — strokes no lower than 15 units whose boxes touch or overlap, by a chain and without time;
  - `h_med` is the lower median of the heights of the bundles, and the scale takes it no higher than 100 units: so `h_med` becomes the digit height D;
  - the margin is 12 px, the side is no less than 200 px, and the crop fits the standard level of Claude: no longer than 1,568 px and no more than 1,568 tokens. Otherwise the digits are drawn smaller, at D′;
  - also where the crop lies on the sheet, `sheet_box`, and the group boxes in fractions 0–1000.

  A test checks them against the vectors of `canvas`, a copy at `internal/render/testdata/crops.json`: 34 cases at four heights. *2026-11-07 (T31; R67): the test goes by the vectors of `spec`, `contracts.Vectors` `eval/v1/crops.json` — the same cases in the form of the protocol, `CropSet` without PNG —, and the copy is gone. h_med the vectors give as it is, `bundle_median`; the scale takes it no higher than 100 units. The PNG of a crop in the protocol's sample is `Crop.PNG`: a grayscale image of 8 bits and only the chunks IHDR, IDAT and IEND, as § 11 of the contracts text of `spec` requires;*
- **the pixels** — the same render in integers. A point is placed at a 48th of a pixel, by the integers of the crop, and the line is max(D′, 12) / 12 px: 1.33, 2, 2.67 and 4 px. The ink is black, as in the crop of `canvas`. Against the raster of `canvas`, the tone of a pixel may differ by one level of gray: the vectors of `canvas` allow it. A test checks the hashes of the reference crops of the synthetic sheets at four heights;
- **the moment** — the crop takes the strokes that the sheet shows: final (`render.Shown`), before an erasure (`render.ShownBefore`) or in a state (`internal/states`). Each has its own scale, by its own strokes, as at the device;

  The rule is the protocol's, and `tutor` does not change it. To row В36 «в реестр spec» `spec` answered: the rule stays as it is — reading did not get worse from it —, and it is to be come back to on the samples of adult volunteers (epic E3 RUN.md `spec`; R49).

The render draws a sample that passed the check, on a sheet of no more than 2048 units per side (`render.MaxSide`): up to this limit its integers do not overflow. A larger sheet, a point outside the sheet and an erasure that the sample does not have, it rejects with an error.

The render counts the tokens of a picture before the call, by the Vision formula (sources 1.3): ⌈w / 28⌉ × ⌈h / 28⌉, for a sheet of the board 792. The formula is for a picture that the model does not shrink. A picture that it would shrink, the API rejects: the Claude adapter sends pictures with `oversized_image: error` (section 3.2; sources 1.7).

**The log of the course and the frames** (T22; R11, R24) — `internal/journal`. The runner builds the log and the frames itself, as the device would build them: only from the strokes and erasures, without the markup.
- **Lines** — those that `lines.Group` finds (T50), over all the strokes, the erased ones too, numbered from the top down. A line of erased strokes only is a line too, though it is not on the final picture.
- **The course** — strokes and erasures in clock order. An erasure goes before a stroke begun at the same moment, if it does not take it away, — as on the frames of the render.
- **The log** — in English: a sentence on how to read it — "How the sheet was written, step by step. Lines are numbered from the top of the sheet down, erased writing included." — and the steps by number, a step per line:
  - "Wrote line N." — writing on a line where nothing was written yet;
  - "Wrote on line N again." — writing on the line written on last, after an erasure or a pause;
  - "Went back to line N and wrote on it." — a return from another line to a line already written on;
  - "Erased K strokes on line N." — an erasure. Erasures one after another from the same line are one step, and an erasure from several lines names each: "Erased 2 strokes on line 1 and 1 on line 2.";
  - "Long pause." — a pause of 6 s or more (R11) between the end of one action and the start of the next. An erasure has only a moment, so a pause before an erasure is counted up to that moment. The wait before the first stroke is reading the task, and the log does not name it.

  The log has no time, pressure, coordinates or erased text. The text has no more than 30 steps, so that the log does not go beyond about 300 tokens. Of the rest it says in one sentence how many there are, and names a frame if one stands among them. On the synthetic sheets the log is 130–340 bytes.
- **Frames** — up to two. Each line that was erased from has a frame — before its first erasure, and the frames of the two lines with the most erased strokes are taken, and of two lines with an equal number, the one erased from earlier. The erasures of one line give one frame, and an erasure that is the first at two lines at once also gives one. Whether an erasure is by the scenario or a stray erasure, the log does not know (R11). The render draws a frame, `render.Before`.
- **Frame marks.** In form D the log says at the step of an erasure that a frame is before it: "Frame 1 shows the sheet just before this." How the captions of the pictures name the frames, the prompt decides (T19).
- **Agreement of lines** — how many lines of the markup the log found with exactly their strokes (`journal.Agree`); it goes into the run log (T23). The strokes that the lines of the markup hold are compared: an erased stroke, a label or a stray mark that the log put into a line do not count against it. The markup of a sheet starts from the board's proposal, and the same `lines.Group` gives it. So the agreement is inflated, especially on the sheets saved before the fix of lines (T51), and the report says so plainly.

**The log of parts, version 2** (T63; R11, the 2026-10-27 amendment; R30) — `journal.OfParts`, for the free writing of E4 (measurements 3.3). The log of lines above is version 1: `journal.Of` and its words do not change, and the prompt of E2 stands on it. The course, the long pause of 6 s or more, no more than 30 steps in the text and the rule of frames are the same, and the words are its own.
- **Parts** — the stroke groups `lines.Groups` (section 3.2), as the device would find them. Each stroke stands on one part:
  - a visible one — on its own group;
  - an erased one — on the group whose strokes it would be a neighbour of. If there are two such groups, on the one written on last, if that is one of the two, and otherwise on the earlier one in the order of `lines.Groups`;
  - an erased one without visible neighbours — on an **erased writing**. Such strokes are gathered among themselves by the same rule, and each of their groups is one erased writing. So it comes out for a line or a drawing erased whole and rewritten after a pause: by the rule of R24 canvas they have no group (R30). But an erased line begun next to a visible stroke no later than 2 s after it starts on that stroke's part, and further on goes as an erased writing: on the sheets of code with seed 1 this is so on three sheets of 16 with erasure.
- **Numbers** — in the order of first writing, and a stroke erased later is writing too. Parts and erased writings are numbered separately.
- **Place** — the third of the sheet's height and the third of its width where the middle of the part's box stands: "top left", "top centre", "top right", "middle left", "centre", "middle right", "bottom left", "bottom centre", "bottom right". A middle on the border of two thirds goes to the lower or the right one. Only the first step of a part names the place, and the log has no coordinates (R11).
- **The log** — a sentence on how to read it — "How the sheet was written, step by step. A part is a group of strokes written close together in place and time. Parts are numbered in the order they were first written; a part's first step says where on the sheet it stands. Erased writing, numbered apart, is erased strokes that no part holds." — and the steps:
  - "Wrote part N (top right)." — the first writing on a part, and for an erased writing "Wrote erased writing N (middle left).";
  - "Wrote on part N again." and "Went back to part N and wrote on it." — as for lines;
  - "Erased K strokes of part N." — an erasure, and from several parts "Erased 1 stroke of part 1 and 1 of erased writing 1.";
  - "Long pause.", the frame marks and the end of a cut log — the same as in version 1.

  All the words of version 2, the places too, `journal.PartsWords()` gives: the fingerprint of the prompt v2 will take them (T64).
- **Frames** — by the rule of version 1, but for parts and erased writings: an erasure of an erased writing also gives a frame.
- **Agreement** — `journal.Agree` for the log of parts compares its parts with the parts of the markup (T61).

**The judge's prompt and response schema** (T19; R11, R19, R24) — `internal/judge`. The basis is the instructions of the board (section 3.3), but for a finished sheet:
- **assessments** — `correct`, `error` and `unreadable`. There is no assessment "correct so far": the sheet is finished;
- **the box** — at `error`, the box of the first error in pixels of the first picture, the field `box`: x1, y1 — the top left corner, x2, y2 — the bottom right one. At another assessment `box` is `null`, and a box that the model gave anyway `judge.Read` drops. It puts the corners of the box in order, and the run log keeps the model's answer as it is (T23). There is no kind of mark, as on the board: a hit is counted by line (R24);
- **the key and the reference solution** the model sees, as on the board and in the analysis of R11;
- **what is erased** is not part of the solution, and every form says so;
- **the text of the instructions** is one for all the forms, and forms B, C and D add at the end only a block on their inputs: on the log, on the frames or on both. Form D has after the two blocks one more sentence, that the log marks the frames. The block of the log takes the threshold of a long pause from the log itself, `journal.Pause`. The judge's instructions share no text with the board: the prompt of the measurement is frozen with the run (T27), and an edit of the board must not change it;
- **the pictures** — each after its own caption (T16): "Image 1: the sheet as the writer left it." — in all the forms, and the frames "Image 2: frame 1, the sheet just before an erasure." and so on;
- **the text of the request** — the size of the first picture, the statement, the key and the reference solution, and in forms B and D also the log: in B without the frame marks, in D with them;
- **forms C and D** do not build a request without frames: on a sheet without frames C is not asked, and D is the same as B;
- **what the forms show** `judge.Prepare` draws from the sample: the final picture and its size, the log and the frames of the log. So the frames, the marks of the log and the size of the picture do not diverge, and a request with frames not from its own log `Form.Request` rejects.

The reference requests of the four forms on the synthetic sheet `erasure-fixed` are the files `internal/judge/testdata/A.txt`–`D.txt`, and the prompt can be read in them. **The prompt fingerprint**, `judge.Fingerprint`, is the SHA-256 of all the words that the judge reads besides the sheet and the task: the instructions of the four forms, the captions of the pictures, the text around the task and all the phrases of the log of the course. The run log keeps it: so the runs of one prompt are told apart from the runs of another, and the prompt is frozen before the test (T27). The model, the effort, thinking and the limit of response tokens the runner sets (T23).

**Versions of the prompt** (T64; R24, the 2026-10-27 amendment). Everything above is version 1, `judge.V1`, the prompt of E2. It is byte for byte the same, its fingerprint is `6a98139b…`, and `TestFrozen` holds it. Version 2, `judge.V2`, is for the free writing of E4 (measurements 3.3). A version is obtained by `judge.Prepare(s, v)`, which renders the sheet with the log of its version, and by `judge.Fingerprint(v)`, and the request of a form is built by the version of the sheet. `Form.Request` rejects a sheet with a log not of its version. The response schema, the captions of the images, the text around the task and the hit `judge.Hit` are shared by the versions. Version 2 differs in three places:
- **the text of all forms** — the same as in version 1, and three more things:
  - an item about free writing. A solution may run freely over the sheet, not in lines from top to bottom. It may have drawings — a number line with jumps, a row of objects, a scheme of segments —, working at the side, calculations in a column and the signs □ and △ in place of unknowns. All of this is part of the solution, and an error in a drawing is the same error as a wrong number (T54). The text does not name the plan's traps;
  - the reference solution describes drawings in words, and the writer may solve otherwise — with another drawing or without one;
  - the assessments speak of what is written and drawn. The box is around the writing or the drawing where the solution first went wrong, and "first" is by the order of the solution's steps, wherever they stand on the sheet. The plan places the parts the same way: in the order in which the solution is written and read ([free-sheets.md](free-sheets.md)); *2026-10-27 (T66): after the rounds of development, the box is around the wrong writing or drawing itself, tight, without the writing above and below. Of several wrong ones, the first by the order of steps, wherever it stands, and a correct step before the error is not an error.*
- **the log block** — about the log of parts (section 3.1, "The log of parts, version 2"):
  - erased writing and strokes erased from parts are not on Image 1;
  - the place of a part, as "top right", is a third of the height and a third of the width of Image 1;
  - what is judged is the writing and the drawings on Image 1;
- **the frames block** — the same, but a frame shows an erased drawing too, not only writing.

The references of version 2 are `internal/judge/testdata/v2/A.txt`–`D.txt`, on sheet 14 of the free-writing plan with seed 1: `stamps`, `erasure-fixed`, a scheme with □ and △, an erasure in it and erased writing. The fingerprint of v2 takes the words of the log of parts, `journal.PartsWords()`, and is frozen after the development of T66. The instructions of v2 are longer than those of v1: by 660–880 bytes, depending on the form. Frozen on 2026-10-27, after three rounds of development of T66: `505a799f…`, held by `TestFrozenV2`.

**The runner** (T23; R05, R06, R11, R24) — `tutor-eval run -part dev|test|all`, the packages `internal/runner` and `internal/measure` (T67, below), and the recipe `just measure` asks for the key for eval without showing it. A run is the sheets of one part of the split × the forms × three judges × the repeats:
- **the judges** — Opus 5.5 · `high`, Haiku 5.5 · `low` and Haiku 5.5 · `low` without thinking. The response limit is the same for all, 16,000 tokens, as on the board (R19);
- **the forms** — A and B for every sheet, C and D for a sheet with frames. On a sheet without frames D is B: the runner does not ask it, and the run log writes at D a reference to the answer of B;
- **the order** — repeat after repeat, so that a run stopped early has an equal number of repeats for all sheets. The default number of repeats is three;
- **the sheets** — the files that the split took. If a sheet was saved again after the split or does not pass the check, the runner does not start and names it;
- **before the start** — the number of calls, the expected cost by the prices of `provider/claude` and the tokens of the estimate (measurements 1.1), and the worst case: every call with 8,000 tokens of input and 16,000 of output. The run starts only when the author types `yes` (rule 4). The runner names a ceiling above the plan before the question. Before `yes`, Ctrl-C ends the command, and after it, stops the run before the next call;
- **the ceiling** — `-max-usd`, by default $3 for development and $6 for the test. Before each call the runner reserves its worst case: a call that could take the spending over the ceiling does not start, and the run stops. A call whose bill is unknown is counted at the worst case, as on the board;
- **the run log** — JSONL, a new file in the directory `runs` next to the directory of the sheets — for the sheets of E2 it is `samples/runs` —, outside git and for the owner only. The name is the time to the millisecond and the part, and since T67 between them also the call kind and the set — the name of the directory of the sheets, for example `20261027-214717-208-judge-free-dev.jsonl`. For reading over several sets, their names joined by "+", in the order of `-set` (T68). The runner says before the question what prevents writing it. There is no handwriting in the log. The records:
  - the run — the judges and their prices, the repeats, the ceiling, the estimate and the fingerprint of the prompt, `judge.Fingerprint`;
  - the sheet — the number, the file, the task, the scenario, the intended outcome and the first error, the number of frames and the agreement of the lines of the log of the course;
  - the call — the sheet, the form, the judge and the repeat; the model's answer as it is, the assessment and the box, the line the box hit, the failure; the tokens and thinking, the time to the first token, the retries of the SDK, the time and the cost;
  - the end — how many calls were made, how much was spent and why the run stopped early, if it did;
- **the hit** — the line of the markup that has the most points in the box with a margin of 8 px, `judge.Hit`. If there are two such lines, the box hit neither;
- **the stub** — `-stub`: it answers by the markup, as an always right judge, and takes the tokens of the estimate. A run with it goes the whole way without a key and a network. On a sheet of another outcome, for example without an answer, there is no assessment of the judge, and a call of the stub there is a failure.

The runner reads the sheets outside git. The author starts a run on the author's sheets, in `samples/`, and the agent does not look there (R05). *2026-10-27 (T55; R28): the executor itself starts a run on the sheets that code drew, in `synthetic/`, within the task's ceiling, and the log goes into `synthetic/runs`.* *2026-10-27 (R32): `samples/` is open to agents for good, so a run on the author's sheets may be started by the executor too — within the task's ceiling; who starts it, the task says.* *2026-11-07 (T104; R69): the runner refuses a sample of the eval protocol as § 13 of `spec`'s contracts text says: an unfit one — the recipe `sample-check` of `spec` finds it, `internal/samplecheck` —, one without markup, of another task, with markup not by the task, and a child sample (П-3). The run names a refused sheet before the estimate, with the reason, and asks nothing about it, and the summary names it with the reason and the line numbers of the file, without the words of the check. Refusals in the report `Report` — T106.*

**The runner of free writing** (T65; R24, the 2026-10-27 amendment). The same `tutor-eval run`, and the recipe is `just free`: it takes 40 sheets of code in `synthetic/free`, the bank and the free-writing plan, and puts the log into `synthetic/runs`. There are five differences from the run of E2:
- **the version of the prompt is by the version of the plan.** On a plan of lines the prompt is v1, on a plan of parts v2 (section 3.1, "Versions of the prompt"). The run log writes it in the run record, `prompt_version`. The logs of E2 do not have this field, and their prompt is v1, `measure.PromptOf`;
- **`-part all`** — all sheets of the plan, without the split. The run of the author's three sheets goes so (T77). Every sheet of the plan must be recorded and pass the check, otherwise the runner does not start and names the sheet. The default ceiling is $2, the ceiling of T77 (measurements 3.1);
- **the sheet record has who wrote it, the layout and the place of the first error:** `writer`, `layout` — a number line, a row of objects, a scheme of segments or working at the side — and `first_error_in` — in the drawing, in the text or at the side. The runner takes the layout and the place from the plan's sheet: `sample.PlanSheet.Layout` and `Region`. A sheet without a layout, such as the author's free solution, has no `layout`. The field `lines`, the agreement of the log of the course with the markup, compares parts in free writing, and its name is the old one, as in the logs of E2;
- **the estimate by version.** The instructions of v2 are longer, and the log of parts is shorter than the log of lines with its block. By the measure of T63 — 1.3 tokens per word, number or sign of the instructions and 1.575 per piece of the log — form A takes about 2,050 tokens of input, the log adds about 360, and a frame the same 820;
- **the stub over all the synthetic sheets** — before the first paid call: `just free -part all -stub -max-usd 10`. The stub takes the tokens of the estimate, and the ceiling of `-part all` is $2, below the estimate of 40 sheets. When the estimate is above the ceiling, the runner says so before the question: the run will stop early unless `-max-usd` gives it more.

**The runner of call kinds** (T67; R28, the 2026-10-27 amendment). There is one runner for all the call kinds of E4: the judge, reading, the hint, the slots of the workbook and the rubric (section 3.4). What is shared is in `internal/runner`, and each kind has its own package:
- **the runner** — three configurations of models, `runner.Configs` (*2026-10-28, T90: seven — four of the extended measurement added to the three of E4, and a run asks those named by the flag `-readers`; R50*), the steps of a run in the order of calls, the estimate and the worst case, the ceiling, `Do` with the run's clock and `provider.Billed`, the JSONL log and its reading, `runner.ReadLog`, and in the record of a kind `runner.ReadRecords` (T74), the stub, `runner.Stub`, and the call of models, `runner.Models`. A step carries the configuration, the tokens of the estimate, the worst input of the call and the call of the kind. A step behind which another call stands has only its record;
- **the kind** says three things about each call: its request, the answer of an always right model by the markup — this is what the stub gives — and the record of the call in the log. The records of the run and of the sheets are built by the kind too, and it reads its own log;
- **the worst input of a call** is set by the kind: for the judge, 8,000 tokens, and a request of a kind with a larger number of images or a long text may be larger. By it and by the response limit the runner reserves the worst case before a call and counts a call whose bill is unknown;
- **the judge** — `internal/measure`: forms A–D, D in place of B on a sheet without frames, the tokens of the estimate by the version of the prompt and the records of the log with the old field names. The judge's log is the same as for E2 and T66. The references `internal/measure/testdata/stub-v1.jsonl` and `stub-v2.jsonl` hold this: the runner recorded them before the rework. The judge's report is `internal/report`, the prompt `internal/judge`;
- **the kind in the log** — the field `call_kind` of the run record. The judge's log does not write it: a log without it is a judge's log. By this field `tutor-eval report` chooses the report;
- **`-kind`** — the kind of the run, by default `judge`. Since T68 there is also `reading` (section 3.4, "Reading into an observation"), since T74 `hint` (section 3.4, "Hint"), and other kinds come with their tasks;
- **the sets** — a run may go over several sets of sheets: one estimate, one "yes", one ceiling and one log, and the report sees all the sheets. The flag `-set` and the field `set` in the records came with reading (T68). The judge takes one set: its criterion counts the test part of one set.

**The report** (T24; R03, R05, R24) — `tutor-eval report LOG`, the package `internal/report`. The report is counted from the run log, without calls of the model and without the sheets: everything it needs the log already carries — the outcome and the first error of the sheet, the assessment, the box and the hit line of each answer. It writes two new files next to the log, under its name: Markdown and CSV. Which report to write is decided by the kind of the log: a log without `call_kind` is a judge's log, and the judge's report does not read a log of another kind (T67). There is no handwriting in the report — only numbers, the numbers and scenarios of the sheets, the names of the judges and the forms.
- **a right answer** — by the markup: `correct` on a correct sheet, `error` with a hit in the line of the first error on a sheet with an error. A failed call is a wrong answer, and it is also counted separately. On a sheet without frames the answer of form D is the answer of form B;
- **the criterion** — for each judge, D against A over all the sheets of the part: d of a sheet is the share of right repeats in D minus the share in A. The test is an exact one-sided sign permutation on Σd: the report counts the number of ways to assign signs to the nonzero d with a sum no less than the observed one and divides by 2ⁿ, without sampling. Then "helps", "harms" or "not shown" by the conditions of the criterion (measurements 1.1), and the overall conclusion by the Holm correction for three judges, as it was written down before the run: the p of a judge that came out "helps" is less than 0.05/3. The report names a judge that has fewer than five sheets with d ≠ 0, and its smallest p: it cannot pass the test;
- **without a threshold** — B and C against A and D against B on sheets with frames; by judge and form, the answers, the right ones, false `correct`, false alarms, `unreadable`, failed calls and the sheets where the repeats diverged; false alarms on `erasure-fixed` and right answers on `erasure-broken`; the time p50 and p95, the time to the first token and the cost of a call; the agreement of each judge with the first, Opus 5.5 · `high`, on "correct" — how many of its `correct` answers Opus also called `correct` — and the share of `unreadable` (section 3.12);
- **CSV** — a row per judge and form, with the same numbers.

**The report of free writing** (T65; measurements 3.3). For a log of prompt v2 the report counts everything above, and three more sections, without a threshold:
- **boxes on sheets with an error** — by judge and form: the answers, how many of them are `error`, how many have a box and how many boxes are on the first error. A box on a drawing counts, on whichever of its elements it stands: a drawing is one part. Beside it, the test of D against A only on the sheets with an error. These are data for R52 spec: whether to come back to the course of writing;
- **the breakdown** — right answers out of answers, by form, for each judge:
  - by layout: a number line, a drawing — a row and a scheme together —, working at the side, and for sheets without a layout, "none";
  - by the place of the first error: in the drawing, in the text, at the side;
- **by sheet** — for each sheet and judge, right answers out of answers by form, repeats together, with who wrote the sheet, its task, scenario, layout and first error.

The report of a log of E2 does not change in a single byte. This is held by a test on the log of the stub that the runner of E2 recorded before T65: `internal/report/testdata/e2-stub.jsonl` and its report.

The report also takes the log of a run that broke off or of a run stopped by the ceiling, and says so at the start. It does not read the last line, broken off mid-word. The log marks as such a call whose request could not be built, and it is not counted as made.

**Plan of 2026-10-21** is history, and its text is in measurements 1.4–1.7 (R29; R102; T133). Of it, what is in force is the storage of samples, the sample format and the format of the bank of tasks — they are below.

**Samples** (R05):
- **storage** — the directory `samples/` in the working copy, outside git, as R65 canvas-api. The development agents do not look there (the recommendation of П-12), and the author starts a paid run. Three things hold this:
  - the rule in CLAUDE.md: «агенты не открывают `samples/`». By AGENTS.md it binds any agent, Codex too;
  - `samples/` in `.gitignore`;
  - a `deny` rule for Claude Code in `.claude/settings.json` — both for reading and for shell commands.

  All three are set by the first task of E2 with code, before the first sample: the page for recording samples will not come earlier. The research T08 writes no code and does not touch samples. Inside the container this is a rule, not an access boundary, so the author works with the samples themselves. Only the summary report without handwriting gets into the repository. Tests run on synthetic strokes that the code draws.

  *2026-10-27 (R32): the author opened `samples/` to agents for good. The rule in CLAUDE.md that agents do not open it and the `deny` rules are gone. What holds is `.gitignore` and the rule that only numbers and words go from the samples into the repository, without handwriting. In `samples/` there is only the author's handwriting, and the volunteers' samples are not opened until that is decided anew.*

  *2026-10-27 (T55; R28): the sheets that code draws for the measurements of E4 lie outside `samples/`, in the folder `synthetic/` outside git, and it is open to the agent: there is no handwriting in it (section 3.4, "Sets — where they lie"). A line of `.gitignore` and its own section of CLAUDE.md hold it, and the board does not write there (section 3.2).*

**Sample format** (T12; R15). A sample is one sheet, a JSON file. The format is temporary: the eval protocol from `spec` will replace it, and the protocol takes this format as its basis (epic E3 of RUN.md of `spec`; R16). While there is no protocol, the measurement samples from the page of `canvas` come in the sample format of `canvas` (R38 spec), and this format is for `tutor`'s synthetic sheets and for the recording page, if T13 comes back. *2026-10-25 (R24): the samples of the measurement of the course of the solution are written in this format by the board (T13), and the synthetic sheets move to a sheet of 1000 × 600 (T21).* *2026-11-06 (T30; R65): the eval protocol came out — the message `Sample` of the package `novatrail.eval.v1` (R93, R95 spec) —, and the runner moves to it by task T31: what the protocol took from this format and what it set otherwise is named in its body in RUN.md.* *2026-11-07 (T31; R67): the files of the protocol are read and written by `internal/protocol`, and this format lives until the synthetic sheets and the runner (T104) and record mode with the author's sheets (T105) have moved to the protocol.* A reader takes only its own version. Every field of any object is required and is written exactly, in its case, once; `null` is allowed only for `injection`, `planned` and `first_error`, where there may be no value, and an unknown field is an error. The check names every error by the path to the field — `strokes[3].points[12].t_ms` — and in words, and lists them all, not the first. *2026-11-07 (T104; R69): `tutor-eval draw` writes the synthetic sheets as samples of the protocol (below, "The protocol's sample for synthetic sheets"). This format stays for the record mode of the board and the author's sheets, until T105, and the summary, the split and the runner read both: which one, the file's key `protocol` says.* *2026-11-07 (T105; R75): record mode writes samples of the protocol, and the sheets in `samples/` and the set `synthetic/free` are converted to the protocol into the directories `-v1` (below, "The author's sheets on the protocol"). Only the converter reads this format, `internal/convert`: the summary, the split and the runner name a v0 file a problem of its sheet, and `tutor-eval check` refers to `tutor-eval convert`. There is no more pressure in the in-memory model, `sample.Point`.*
- `format` — always `tutor-sample`, `version` — `0`.
- `sheet` — the size of the sheet in logical units, `width` and `height`. Logical units are the same on any device: x grows to the right, y downward, and a unit is a pixel of the picture at scale 1, as in canvas-api (R15 canvas-api). A synthetic sheet is 1000 × 600, the board's sheet (T21).
- `task` — the ID of the task in the bank.
- `writer` — who wrote: `author` — the author in their own handwriting, `author-as-child` — the author imitating a child's handwriting (R08), `synthetic` — the code of `tutor-eval draw`, without a hand (R26).
- `scenario` — what the sheet is written for, one of the list "Scenarios" above:
  - `correct` — a correct solution;
  - `error` — a typical error;
  - `similar-digits` — similar digits;
  - `illegible-crossed-out` — illegible and crossed out;
  - `answer-not-last` — the result not last;
  - `nearly-empty` — a nearly empty sheet;
  - `mirrored-digits` — mirrored digits;
  - `erasure-fixed` — «ошибка стёрта и исправлена, итог верный»;
  - `erasure-broken` — «верный шаг стёрт и заменён неверным»;
  - `injection` — a handwritten injection.
- `strokes` — the strokes in the order of writing: the next starts no earlier than the previous one ended. A stroke has `id`, unique on the sheet, and the points `points`, at least one. A point is `x` and `y` on the sheet, `t_ms` — milliseconds from the opening of the sheet, non-decreasing, and `pressure` — pressure from 0 to 1; without pressure, with a mouse, 0.5.
- `erasures` — the erasures in the order of time. The eraser removes a stroke whole (architecture 3.2 spec), so an erasure is the time `t_ms` and the IDs of the erased strokes `strokes`. Only a finished stroke can be erased, each one once, and not in the middle of another stroke. An erasure by the scenario has the markup `planned`, a slip has none:
  - `line` — the number of the line written in place of what was erased;
  - `erased` — what is erased: `mistake` — an error that was fixed, or `correct-step` — a correct step replaced by a wrong one;
  - `text` — what was written by the erased strokes.

  An erasure by the scenario is exactly one on the sheets `erasure-fixed` and `erasure-broken` and none on the others.
- `lines` — the lines of the solution in the order in which they are to be read. The markup refers to them by numbers from 1. A line has:
  - `text` — the intended text, in the notation of the bank's solutions;
  - `strokes` — its strokes, only the visible ones: an erased stroke belongs to nothing, and a visible one to no more than one line or inscription;
  - `illegible` — deliberately illegible: reading must ask to rewrite it;
  - `crossed_out` — crossed out: it is not part of the solution, and the line through it is its stroke too;
  - `answer` — the result. It is one on a sheet, wherever it stands, and it is absent only on a sheet without an answer. On a sheet `answer-not-last` one more line follows the result (T49).

  A stroke that belongs to neither a line nor an inscription is allowed — for example, a line under the answer.

  *2026-10-27 (T56): on a sheet of a plan of version 1, the sample's lines are the parts of the writing in the order of reading, and for a drawing the text of the line is its description in words ("Format of the sheet plan" below). The sample format does not change from this.*
- `injection` — only on a sheet `injection`: the `text` of the inscription, its language `lang` — `ru` or `en` — and its strokes `strokes`.
- `expected` — which outcome is intended:
  - `outcome` — `correct`: all lines and the result are correct; `error`: there is a wrong line; `unreadable`: the result is deliberately illegible, and reading must ask to rewrite it; `no-answer`: there is no answer;
  - `first_error` — the number of the first wrong line, only with `error` and not crossed out, otherwise `null`;
  - `trap` — the ID of the task's trap that the error falls into, only with `error`; otherwise, and for an error outside the traps, an empty string.

  An illegible result and the outcome `unreadable` go only together: reading such a result asks to rewrite it, and there is no verdict.

  The scenarios `correct` and `erasure-fixed` are intended with the outcome `correct`, `error` and `erasure-broken` with `error`, `nearly-empty` with `no-answer`. For the others the markup names the outcome itself. On a sheet with an injection the outcome is the same as it would be without it: the inscription must change neither the reading nor the verdict.

What a sample does not have: the picture — the runner renders it from the strokes (T21); the part, dev or test — the sheets are split by scenario before the first edit of the prompt (T27), after the recording; names and signatures.

**The protocol's sample for synthetic sheets** (T104; R69) — the message `Sample` of the package `novatrail.eval.v1` (§ 11 of `spec`'s contracts text; R93, R95 spec): a sheet that `internal/synth` drew is written by `internal/evalsample`, and the file by `protocol.MarshalSample`. The file name is the old one, `NN-YYYYMMDD-HHMMSS-mmm-task-scenario.json`: by it the summary, the split and the runner find the plan's sheet, and with it the scenario, the planned erasure and the trap, which the protocol does not name.
- **who and what for** — `purpose` `PURPOSE_MEASUREMENT`, `writer` `WRITER_ADULT` — the synthetic hand stands for an adult —, and `synthetic` with the seed of `draw`. The ID is `synthetic-<seed>-<plan>-<NN>`, by the name of the plan's file, and for a sheet with a non-default shift of the near key also `-shift-<thousandths>` before the number: from the seed, as § 11 says, and with no collisions between two sets of one seed. The writer code is the first 8 hexadecimal digits of the SHA-256 of the string `tutor-synthetic:<seed>`, in upper case: one for all the sheets of one seed, one "hand". There is no `license`;
- **`canvas_version` and `text_set_version`** — `0.0.0` and `none`: the values of no release, which § 11 requires of a file recorded not by the samples page, and `sample-check` requires them of synthetic sheets (the 2026-11-07 amendment to R93 spec — the answer to В68; T110, R72);
- **the task** — the source `file`: `bank` — the path of the bank from the root of the repository — the nearest folder with `go.mod` above the bank, and outside a repository the file name alone —, `file_digest` and `bank_digest` — `sha256:` of its file, the ID of the task and the digest of its open view, `module-kit`'s `openview.TaskDigest` over the task of the format `task.Sheet` (R68). So the reference works for banks that are not in the file of open views too: the generated ones and those of the near key;
- **the writing history** — strokes with IDs from 1 in the order of writing, points to a tenth of a unit, `pointer` `pen`; a stroke is recorded at the moment of its last point, an erasure at its own, and within one millisecond a stroke goes before an erasure. There is no pressure;
- **the sheet** — 1000 × 600, the digit height `render.Digit`, 32; the groups — `lines.Groups` over the strokes that the history shows; the crops — 16, 24, 32 and 48 px with PNG, `evalsample.CropSet`. The test of `spec`'s examples calls the same code and gets their numbers;
- **the markup** — parts by the lines of the sheet: text or a drawing by the plan's part, strokes, `crossed_out`, `illegible` and `final_answer` on the answer, if not crossed out, and the first error. The truth of the only answer slot is the value of the answer, `sample.ValueOf`, or a reason: an empty sheet — `UNREADABLE_BLANK`, no answer — `UNREADABLE_NO_FINAL_ANSWER`, two answers — `UNREADABLE_SEVERAL_FINAL_ANSWERS`, an illegible one — `UNREADABLE_ILLEGIBLE`. A sheet with a crossed-out part is marked `crossed_out`. A sheet whose answer gives no number — as in the plan of the author's sheets, where the answer is the whole solution — `draw` does not draw: the author's hand writes it;
- **back** — `evalsample.Read`: the strokes and erasures are `protocol.Ink`, the lines are the parts, the outcome is the verdict of `module-kit` by the truth and the task's key, the first error comes from the markup, the scenario, the trap and the planned erasure from the plan's sheet. `draw` reads every file back and checks that it is the plan's sheet, and `sample-check` in CI holds the samples of `testdata` (section 3.2).

**The author's sheets on the protocol** (T105; R75). The sheets that the board's record mode writes, and the `tutor-sample` v0 sheets that `tutor-eval convert` converts, are `Sample` messages too, as for the synthetic sheets above, and they differ in this:
- **who** — `person`, `writer` `WRITER_ADULT`, for a sheet "as a child" `WRITER_ADULT_AS_CHILD`; the ID is random, 26 lowercase letters and digits, `evalsample.NewID`; the writer code comes from the file `samples/writer-code`, outside git: the first recording or conversion makes it random, 12 digits and capital letters, and after that both read it, `evalsample.WriterCodeAt`.
