For a directory outside `samples/` — a test — the default code file is next to the directory, and a person's sheet is written into no directory of `synthetic/`. `canvas_version` and `text_set_version` are `0.0.0` and `none`, as § 11 requires of a sheet recorded on the board or converted from the earlier file;
- **the history** — for the board, the events as the page sent them: `write` with the browser's `pointer`, `erase`, `undo` and `clear`; for a converted sheet, the strokes and erasures of v0, and `pointer` is `mouse` when every point has the pressure 0.5, and `pen` otherwise. Pressure is not written;
- **the markup** — as for synthetic sheets, and every visible stroke is in a line or a part: the board does not save a sheet with a stroke outside them, and the converter refuses it. The truth of the answer is the value by `sample.ValueOf`, and for a plan sheet of one part, the author's free solution, it is the value of the key for a sheet marked correct, and for a sheet with an error the number after the last word «ответ» in the text of the markup — a separate word, not inside «ответа». A correct sheet whose text names another number, a sheet with an error without a number or with the key's number, and a sheet with another outcome without the mark "illegible" are refused: the markup gives no truth. Code does not draw such a sheet;
- **the sheets of code in v0** — the 39 sheets of `samples/sheets` and the set `synthetic/free` — the converter writes as `draw` writes them from the seed, `evalsample.Of`, byte for byte, if the current `synth` draws them the same with the same seed; otherwise, a refusal;
- **the directories** — `<set>-v1` next to the old one: `samples/sheets-v1`, `samples/free-v1`, `samples/free-messy-v1` and `synthetic/free-v1`, with the old file names, so that the `split.json` of the old directory, which the converter copies, names the same sheets. The old directories do not change. The converter writes sheets from `samples/` only into `samples/`. `sample-check` checks each file before it takes its name when the directory is in the working tree, and a repeated launch writes nothing.

**Samples of pages and essays** (T107; R71) — `Sample` messages too, as for the sheet above: a measurement, an adult, `synthetic` with a seed, the writer code, `0.0.0` and `none`, the writing history with `pointer` `pen` and the task — the source `file`. They differ in the surface, the markup and how the runner finds its own in the file:
- **the files** — `page-NN.json` and `essay-NN.json`, by the number of the page or the essay in `eval/workbook.json` or `eval/essays.json`, in their own directory: by default, `synthetic/` and the file name, `synthetic/workbook`. `tutor-eval draw -workbook F` or `-essays F` with `-seed`, `-dir` and `-png` draws them, only those not in the directory, and reads each back. The directory is in `synthetic/`: the handwriting is code's, and `samples/` is for the author's handwriting. `draw` writes outside the working tree too, but the summary and the run will not read such a directory: `sample-check` takes only paths of the repository (§ 11). Samples of different seeds in one directory are a refusal: the run would not tell them apart. The ID is `synthetic-<seed>-workbook-<NN>`, as for a sheet, by the file name;
- **the task** — `task.Page` or `task.Essay`, `workbook-NN` or `essay-NN`, and the digest of its open view. By the task's ID a sample finds the page or the essay of the file, and from the file the runner takes what the protocol does not carry: the box's scenario, the written and the crossed-out values, the writing of the fraction, the errors in the words and the part;
- **the page** — the surface `workbook`: 1000 × 1400, the digit height `render.Digit`, each box of the page in the order of the task — 100 × 100 at the place where `synth` puts it —, and the filled ones, with crops by the box's strokes: the table "box → strokes" is given by the box rule, `protocol.Table`, over the final strokes of the history, `protocol.FinalOf`. `draw` fails if the rule gave other boxes than code drew. The markup is the truth of each box: an empty one — `UNREADABLE_BLANK`, a mirrored one — `UNREADABLE_AMBIGUOUS` with the mark `mirrored`, a rewritten one — the value left with the mark `crossed_out`, the others — the written value; neither parts nor criterion marks;
- **the essay** — the surface `sheet`, 1000 × 1400, with groups and crops, as for a sheet. The truth of the only answer slot is the title, a line break and the text, `sample.Essay.Written`; the marks are all the criteria of the rubric by place, met unless the file names it unmet;
- **back** — `evalsample.ReadPage` and `ReadEssay` refuse by § 13, as `Read` of a sheet does: no markup, another task, markup not by the task — the truth is not an observation of the task by `module-kit`, for a page parts or marks, for an essay parts, a criterion outside the rubric or marks on an unreadable one —, and a child sample. A sample that got no refusal but diverges from the file — the truth, the marks, the boxes — is a problem of the page or the essay: a path without values. `evalsample.ReadPagesDir` and `ReadEssaysDir` read the directory and check it with `sample-check`;
- **the crops for the model** the runner cuts by the sample's strokes, as for a sheet, and does not take the sample's PNG: they are byte for byte those that were drawn in memory before T107 (the test `TestPages` and `TestEssays` of `internal/evalsample`). Nor does the runner take the PNG of a `canvas` sample, where the grey level may diverge: for them too it cuts the crops by the strokes (T32; R76);
- **`testdata`** — page 5, with all seven box scenarios and both writings of the fraction, and essay 16, under the same test and `ci-sample-check` as the sheets.

**The runner's report `Report`** (T106; R74) — the message `Report` of the package `novatrail.eval.v1` by § 14 of `spec`'s contracts text. The CI of `modules` and the admission rule read it, and the log, Markdown and CSV stay with `tutor`. `tutor-eval report` writes it from the run log, next to the log:
- **the files** — `<log>.<reader>.report.json` for each reader alone, with all its repeats, and `<log>.<strong>+<fast>.report.json` for each pair — the one that reads once, with each that reads in repeats. `module-kit`, `observation.Join`, joins the pair, repeat to repeat, and a pair has the smaller of the two numbers of repeats. The measures of § 14 over all the Reports are in `<log>.protocol.csv` and in the Markdown section "The runner's reports of the protocol (§ 14)";
- **what is taken** — what the platform will ask: a sheet without the key, a workbook page without captions, finished work, digits of 32 px. The capability is `CAPABILITY_HANDWRITING_READING` for the sheets and the boxes of the workbook and `CAPABILITY_RUBRIC_ASSESSMENT` for the essays;
- **the header** — `runner`, the tag of `tutor`, and `verdicts`, `VERDICT_CODE_MODULE_KIT` with the version of `module-kit`, — from the build of the run, its record `runner` and `module_kit`. A build not from a tag is `v0.0.0`, as in `spec`'s examples: it is not a release. The reader is the provider by the code of the admission table, `anthropic`, and the model with its setting, `claude-haiku-5-5 low` or `claude-haiku-5-5 low no-thinking`. The set is `capability_set` with a name from the run's sets joined by "+" and the part: `lines+free+near-dev`, `workbook-test`, `essays-all`;
- **the samples** — in ascending order of ID. The refused ones come with the reason of § 13, from the run record `refused`, and a file without a readable ID only in Markdown. The measured ones carry:
  - for a sheet and a page — each answer slot that the model reads: for a page only the filled boxes, with the verdict of `module-kit` by the truth and the marks `mirrored` and `crossed_out` from the record of the sheet or the page `truth`. The reading of a repeat is the verdict of `module-kit` on the observation from the call record, `reading.RecordObservation` and `workbook.RecordObservation`, in the words of `module-kit`. "Unchecked" comes with a cause: `UNCHECKED_CAUSE_INVALID` for an answer outside the schema, the field `invalid` of the call record, and for a workbook answer without an answer slot of the box or with two; `UNCHECKED_CAUSE_FAILURE` for any other failure, and for the provider's limit too — its answer cannot be told from a failure; `UNCHECKED_CAUSE_LIMIT` for a repeat the run did not reach, stopped by the limit `-max-usd`, and for an interrupted run it is a failure; `UNCHECKED_CAUSE_CHECK` for an answer slot whose check gave no verdict on a valid observation: the predicate gave an error or not a `bool`, — this is a cause of the task, not of the model (§ 14 of `spec`'s contracts text, since `v0.9.10`; T128, R87). Without an observation, when the pair does not join and when `module-kit` refuses to check, it is still a failure, and `tutor`'s own checks do not call predicates;
  - for an essay — the people's marks from the markup, the record `people`, and the truth when it is without text; for each repeat, the AI's mark for each criterion of the people: "met", "unmet" or `NONE` for a criterion "unchecked", — and without a single mark `UNCHECKED_CAUSE_NO_MARK`;
- **the measures** — `report.MeasuresOf` over the records of the Report, by the rules of § 14: for reading, the false alarm, the false "correct", the extra and the kept "unreadable", another trap and "unchecked" by cause, all of it also separately for the mirrored and the crossed-out; for essays, the extra "unreadable", the false alarm, agreement, the marks `NONE` and Cohen's kappa by criterion. The earlier tables of the reports — the key, the heights, the states, the captions, the pairs of R08 — stay: the Report does not carry their data. *2026-11-08 (T111; R77): the admission rule, when it is written, counts no measures on the first error and the essay marks of a synthetic sample: code chose them by the seed, and they say nothing about the solution (the 2026-11-08 amendment to R95 spec; § 11 and § 14 of `spec`'s contracts text).*;
- **a log without sample IDs** — of past runs and of the author's `tutor-sample` v0 sheets — gives no Report, and its report for people is as before. The repository cannot yet check its Report with a recipe of `spec` (row В69 of RUN.md): a test of `tutor` reads it strictly, `protocol.ReadReport`, and holds the rules of § 14 itself. *2026-11-08 (T111; R77): `spec` writes the recipe `report-check` in epic E7 (the second 2026-11-08 amendment to R95 spec), and the CI of `tutor` will call it on its Reports when E7 comes out in a release.* *2026-11-09 (T128; R87): the recipe came out in `v0.9.12`, and `canvas-check` calls it on the reports of `canvas`'s synthetic sheets — below, "`canvas`'s synthetic sheets".*

**`canvas`'s synthetic sheets** (T32; R76) — the samples of the protocol that the code of `canvas` draws and that come out as the archive `synthetic-samples_v<X.Y.Z>.tar.gz` of its release (R38 spec). The CI of `tutor` checks that the runner reads them, without a key and without spending:
- **the release** — pinned in `justfile` by the tag and the sha256 sum of the archive, `CANVAS_TAG` and `CANVAS_SYNTHETIC_SHA256`: now v0.1.6, 16 samples `synthetic-101` … `synthetic-116`. The signature of the archive was checked by `spec`'s `release-verify` at pinning, and from then on the sum holds the file. A new tag is pinned the same way: two lines, `release-verify` of the new archive and a green `just canvas-check`;
- **the task** — a sample names its source "a release of `tutor`": the tag, the digest of the file of open views, the bank and its digest. The runner takes the bank with the keys at this tag, from the tree `eval/` of the tag, which the recipe lays out by `git archive` into `synthetic/tutor-trees/<tag>` — it takes the tags from the samples themselves, and fetches a missing tag without `--depth`, so that a full clone does not become shallow —, and compares both digests with the sample (`evalsample.Releases`). A bank edited after the tag does not affect the samples;
- **the purpose** — the capability's set, `tutor`'s (§ 13): measurement samples and eval-set samples are read, child samples are refused until П-3, and the check counts this as expected;
- **the sheet** — without a plan sheet: the plan is taken from the sample, `evalsample.ReadSheet` — the task from the reference, the parts of the markup, the outcome by the verdict of `module-kit`, the first error from the markup, the trap from the verdict, and there is no scenario. The runner reads the final geometry of eval-set samples and child samples by the visible strokes, without erasures and time. A sheet without strokes does not go to the model: it has no crop, as an empty box of the workbook. This rule is shared — the author's sheets have it too, where such a sheet used to crash the run;
- **the page** — is marked up anew by the sample's truth, `evalsample.ReadMarkedPage`: the checks and keys are the workbook file's, the values are the sample's, a crossed-out value is "rewritten", a mirrored digit is the key with a mirrored digit. A markup that the workbook run cannot pass on is a problem of the sample, and so is a second page with the same number: the run and the report of the workbook know a page by its number;
- **the run** — `tutor-eval canvas`: the sheets by a reading run, the pages by a workbook run, both on the stub, with their own logs in `synthetic/runs` and reports next to them, and the command joins the reports of one reader or pair into one Report of the set, `<time>-<set>.<reader>.report.json` (`report.Joined`);
- **red** — a sample that `sample-check` finds breaking a rule, refused other than as a child sample, not read, or a report does not measure a read sample, or the report has a reading "unchecked". The recipe `canvas-check-test` holds this: a spoiled sample of the set — zeros in the digest of the open view — the check refuses as another task;
- **the PNG of the crops** of `canvas`'s samples is not taken by the runner: it cuts the crops by the strokes, `internal/render` (R71);
- **`spec`'s report** — the check `checkReport` and `sampleBreaches` accepts the reports of the set, except for the box of an empty sheet: its rule expects a box on every sheet — В70 «в реестр spec». *2026-11-08 (T111; R77): `spec` v0.9.9 accepted this rule — a sheet without strokes is measured without answer slots (the third 2026-11-08 amendment to R95 spec) —, and its check accepts all 5 reports of the set whole.* *2026-11-09 (T128; R87): `canvas-check` calls `spec`'s recipe `report-check` itself — the reports of the set with its 16 samples after `--samples`, the reports of the sheets and pages without samples —, and the logs of its runs are in `synthetic/runs/canvas-check`, anew at every check.*

**Format of the bank of tasks** (T12; R15) — a JSON file: `format` — `tutor-bank`, `version` — `0`, and `tasks` — the tasks, as a task of a module (concepts 6, 7 spec):
- `id` — unique in the bank;
- `kind` — `linear-equation`, `calculation` or `free-solution`: the three kinds of tasks of the plan of 2026-10-21 (measurements 1.4), and how the lines are compared depends on the kind. *2026-10-26 (T15): the program compares the lines for none of the kinds — the check by lines went with R19, and the markup sets the first error (R24).*
- `statement` — the statement, in Russian;
- `key` — the key, in the notation of the last line of the solution;
- `solution` — the correct solution by lines;
- `traps` — the traps: `id` and `description`;
- `leaks` — leak phrases beyond the key itself: the leak check looks for them in every hint;
- `false_keys` — false keys, at least one: `key` and `solution` — the same steps with another number, as many lines, but not a correct solution (R03).

The bank of the measurement of the course of writing is `eval/bank.json` (T15). The same error in different tasks of the bank has one trap ID and one description, as a trap of a module (concepts 7 spec). The bank is checked by `tutor-eval check` and the test of `cmd/tutor-eval`. The same test compares the board's tasks with their copies in the banks: five tasks of the board are in this bank, and six with free writing in the bank `eval/free-bank.json` (T52; measurements 3.1). The test holds the rule "one error — one ID and one description" in both banks.

**Format of the file of open views** (T37; R64) — a JSON file that the release of `tutor` carries for the samples page of `canvas`, `tutor_<version>_open-tasks.json`, a copy of `eval/open-tasks.json`. It holds what the learner sees in a task of the measurement, and nothing of what `tutor` holds (R05 spec):
- `format` — `tutor-open-tasks`, `version` — `0`, `module_format_version` — `"0.3"`: the format of the module whose open views of tasks are in the file. Before T30, `"0.2"`: format 0.3 changed only `$id` in the task schema, and a v0 reader reads only the current version (R65);
- `banks` — the task files in order: the bank of T15, the bank of free tasks, the workbook pages and the essays (section 3.4). Each has:
  - `bank` — the path in the repository;
  - `digest` — `sha256:` and the SHA-256 of its bytes, as the format writes a digest;
  - `language` — the language of the texts, `ru` or `en`;
  - `tasks` — the open views of its tasks, the type `open.Task` of `spec`'s Go module (R72 spec): `id`, `statement`, `canvas` and `items`.

A task of a bank is a sheet: the statement, the canvas `handwriting_sheet` with the default grid and one answer slot `anywhere`. A workbook page is `workbook-01` and onward: the statement «Впиши ответы в окошки.», the canvas `workbook` and a line `line` per item, where `[ ]` is a box `box`. An essay is `essay-01` and onward: the topic, the canvas `handwriting_sheet` with the grid `lines` and one answer slot `sheet`. There are no keys, solutions, traps, leak phrases, false keys or checks in the file. A sample of a page will name the task by the release tag and the sha256 of the file, by the bank with its `digest` and the task ID, and the runner at the same tag will find the key by them. `just open-tasks` writes the file, and `internal/openview` reads and checks it: only in the form in which the command writes it and by the rules of the format that are not in the types, — and the test compares their templates with the schemas of `spec`'s module. Pictures, `givens`, `unknown`, zones and choice never occur in the file: the tasks of the measurements have none. *2026-11-07 (T103; R68): every task of the measurements has a task of the format with checks, `internal/task`, and `module-kit`, `openview.Task`, builds the file's open view from it: the file stayed the same byte for byte. The digest of the open view by which a sample of the protocol names the task is `module-kit`'s `openview.TaskDigest`.*

The synthetic bank — a task of each kind — and a synthetic sample of each scenario are drawn by code, `internal/synth`, not by a hand: there is no handwriting in the repository. The strokes of a synthetic sheet go in a simple vector font, with time, pressure and a small jitter. Letters that the font lacks — for example, the inscription of an injection — are drawn as a wavy line: tests need strokes where the text is, not a legible alphabet. Every sample comes out the same at every launch.

**Format of the sheet plan** (T49; R15, the 2026-10-26 amendment; R25) — a JSON file: `format` — `tutor-sheet-plan`, `version` — `0`, and `sheets` — the sheets in the order in which the author writes them. The plan says what to write on each sheet, and by that sets its markup: the board in record mode fills it in from the plan (R25). The plan is read as strictly as a sample and a bank; `null` is allowed only for `erasure` and `first_error`. A sheet has:
- `number` — the number of the sheet, in order from 1;
- `task` and `scenario` — as in a sample. The plan does not say who writes: in the measurement of the course of writing the author writes all the sheets in their own handwriting, `author` (R24);
- `lines` — the texts of the lines that stay on the sheet at "Done", from top to bottom, in the notation of the bank's solutions;
- `answer` — the number of the line with the result;
- `expected` — the intended outcome, as in a sample: `outcome`, `first_error` and `trap`;
- `erasure` — the erasure by the scenario, only on the sheets `erasure-fixed` and `erasure-broken`, otherwise `null`:
  - `line`, `erased` and `text` — as in the markup `planned` of a sample: the line written in place of what was erased, what was erased and what was written;
  - `replacement` — what is written in place of what was erased;
  - `part_of_line` — only part of the line is erased, for example one number: then `text` and `replacement` are this part, and the rest of the line stays.

The lines of a sheet in the plan are only texts, and every sheet has an answer line. The plan has no inscription of an injection and no marks of an illegible and a crossed-out line. So the scenarios `illegible-crossed-out`, `nearly-empty` and `injection` and the outcomes `unreadable` and `no-answer` are not in a plan of version 0: the check names what such a sheet lacks. If such sheets are needed, a new version of the format will bring them.

Any other sheet of the plan is checked as the markup of a sample. A stub sample is built from the sheet — a stroke per line and one more, erased, if the sheet has an erasure by the scenario —, and `Sample.Check` checks it. So all the markup rules of "Sample format" act on the plan: the outcome by the scenario, the first error and the trap, one erasure by the scenario and its kind, a line after the result on the sheet `answer-not-last`. An error is named by the path to the field of the plan — `sheets[8].erasure.line`. Beyond these rules:
- the sheets are numbered in order from 1;
- a sheet has lines, and the answer line is one of them;
- `replacement` is the line `line` whole, and with `part_of_line` a part that stands in this line exactly once. And it is not the erased text.

With the bank, the plan is checked as a sample is: the task of each sheet is in the bank, and the trap is one of the task's traps.

The plan of the measurement of the course of writing is `eval/sheets.json`, and the table "Sheets" in [sheets.md](sheets.md) shows it. The test of `cmd/tutor-eval` builds this table from the file and compares it with the document byte for byte, and `tutor-eval check` checks the plan with the bank.

**Version 1** (T56; R15, amendment 2026-10-27) — the plan of free writing (measurements 3.3). A sheet is as in version 0, but in place of `lines` it has `parts`, the parts in the order in which the solution is read. The numbers `answer`, `expected.first_error` and `erasure.line` are numbers of parts. A part has:
- `kind` — `text` or `drawing`;
- `place` — `left`, the main column, `right`, working at the side, or `wide`, across the whole width of the sheet. A drawing stands in the main column or across the whole width: at the side, only text (T60);
- `text` — for a text, its text, and for a drawing, a description in words. This is also the text of the sample's line;
- `figure` — what the code draws the part by: an object of four fields, `number_line`, `row`, `scheme` and `column`. For a drawing exactly one of the first three is not `null`, and for a text all four are `null`, or only `column` is not.

Figures:
- `number_line` — a number line:
  - the ticks — from `from` to `to` with the step `step`, no more than 31;
  - `labels` — the labelled ticks, each once;
  - `jumps` — the arcs of jumps `{from, to}` in the order of writing, from a tick to another tick;
- `row` — a row of objects, one of which is marked:
  - `object` — for now only `house`;
  - `left` and `right` — how many objects are to the left and to the right of the marked one, and there are no more than 15 of them in all;
  - `numbers` — whether the objects are labelled with numbers from left to right;
- `scheme` — a scheme of segments:
  - `segments` — the segments from top to bottom, no more than six. Each has a label `label` and pieces `pieces` from left to right: a length `length` in units of the scheme and a label `label`. A segment is no longer than 40 units;
  - `total` — the label of the brace that joins the segments;
  - the labels — empty, numbers, □ or △;
- `column` — a calculation in a column: `top`, the sign `op` — `+`, `−` or `×` —, `bottom` and `result`, numbers of digits. The part's text is the column calculation written inline: "14 × 3 = 42".

The answer is a text part. An erasure by scenario:
- **in a drawing** — with the field `erasure.figure`, the drawing as it was before the erasure:
  - of the same figure and on the same skeleton: a line has the same ticks, a row the same object, a scheme as many segments;
  - but not the same as the final one;
  - the erasure takes elements of the drawing, so `part_of_line` is `true`, and `text` and `replacement` name the erased and the new element in words. `replacement` stands in the description exactly once — "7 → 11";
- **in a column calculation** — the erasure takes one of its numbers: `part_of_line` is `true`, `text` is the erased number, and `replacement` is one of the numbers of the column calculation, and it occurs only once among the three. So the code knows which number to rewrite;
- **in a text** — `figure` is `null`.

The reader first looks at the version — an integer under the key `version` — and then reads the plan strictly, as a file of its version. A version it does not know it names alone, and with it a foreign format, if the file names one. In memory the texts of the parts are the lines of the plan's sheet, so the markup check, the summary and the board's proposal of lines work on a plan of version 1 as on version 0. A plan of version 0 is read and written as before, byte for byte.

The check names each error by the path to the field — `sheets[0].parts[0].figure.number_line.jumps[3].to` — and speaks of parts, not of lines. `check`, `sheets` and `split` take a plan of version 1, and `draw` draws all its sheets — text, column calculations and drawings (T59, T60). While the runner and record mode do not know parts (T65, T61), `run` and `board -record` refuse a plan of version 1. The reference of version 1 is `internal/sample/testdata/parts-plan.json`, four sheets on the bank of free writing.

The plans of free writing are `eval/free-sheets.json`, 40 sheets of code, and `eval/free-author.json`, three of the author's sheets, and their tables are in [free-sheets.md](free-sheets.md) (T57; measurements 3.3). A test of `cmd/tutor-eval` builds the document's tables from the files and compares them byte for byte, and holds the author's sheets to free solutions of one part (R28, amendment 2026-10-27), and `tutor-eval check` checks both plans with the bank.

**The author's sheets and their split** (T48; R24, R25). The board's record mode puts each sheet as a file into the directory of sheets, `samples/sheets` (*2026-11-07, T105; R75: `samples/sheets-v1`, as a sample of the protocol*), under the name `NN-YYYYMMDD-HHMMSS-mmm-task-scenario.json` (section 3.3). `internal/sample` knows the scheme of the name, and by it the board, the summary and the split read the directory:
- a sheet of the plan may have several files, and the last by name counts: the start of the name is the sheet's number and the time of saving, of fixed width;
- a file whose name does not name the number, the task and the scenario of a sheet of the plan is not read — for example, a file of another plan with the same number.

**Summary** — `tutor-eval sheets`, against the plan:
- how many sheets are recorded and how many pass the check with the bank, which are missing, which are saved more than once and which files are not read;
- a sheet that does not pass the check — with the paths to the fields where it fails it; *2026-11-07 (T104; R69): a refused sample of the protocol — with the reason for the refusal and the numbers of the file's lines that `sample-check` named, but without its words: they could quote the text of a part;*
- for the sheets that pass: by tasks, scenarios and outcomes — how many of those planned, the erasures by scenario and the stray erasures, the sheets with frames, that is with an erasure, the sheets by mouse — pressure 0.5 at every point — and the sheets whose markup is not by the plan. The summary takes the differences from the plan from the same function as the board (`sheets.Differences`) and names them by the paths to the fields; *2026-11-07 (T104; R69): the mouse of a sample of the protocol — by its `pointer`, and of a v0 sample — by the pressure;*
- whether the split is done, and for which of its sheets the file is no longer the one that counts — the sheet was saved anew or the file is gone. The split does not change because of this.

The summary has no handwriting: only numbers, the sheets' numbers, the files' names and the paths to the fields. It is what the agent sees (T26).

**Format of the split of sheets** (T48; R24) — the JSON file `split.json` in the directory of sheets: `format` — `tutor-split`, `version` — `0`, `seed` — the seed, and two parts, `dev` and `test`. Each part holds sheets: the number of the plan's sheet, `number`, and the name of the file that counted at the split, `file`. The split is read as strictly as a sample. The check:
- each part has sheets;
- each sheet is in one part, and once;
- the file's name is the name of a saved sheet with the same number.

With the plan the split is also checked so: it holds all the sheets of the plan, and each file is a file of its sheet of the plan.

The split is made once, before the first edit of the prompt, and is not overwritten: so the dev part is not fitted to the result (R24). `tutor-eval split` splits:
- each scenario gives the dev part its share, the closest in whole sheets to its share in the plan. The remaining sheets go to the scenarios with the largest remainders, and of two equal ones to the one earlier in the list of scenarios of the section "Sample format". On the measurement's plan of 40 sheets with 10 in the dev part, this is 3 `correct`, 3 `error`, 2 `erasure-fixed`, 2 `erasure-broken` and no `answer-not-last`;
- the seed decides which sheets of a scenario go to the dev part. The sheets are ordered by the SHA-256 of the seed and the file's name, and one seed on the same files always gives one split;
- a split can be made only when all the sheets of the plan are recorded and pass the check;
- a scenario that has no sheet left in one of the parts, `split` names. On the measurement's plan this is `answer-not-last`: the prompt is tuned without its sheet.

The file's name holds the sheet's number in two digits, so the plan of record mode has no more than 99 sheets (`sample.MaxSheets`); both the board and the summary refuse a longer plan.

### 3.2. The code

The first code of `tutor` is the skeleton of a Go module (T11). The tasks of epic E2 bring the types and the behaviour, and the layout of the packages is set now, so that they have a place to go.

- **Module** — `github.com/altnovatrail/tutor`. The line `go 1.27.0` in `go.mod` is the language version and the lower bound for whoever imports `tutor`, for example for `api`. The exact version, Go 1.27.3, is named by the line `toolchain`, and it is held by the `toolchain` stage of the Dockerfile — the one the devcontainer stands on and the checks of CI run in: `GO_VERSION` and `GOTOOLCHAIN=local`, with which the command `go` downloads no other version (R12, amendment 2026-10-21). *2026-11-02 (T37; R64): the line was `go 1.27`, and the module `spec` requires `1.27.0`.*
- **The module `spec`** — `github.com/altnovatrail/spec`, the files of `spec` for code, by the tag of its release (R64 spec). Since T37 the code of `tutor` takes from it the types of a task's open view, `open.Task` and its parts (R72 spec; R64). The schemas, examples and vectors of `spec.Format` the eval runner will take when it reads the format (epic E3). The module's version in `go.mod` and the submodule `spec/` move together, by one move to a tag — the test `TestSpecVersion` compares the module's version with the tag that CLAUDE.md names and, where the submodule is present, with its tag, — and the module `spec` is excluded from Dependabot's `gomod` (R49; Д46, RUN.md of `spec`).
- **The contracts module of `spec`** — `github.com/altnovatrail/spec/contracts`, the code of the API contracts generated from the `.proto` files of `spec`, by the tag `contracts/<tag>` on the commit of the `spec` tag (R83 spec). Since T31 the code of `tutor` takes from it the messages of the eval protocol, `Sample` and `Report` of the package `novatrail.eval.v1`, and the vectors of the stroke rules, `contracts.Vectors`. Its version is the same as that of the module `spec`: `TestSpecVersion` compares them, and it is excluded from Dependabot's `gomod` too (R67; Д92, RUN.md of `spec`).
- **Packages:**
  - the module's root, the package `tutor`, is the library of AI capabilities that `api` calls in its own process (R15 spec). It will hold the request types and the observation, the calls of reading, analysis and the hint, the usage of a call and the admission function. The types have no fields for the learner's and the account's identifiers (section 1); *2026-11-08 (T114; R81): it holds `New` and `Config`, the call's route `Route` with its own key `OwnKey`, the choice of a model `Choose`, the outcomes `Outcome` and the record of usage `Usage`, and the admission function is the subpackage `admission` (section 3.10, "Code");*
  - `admission` — the admission function: the table of `spec`, the file of deployment facts and the answer by them (section 3.9; T112; R79). `api` calls it too, so the package is at the module's root, not in `internal/`; *2026-11-08 (T113; R80): also the list of admitted models, `Admitted`, and the choice of a model, `Table.Select` (section 3.9, "The admission rule and the list of the admitted");*
  - `provider` — the port of AI providers: the question to a model about pictures, with the JSON schema of the response and the effort level, and the model's answer with its token usage. The pictures go in order, and each may have a caption that introduces it — for example, "Image 1: the sheet as it was finished." (T16). Each provider's adapter implements the port in its own package under `provider/`, and only that adapter imports the provider's SDK. So the SDKs reach the build of `api` only through `tutor` (architecture 3.6 spec). The port converts a place that the model named from the picture's pixels into thousandths, 0–1000, `provider.Place` (R53 spec; T74);
  - `provider/claude` and `provider/gpt` — the adapters of Anthropic and OpenAI, through their official SDKs (R19). The Claude adapter knows four current models with their prices and five effort levels, and the GPT adapter one model and the level `high` (R19, amendment 2026-10-24). The Claude adapter sends each picture as a block of its own, after the block of its caption, and with `oversized_image: error`, and the request's text after the pictures. It takes no more than 20 pictures: beyond 20, each picture of the request has a stricter size limit (sources 1.4), and with `oversized_image: error` the call would fail. The GPT adapter takes one picture and refuses several before the call. This is a limit of the adapter, not of the model: the measurement of the course of writing does not ask GPT (R21). A request without a picture, with an empty picture or with several pictures that have no caption, both adapters refuse before the call (T16). `provider.Billed` says whether the call's whole bill is in its tokens: an answer cut off before the tokens are counted and a call interrupted before the bill, the board and the run count by the worst case (T23); *2026-11-10 (T129; R90): each adapter has `Dial`, the adapter as `tutor.Config` takes it (`provider.Dial`), and the Claude models with their prices and effort levels are the package `provider/claude/catalog`, without the SDK, one list: by it the board and the runner name and count the models, and the Claude adapter, `claude.Dial`, is given to the runner by `cmd/tutor-eval` (Д185, RUN.md of `spec`);* *2026-11-13 (T137; R105): neither adapter takes anything from the process's environment — not the address, a key, a token, a profile, the federation or headers, nor the tracing (sources 1.17; Д225 DEBT.md of `spec`). The Claude adapter builds its client with `option.WithoutEnvironmentDefaults()` and `option.WithoutOpenTelemetry()`: the SDK writes no span and sends no trace context to Anthropic, so the traces of `api` hold no span of the provider, and the time of a call is the usage record's. The GPT adapter builds the two services it calls, `responses.NewResponseService` and `openai.NewModelService`, without `openai.NewClient`, whose defaults of the environment have no public switch. Both send by `provider.HTTPClient`: one transport of the adapters, shared, with the settings of Go's default and the SDKs' timeout of 10 minutes for the headers of an answer — not `http.DefaultTransport`, so that an instrumentation the process puts there sends no trace context to the provider. What that transport takes from the process — the proxy of `HTTPS_PROXY` and `NO_PROXY`, the certificates of `SSL_CERT_FILE` — is the deployment's, as for every client of `api`. Every client the board and the runner build goes through the adapters' `New`, and the tests set each variable the SDKs read and hold the address, the key and the headers of the call, by what they share in `internal/providertest`: a transport that answers itself and keeps each request, and the check of the requests;*
  - `cmd/tutor-eval` — the CLI of the eval runner on the same code (R15 spec). Commands: `tutor-eval board` — the check board, and with `-record` its record mode (section 3.3); `tutor-eval check FILE…` checks samples, banks, sheet plans, pages of the workbook and essays, and samples and plans also by the bank, if it is passed together with them; `tutor-eval draw` draws by code each sheet of the plan that the directory does not have yet, with `writer` `synthetic`, and with `-png DIRECTORY` also the final picture of each drawn sheet and a frame before each of its erasures, in a directory of its own (R26 and the 2026-10-27 amendment to it). With `-shift FRACTION` the last digit of the answer of a near-key sheet leans toward the digit of its pair by this fraction, and without the flag by 0.1 (T93; R50); `tutor-eval gen sheets|near` builds a bank of tasks and the plan of their sheets with truth by construction — sheets of lines, six per task, or near-key sheets, two per task, — and `tutor-eval gen workbook|essays` pages of the workbook or essays, and writes them as new files (T93, T94; R50); `tutor-eval sheets` — the summary of the recorded sheets against the plan, without handwriting; `tutor-eval split -seed N` — the split of sheets into the dev and the test parts, once (T48; section 3.1); `tutor-eval run -part dev|test|all` — a run of the calls of a kind on a part of the split or on all the sheets of the plan, after the estimate and the author's "yes". The kind is `-kind`, `judge`, `reading`, `hint`, `workbook` or `essay`, and the set of sheets is `-dir`, `-bank` and `-plan` or, for reading and the hint over several sets, `-set` for each (T23, T65, T67, T68, T74; sections 3.1 and 3.4). What reading reads, `-reads` names: the key conditions, the other digit heights and the states (T69). `tutor-eval report LOG` — the report of a run by its log, by the log's kind, without calls of a model (T24, T67; section 3.1), and for the hint and essays also a file for the author: the hints, `.hints.md` (T75), and the feedback, `.feedback.md` (T83); `tutor-eval workbook` and `tutor-eval essays` check the pages of the workbook and the essays, draw them in memory and print a summary (T78, T82); `tutor-eval synth DIRECTORY` writes a synthetic bank and a sample per scenario and overwrites no files. `tutor-eval open -file FILE FILE…` writes as a new file the open views of the tasks of the banks, the workbook and the essays, without keys, solutions, traps and checks — the file for the samples page of `canvas` (section 3.1; T37; R64). The parts of the runner go under `internal/`: only the module itself sees them. *2026-11-07 (T104; R69): `draw` writes samples of the eval protocol, and `check` does not check a sample of the protocol — its rules are held by `spec`'s `sample-check`, and the summary reads it against the plan.* *2026-11-07 (T107; R71): `draw -workbook F` and `draw -essays F` draw the pages of the workbook and the essays as samples of the protocol into a directory of their own, and with `-png` their crops at 32 px; `tutor-eval workbook` and `essays` no longer draw in memory — they read the directory of samples `-dir`, with refusals, — and the run of the workbook and the essays takes `-dir` in place of `-seed`.* *2026-11-08 (T32; R76): `tutor-eval canvas -dir D -at D` checks that the runner reads `canvas`'s synthetic sheets: each sample by `sample-check`, reading by the task it names, with the bank at its `tutor` tag from the trees of `-at`, and runs on the stub with the set's report (section 3.1, "`canvas`'s synthetic sheets").*

    The directories are outside git, in two folders at the root of `tutor` that `.gitignore` ignores. The board and record mode write the author's handwriting and take a directory only in `samples/`. `draw`, `sheets`, `split` and `run` take it in `samples/` or in `synthetic/`, where what the code draws lies, with the runs on it (R05 and the 2026-10-27 amendment to it; R28). They do not take the folder itself as the directory of sheets: the run log goes beside the directory of sheets, in `runs`. `tutor-eval workbook` and `tutor-eval essays` with `-png` write the crops of the workbook's boxes and of the essays' pages only in `synthetic/` or outside the tree (T78, T82). The run log on sheets in `samples/` goes only there too. A command refuses a directory in the working copy outside its folders before it creates it — `checkIgnored`. So handwriting lies neither beside the code nor in the folder open to the agent;
  - `internal/board` — the check board: the page, the tasks, the question to the models, the log of marks (section 3.3; T39). It also holds the server of record mode (T50);
  - `internal/judge` — the judge of the measurement: the instructions, the request of each form and the response schema (section 3.1; T19). There are two versions of the prompt: `judge.V1` for E2, `judge.V2` for free writing (T64);
  - `internal/journal` — the log of the course and the choice of frames: the steps of writing in English from the strokes and erasures, without markup, up to two frames before an erasure, and the agreement of the log's lines with the lines of the markup (section 3.1; T22). There are two versions, each with its own words: the log of lines `journal.Of` for E2, the log of parts `journal.OfParts` for free writing, on the groups `lines.Groups` (T63). The order of writing, strokes and erasures together, is given by `journal.Writing`, and the line or the part of a stroke by `LineOf`: by them `internal/states` chooses the moments (T69);
  - `internal/lines` — the lines of a sheet: the function `lines.Group` gathers into a line the strokes at one height: a stroke stands at the middle of its band, and a new line is started by a step between the middles of no less than the constant `Gap`, 12 units of the sheet, or half of the usual height of a stroke of the sheet. A line of only small strokes goes into the nearest line (R25 and the 2026-10-26 amendment to it; T50). `lines.Band` gives the band of the sheet that the strokes take up, and `lines.On` the line on which the band stands. By them `lines.TakenFrom` and `lines.ErasureLine` find on which line the strokes of an erasure stood: the board, for the erasure by scenario and the check, and the summary, for the differences from the plan (T47, T51, T48). The stroke groups, as the device will find them, are `lines.Groups`, by the rule of the eval protocol, R24 canvas, to the bit (R30; T58):
    - neighbours — a pause of no more than 2 s, from the end of the earlier stroke to the start of the later one, and a gap between the boxes of their points of no more than 50 units on both axes, and everything is counted in whole tenths of a unit and in milliseconds;
    - a group is a chain of neighbours, the label is by place, and an erased stroke is in the groups of its visible neighbours;
    - the test compares the rule with the vectors of `canvas`, a copy, `internal/lines/testdata/groups.json`. *2026-11-07 (T31; R67): with the vectors of `spec`, `contracts.Vectors` `eval/v1/groups.json`, — 16 cases with a writing history, groups of erased strokes and group boxes, — and the copy is gone.*

    The crop takes the tenths of a unit, `lines.Tenths`, too (T69);

    `lines.ErasurePart` — the place of an erasure on the plane for a sheet of parts: the part with the stroke nearest to the erased one, no further than 50 units, without time. `sheets.ErasurePlace` chooses between it and the bands by the sheet's plan, and by it `sheets.Differences` and the check of record mode compare the erasure. `lines.PartsTaken` counts by the same rule how many strokes each erasure took from each part: by it record mode chooses the erasure by scenario for a sheet of parts (T61);
  - `internal/runner` — the runner of all call kinds: the configurations of models — three of E4 and four of the extended measurement, and a run asks those that the flag `-readers` names, `runner.Chosen` (T90; R50), — the steps of a run, the estimate and the ceiling, `Do`, the run log and its reading, `runner.ReadLog`, `runner.ReadRecords` and, for the workbook's pages, `runner.ReadItems`, the readers of the kinds of reading, `runner.ReadersOf`, the stub, which answers with the right answer of the kind, and the call of models (section 3.1, "The runner of call kinds"; T67);
  - `internal/measure` — the kind "judge" on the runner: the plan of calls by forms and the tokens of the estimate, the records of the judge's log and their reading, `measure.ReadLog`, and the answer of an always-right judge by the markup, for the stub (section 3.1; T23, T24, T67). A run goes in one version of the judge's prompt, and for free writing the log names both the sheet's layout and the place of the first error (T65);
  - `internal/report` — the report of a run: correct answers by the markup, the criterion with the exact sign permutation test and the Holm correction, numbers without a threshold, Markdown and CSV (section 3.1; T24). For free writing, also the boxes on the sheets with an error, the breakdown and the table by sheets (T65). The report of reading is `report.NewReading`: the threshold of R03 by each configuration, the readings by reader and condition with upper bounds by sheets, `report.Upper`, and two readers (section 3.4; T71), and also the digit heights with the advice of `spec`, the states and the policies of О-8 (T72). The report of the hint is `report.NewHint`: leaks, form, fallback text and place by configuration, language and rung, and as a third file about 30 hints for the author (section 3.4, "Hint"; T75). The report of essays is `report.NewEssays`: false alarms and missed "not met" by criterion with a bound by essays, Cohen's kappa, the criteria that repeats assessed differently, reading and the feedback rule, and as a third file the feedback for the author (measurements 3.8; T83); *2026-11-07 (T106; R74): also the runner's report `Report` by § 14 of the contracts text of `spec` — for each reader and for the pair, as a file beside the log, — and its measures, `report.MeasuresOf`, as a section of the Markdown and as the CSV `<log>.protocol.csv` (section 3.1, "The runner's report `Report`");* *2026-11-08 (T113; R80): also the admission rule, `report.Admit`: the rung up to which the runner's report admits the pair of its readers, and in the admission run, `-reads plain`, the repeats are for each reader;*
  - `internal/value` — the check kinds `number` and `fraction` of the format of `spec`: reading a number in seven steps, the notations and the exact comparison with the key, for a fraction also the form, any or irreducible, the verdict of the answer slot and of the item, the trap by value. The reference is the verdict vectors of `spec`, their copy in `testdata` (section 3.4, "Reading into an observation" and "Workbook"; T68, T78). `module-kit` gives the same verdicts (T103; R68), and the package's checks go with the move of the runner to it (T104). *2026-11-07 (T104; R69): they go in T108, which T104 split off;* *2026-11-07 (T108; R73): the checks went to `module-kit` together with the copy of the vectors; what stays is the normalization of steps 1–4, reading a number without units, `Read`, and writing a number as a rational, `Rational`, — for reading, the leak check and the reports;*
  - `internal/workbook` — the workbook of the measurement: the slots of a page — the crop of each filled box by its strokes (T78; R40), — and its reading: the prompt and its fingerprint, the request of a page, the strict parsing of the answer, the verdicts of the boxes and the items, the stub's right reader and the kind `workbook` on the runner with its log, `workbook.ReadLog` (section 3.4, "Workbook"; T79; R41). The run of the workbook is `tutor-eval run -kind workbook`: it takes the pages from `-workbook`, draws them by the seed `-seed` and writes the log into `synthetic/runs`. *2026-11-07 (T107; R71): the run takes the pages as samples of the directory `-dir` — `evalsample.Page`, the strokes of each box, — and the seed is the samples';*
  - `internal/essay` — the essays of the measurement, in one call and in two: reading, then the assessment of the read text without the picture (`essay.NewTwo`, the flag `-two` of `tutor-eval run`; T92). The provider port lets a request of text alone through only when it is marked `NoPicture`. The essays of the measurement: what the model sees of an essay — its lines and the crop of the page by the rule of the eval protocol, `essay.PictureOf` (section 3.4, "Essays"; T82; R44), — and the assessment by the rubric: the prompt and its fingerprint, the request of an essay, the strict parsing of the answer, the marks of the criteria, `essay.Marks`, the feedback rule, `essay.Feedback`, and the reading of an essay, `essay.ReadingOf`, the stub's right assessor and the kind `essay` on the runner with its log, `essay.ReadLog` (section 3.4, "Assessment by the rubric"; T83; R45). The run of essays is `tutor-eval run -kind essay`: it takes the essays from `-essays`, draws them by the seed `-seed` and writes the log into `synthetic/runs`. *2026-11-07 (T107; R71): the run takes the essays as samples of the directory `-dir`, `evalsample.Essay`, and cuts the crop by their strokes, `essay.PictureFrom`;*
  - `internal/reading` — reading a sheet into an observation: the prompt and its fingerprint, the strict reading of the answer, the verdict by the observation, "read under the key", the stub's right reader and the kind `reading` on the runner with its log, `reading.ReadLog` (section 3.4, "Reading into an observation"; T68). Reading sees the crop, and the run also reads other digit heights and states — what exactly, `reading.Reads` says (T69); *2026-11-08 (T113; R80): also the admission run, `reading.Plain` — the final sheet without the key at 32 px, each reader in each repeat, and the mark "reads once" of Opus stays the mark of the strong reader;*
  - `internal/hint` — the call of analysis and the hint: the prompt and its fingerprint, the strict reading of the answer, the path of a box from the crop's pixels into thousandths, 0–1000, and into a part of the sheet, the stub's right model and the kind `hint` on the runner with its log, `hint.ReadLog` (section 3.4, "Hint"; T74; R38). The run checks each hint as `api` does: the leak check, the form check, `hint.Form`, and the rung's fallback text, `hint.Fallback` (T75; R39). The form counts sentences, questions and words by `hint.Count`: the feedback on an essay is counted the same way (T83); *2026-11-08 (T118; R85): also the cases of the set of words, `hint.Cases`, their run, `-cases`, and the measures by code — a repeat of the previous question, an echo of the working at the side and a rough search for gender;*
  - `internal/leak` — the leak check of the format of `spec`: the task's key by value, if the statement and the texts of its item do not show it, and the leak phrases — as the format text says in its section on the leak check. The reference is the leak vectors of `spec`, their copy in `testdata` (section 3.4, "Hint"; T75; R39); *2026-11-10 (T129; R90): with format 0.4, also a key of the kind `time` by hours and minutes, `07:30` is the time `7:30`, and the vectors are 19 files of `spec` `v0.14.0` (R111 spec);*
  - `internal/wordsjudge` — the judge of words: the prompt and its fingerprint, the strict reading of the verdict, the control phrases and the kind `words-judge` on the runner, over the log of the hint or over the control phrases (section 3.4, "The set of words"; T118; R85);
  - `internal/states` — the states of a sheet for reading unfinished work: the moments after a line, at a pause and before an erasure, what the sheet shows at them and how far each part of the markup is written by them, and the readings per sheet by each policy of reading at a pause (section 3.4, "Reading into an observation"; О-8; T69);
  - `internal/openview` — the open views of the measurement's tasks: the open view of a task of the format `internal/task` from `module-kit`, `openview.Build`, writing, strict reading — only in the form in which `openview.Marshal` writes it, — and the check by the rules of the format that the types of `spec` do not have (section 3.1; T37; R64);
  - `internal/protocol` — the eval protocol of `spec`, the package `novatrail.eval.v1` (T31; R67): reading a sample `Sample` and a report `Report` — first the field `protocol`, then strictly, `protojson`, — and writing them with the protobuf names, all the fields and indentation, the same bytes on any build. The fold of the writing history of § 12 of the contracts text, `protocol.Fold`, and the strokes of the history and of the final geometry for the code of `tutor`, `protocol.Ink` and `protocol.Final`, and the final strokes of the history for the box rule, `protocol.FinalOf` (T107). The workbook's box rules, `protocol.BoxOf` and `protocol.Table`, and snapping, `protocol.Snap`, — by the vectors of `spec`. It has no check of the file of its own: the rules of § 11 and 12 are checked by `spec`'s recipe `sample-check` (R93 spec). `internal/protocol/protocoltest` gives the tests the examples of `spec` from the module cache;
  - `internal/evalsample` — a sheet of the measurement as a sample of the eval protocol (T104; R69): `Of` writes a sheet that the code drew, `Read` reads a sample back into the model of `internal/sample`, with the sheet of the plan, and refuses by § 13 — without markup, of another task, with markup not by the task (`module-kit`), and a child sample (section 3.1, "The protocol's sample for synthetic sheets"). Its `testdata` holds one sample of a sheet per kind that `draw` draws, byte for byte: `sample-check` checks them in CI, and `go test -update` writes them anew. *2026-11-07 (T105; R75): also a person's sheet — `PersonOf`, `HistoryOf` with the pointer, `NewID`, `WriterCodeAt` and `Loose`, a stroke outside the parts;* *2026-11-07 (T107; R71): also pages and essays — `OfPage`, `OfEssay`, `ReadPage`, `ReadEssay`, the comparison with the file and reading a directory, `ReadPagesDir` and `ReadEssaysDir`, — and a common source of tasks, `Source` (section 3.1, "Samples of pages and essays");*
  - `internal/samplecheck` — the rules of § 11 and 12 by the same code as `sample-check` (T104; R69): it calls `just -f spec/justfile sample-check` from the repository's root and reads which files and lines break a rule. It has no check of its own (R93 spec). The summary, the split and the run refuse a sample that it named;
  - `internal/task` — the measurements' tasks as tasks of the format of `spec` 0.3 with checks, `formatv0.Task` (T103; R68): a sheet of a bank is an answer slot `anywhere` with the check `number` by `sample.KeyOf` of the bank's key and the bank's traps in `traps`, a page of the workbook is item lines with boxes and their checks `number` and `fraction`, an essay is an answer slot `sheet` with a rubric. By them `module-kit` gives the open view, its digest and the verdicts by the observation, `observation.Validate`, `Join` and `Verdicts`. The closed fields that the measurements do not have — `skills`, `band` and `difficulty` — are constant. The package depends only on `sample` and `value`; *2026-11-07 (T108; R73): `SheetItem` is the sheet's answer slot by the key, by it reading judges the observation, and the report joins the pair; the format's check of a box is `sample.BoxCheck.Format`, and the package depends only on `sample`;*
  - `internal/newfile` — writing a new file: only if it does not exist yet, and an unfinished one is deleted. So write the board, `tutor-eval convert` and `tutor-eval split` (T48). *2026-11-07 (T105; R75): `tutor-eval synth` is gone;*
  - `internal/convert` — the only reader of `tutor-sample` v0 (T105; R75): the strict reading of the former format, with the pressure of the points, which says only whether a mouse or a pen wrote, and the conversion of a sheet into a sample of the protocol — an author's sheet by `evalsample.PersonOf`, a sheet of code by `evalsample.Of` after the comparison with what `synth` draws by the same seed (section 3.1, "The author's sheets on the protocol");
  - `internal/render` — the render of a sheet: a picture from the strokes at any moment of writing — the final one and a frame before an erasure, — its PNG and its tokens by the Vision formula (section 3.1; T21). It also holds the crop by the rule of the eval protocol: its numbers, `render.CropOf`, and its pixels, `Crop.Draw` (section 3.1, "Rendering a sheet"; T69);
  - `internal/sample` — the format of a sample, of a bank of tasks, of a sheet plan — versions 0 and 1 — and of a split, and also of the pages of the workbook, `tutor-workbook` (T78), and of essays, `tutor-essays` (T82): the types, reading JSON and the check, which lists all the errors. The value of a written answer and of the bank's key are `sample.ValueOf` and `sample.KeyOf` (T103; R68). The sentences, words and linking words of an essay are counted by `sample.Sentences`, `sample.Words` and `sample.LinkingWords`, and the criteria that the program counts, by `Essay.Met`. It also holds the scheme of the name of a saved sheet's file (section 3.1; T12, T49, T48, T56);
  - `internal/sheets` — the directory of sheets against the plan: the last file of each sheet, read and checked as a sample, the summary without handwriting and the split. It also holds the comparison of a sheet's markup with the plan, `sheets.Differences`, which the board takes too (section 3.1; T48), and the sheet that a run takes, `sheets.Chosen`, with its set (T68); *2026-11-07 (T104; R69): the file is a sample of the protocol or `tutor-sample` v0, by the key `protocol`; a sample of the protocol is checked by `internal/samplecheck` and read by `internal/evalsample`, and a refused sheet is `Sheet.Refused`.*
  - `internal/gen` — the generators of the sets of the extended measurement with truth by construction: `gen.Sheets` and `gen.NearSheets` — a bank of tasks and the plan of their sheets (T93), `gen.Workbook` — pages of the workbook, `gen.Essays` — essays from banks of sentences (T94; R50). What they give passes the checks of their formats;
  - `internal/synth` — the synthetic bank, synthetic samples on the board's sheet of 1000 × 600 and a plan of those that a plan can hold, for tests (T12, T48, T21). It draws the sheets of a plan of version 1 by the place of the parts (T59; R26, amendment 2026-10-27):
    - the main column — on the left, from top to bottom;
    - the column calculations at the side — in a row on the right, beside the part of the main column written before them;
    - a part — without pauses longer than 2 s, and between the parts a pause from 2.1 s, and the step of the letters no more than 32 units. So the stroke groups, `lines.Groups`, match the parts of the plan (R30).

    A drawing — a number line, a row of houses, a scheme of segments — it draws as things with keys, each beside the one just drawn. An erasure in a drawing it draws as the figure "before", removes with one movement of the eraser what the final one does not have, and draws the new in addition (T60; R26, amendment 2026-10-27). The v0 sheets of other scenarios it draws byte for byte as before: this holds the hash of the 40 sheets of E2 in the test. On a v0 sheet of the scenario `similar-digits` the last digit of the answer line leans toward the digit of its pair: both strokes are taken at 24 points along their length, the second in the direction where the points are closer, and they are mixed by the fraction `synth.SimilarShift`. Points are taken also at all the vertices of both strokes, so at the fraction 0 the digit is the same as the font's. A digit without a pair, an answer with an erasure and a sheet of parts do not lean (T70). The pages of the workbook, 1000 × 1400, it draws in memory, `synth.PageOf`: box by box, the writing does not leave the box, and a digit may lean toward its pair, be mirrored or stand beside a crossed-out one (T78; R40). English text it writes with its own strokes: the lowercase and capital Latin letters and the signs of English text — as polylines in the font's box, like the Russian letters, and `synth.Text` writes lines of text in a handwriting from a seed on a sheet of a given size. It has no one else's font (T81; R43). An essay it writes on a sheet of 1000 × 1400, `synth.Essay`: the title as the first line, the text wrapped by words at 28–40 characters from the seed, with a gap of two spaces between words (T82; R44).
- **Data** — `eval/bank.json`, the bank of tasks of the measurement of the course of writing (T15), and `eval/sheets.json`, its sheet plan (T49; section 3.1). These are files, not Go packages: they are opened by `tutor-eval check`, the tests of `cmd/tutor-eval`, the board's record mode (T50), the summary and the split of sheets (T48) and the runner (T23). Beside them are `eval/free-bank.json`, the bank of free writing (T52), and its plans `eval/free-sheets.json` and `eval/free-author.json` of version 1 (T57; measurements 3.3), and also the bank and the plan of the near key, `eval/near-bank.json` and `eval/near-sheets.json` of version 0 (T70), the pages of the workbook, `eval/workbook.json` (T78), and the essays, `eval/essays.json` (T82). From the banks of T15 and of free writing, the workbook and the essays, `just open-tasks` writes `eval/open-tasks.json` — their open views, which the release carries (T37; R64). They are opened by `tutor-eval check`, the tests, the summary, the split and the synthetic sheets (T59, T60). The runner and record mode — since T65 and T61 (section 3.1, "Format of the sheet plan"). *2026-11-08 (T113; R80): also the bank and the plan of the author's sheets for the admission set, `eval/author-bank.json` and `eval/author-sheets.json`, — `tutor-eval gen sheets` built them with the seed 113, and the author writes them with the recipe `admit-record`.*
- **Recipes** of the `justfile` — for work in the devcontainer:
  - `build` and `test`;
  - `board`, `record` and `measure` — the check board, its record mode and the run of the measurement (sections 3.1, 3.3). `free-record` — record mode on the author's three sheets, the plan `eval/free-author.json`, into the directory `samples/free` (measurements 3.3; T61). *2026-11-07 (T105; R75): the recipes' directories are `-v1`: `record` — `samples/sheets-v1`, `free-record` — `samples/free-v1`, `messy-record` — `samples/free-messy-v1`, `live` — `samples/free-v1`, `free` — `synthetic/free-v1`;*
  - `lint` — the linters and the formatting check of golangci-lint;
  - `fmt` — formatting in place; *2026-11-08 (T113; R80): `admit-record` — record mode on the 54 sheets of the plan `eval/author-sheets.json`, into the directory `samples/author-v1`; `tutor-eval admit REPORT…` — the decision of the admission rule by the runner's reports.*
  - `open-tasks` — the file of open views `eval/open-tasks.json` anew, when the bank, the workbook or the essays changed: until then the test fails (T37).
- **The checks of CI** — the recipes `ci-lint`, `ci-test`, `ci-vuln` and `ci-licenses`, with the names from the agreement of `.github`'s shared `go.yml` (R16 .github; R13). They are run by the job "Go" of `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, through `go.yml`: each recipe has a job of its own and a check of its own, "Go / lint" and so on. They run in the `toolchain` stage of `.devcontainer/Dockerfile`, as the CI runner's user and with `HOME=/tmp`, from a checkout without the `spec` submodule. So the code and the tests must not read `spec/`: the code takes the files of `spec` from its releases (R21 spec), and a test that opens `spec/` passes in the devcontainer and fails only in "Go / test".
  - `ci-lint` is `lint`: CI and `just lint` check with one command;
  - `ci-test` — the tests with the race detector and without the cache, and before them `release-files-test`, the check of the release recipe. It writes the test coverage as the profile `coverage/go.out`, by packages, and the job "Go / coverage" of `go.yml` sends it to Codecov by OIDC; the check `codecov/patch` — no less than 90% of new and changed lines, `.github/codecov.yml` (R66);
  - `ci-vuln` — govulncheck: known vulnerabilities in the code that the module reaches;
  - `ci-licenses` — go-licenses: the license of each package that the module is built from is from the list `ALLOWED_LICENSES`, the ASF's category A, which `go.yml` gives (R12 spec). Without the list the recipe fails.
  - `ci-sample-check` is `sample-check`: the samples of `internal/evalsample/testdata` by the rules of § 11 and 12, by the recipe of `spec` (T104; R69). `go.yml` does not take the submodule, so it is run by a job of its own, "Samples" of `ci.yml`, as the shared `documents.yml` runs its own check: a checkout with the submodule and the `toolchain` stage, where since T104 buf 1.75.0 stands, the version of the devcontainer of `spec` at the submodule's tag.
  - `ci-canvas-check` — `canvas-check` and `canvas-check-test`: `canvas`'s synthetic sheets of the pinned release — the archive by the tag and the sum from the `justfile`, the trees of `eval/` of the `tutor` tags that the samples name — are read by `tutor-eval canvas`, and a spoiled sample fails it (section 3.1, "`canvas`'s synthetic sheets"; T32, R76). The reports `Report` of the runs are held by `spec`'s recipe `report-check` (T128, R87). It needs the submodule and the network, and it is run by a job of its own, "Canvas" of `ci.yml`, as "Samples" is: a checkout with the submodule and all the tags, and the `toolchain` stage.

  The versions of govulncheck and go-licenses are in the `justfile` itself: `go run` takes exactly the named version, they are not installed into the image, and Dependabot does not see them, so they are raised by hand (R13).
- **CodeQL** reads Go: `.github/workflows/codeql.yml` passes to `.github`'s shared `codeql.yml` the input `languages: go`, and the analysis builds the code by autobuild with the Go of the `toolchain` stage (the amendment to R09 .github; R13). Findings hold the merge by the ruleset's rule for CodeQL (R30 spec).
- **Linters** — golangci-lint's set `standard` (errcheck, govet, ineffassign, staticcheck, unused) and also errorlint, misspell, nolintlint and unconvert. Formatters — gofmt and goimports (`.golangci.yml`).
- **Release** (R62) comes out after each green merge into `main` that changes the list `paths` in `.github/workflows/release.yml`: Go code without tests, the files of `//go:embed`, `go.mod`, `go.sum`, `justfile`, `LICENSE`, the Dockerfile, `release.yml` itself and the file of open views `eval/open-tasks.json` (R64). It is released by `.github`'s shared `release.yml`, pinned at `v0.1.11`. The number is the next patch of the line `0.1`, starting from `v0.1.0`, and the release sets the tag itself.
  - **The tag of the Go module** is what `api` takes `tutor` by: `go get github.com/altnovatrail/tutor@vX.Y.Z`. sum.golang.org keeps a module's version forever: it cannot be removed, only retracted by the directive `retract` (R64 spec).
  - **The files with a Sigstore signature** — `tutor_<version>_source.tar.gz`, the commit's sources from the tree of `git archive` without the `spec` submodule; `tutor_<version>_open-tasks.json`, a copy of `eval/open-tasks.json` — the open views of the measurement's tasks for the samples page of `canvas`, from the first release after the merge of T37 (section 3.1; R64); and `SHA256SUMS` of both. The recipe `release-files` writes them, and only in the tree that `git archive` wrote: in a folder with `.git` and in a tree with a folder that git ignores it refuses, so that `samples/` and everything that is not in git do not get into the archive. `release-files-test` checks the recipe and verifies that each file of `//go:embed` and the file of open views stand in `paths`, and `ci-test` calls it.
  - **Binaries of `tutor-eval`** the release does not have: they will come when the eval runner takes on the CI of `modules`.

### 3.3. The check board

The MVP of `tutor` (R19): the author solves given tasks by hand, the models that they ticked read the solution and assess it themselves, and the author marks under each whether it was right. Since T45 the model answers only with an assessment, checking is possible along the course of the solution, and the model marks the error on the board itself (R19, amendment 2026-10-25). Since T52 there are 11 tasks: six with free writing were added to the first five, for the pilot of epic E4 (R19, amendment 2026-10-26; measurements 3.2). In `spec` the board was recorded by R43 spec: the eval CLI serves it on 127.0.0.1 in the devcontainer, and it is not a service (architecture 3.6 spec). The card and repositories 6 spec, item 5, since `v0.3.2` say "the author solves the board's tasks", and which tasks these are and how many, this section says (В32; R49). The board has no programmatic check. The big measurement — measurements 1 — was put off for the sake of the board, and now goes after it, only on Claude models (R21).

**Result** (R20, 2026-10-23): the author solved all five tasks, in their words, «максимально сложно и непонятно», and Claude Opus 5.5 assessed all five correctly; a check costs about a cent and takes about 5 seconds. GPT is not connected to the board: there is no OpenAI key, and the board works with one model. The board does not give the model the course of the solution, only the picture: whether it helps, the big measurement will show (R21).

**What it looks like:**
1. Above the board — the task, arrows to the neighbouring tasks and how much has been spent in the launch. Below them — the models: each has a tick "ask", an effort level and a price, and Haiku 5.5 and Sonnet 5.5 also have the option no thinking.
2. The author writes the solution on the board with a mouse or with the pen of a graphics tablet — sometimes with an error on purpose or illegibly. There are Undo stroke, which undoes a stroke, and Clear, which erases everything.
3. Check can be pressed at any moment, in the middle of the solution too. It sends the picture of the board — black lines on white, without the task's text — to all the ticked models at once, each with its effort level and, if ticked, without thinking. To compare two levels of one model, the author presses Check again on the same strokes, having changed the level.
4. Under the board — each model's answer, side by side:
   - the assessment — Correct, No mistakes so far — keep going, Error or Cannot read it;
   - the time of the answer and when its first token came;
   - the cost;
   - the input and output tokens, and of them the thinking tokens;
   - the SDK's retries, if there were any.

   The model marks the error on the board itself, in its colour — underlines, circles or boxes it — and the card says how. The card is of the same colour.
5. Under each answer — AI was right and AI was wrong. "Right" is when both the assessment and the place of the mark, if there is one, are correct. This mark can be changed: the summary keeps the last one.
6. At the bottom — a summary by each pair "model · effort", with thinking and without:
   - how many answers are marked and how many of them "AI was right";
   - how many answers in all and how many without an assessment;
   - the mean cost and the mean time of an answer;
   - the mean time to the first token, the mean output and thinking tokens — by the answers with an assessment: the bill of a failed call may be cut off.

   The answers with analysis recorded before T45 go as rows of their own.

The board's buttons, captions and messages are in English, like all the code (R14 spec); the statements of the tasks are in Russian (R19).

**What the model sees** — the picture, the task's statement, the correct answer and the reference solution, and the request's text also names the picture's size in pixels. The instruction tells it:
- to read the writing exactly as written, correcting nothing;
- to compare it with the correct answer and the reference — another way of solving is correct too;
- to judge only what is written: the solution may be unfinished, and a step that is not there yet and an unfinished last line are not an error;
- to answer in JSON by the schema. In it, the assessment — `correct`, `so_far_correct`, `error` or `unreadable` —, and at an error a mark: the kind — `underline`, `circle` or `box` — and the box `x1, y1, x2, y2` around the erroneous writing.

The fields go in order — the assessment, then the mark. The schema's properties are ready JSON: the SDK sorts the keys of a map (section 3.12).

**Mark** — on a layer of its own above the board, and this layer does not get into the picture for the model.
- The board draws the model's box as it is, without snapping to the strokes, and takes the box's corners in any order.
- A box that goes beyond the edge of the picture the board clamps to the edge, as Anthropic advises (sources 1.7).
- A box wholly beyond the edge, without width or without height — except an underline — the board does not draw. The card then says that the model gave no place, and the assessment stays.
- The model does not reduce the picture: the board's picture is within its limits. And a picture that the API would reduce, the request tells it to refuse with an error: otherwise the model's pixels would diverge from the board's pixels.
- Clear, moving to another task and a new check remove the marks. Undo and new strokes do not: Undo, as before, leaves the answers, and the marks belong to the picture that the models checked.

**Models** — the four current Claude models: `claude-haiku-5-5`, `claude-sonnet-5-5`, `claude-opus-5-5` and `claude-fable-5-1`. Each can be asked at any of five effort levels — `low`, `medium`, `high`, `xhigh` and `max` (T40; R19, amendment 2026-10-24). By default only Opus 5.5 with `high` is ticked: so the board asked while there was no choice. The output limit is one at all levels — 16,000 tokens, so that the levels are compared at one limit, and the board waits up to 10 minutes for an answer. GPT-6 Astra, `gpt-6-astra`, — only with an OpenAI key, only with `high`, and not ticked by default: GPT is not connected to the board (R20).
- **Without thinking** Haiku 5.5 and Sonnet 5.5 can be asked, at `low`, `medium` and `high`: for Haiku the board sends `thinking: {type: "disabled"}`, for Sonnet `between_tools`. For Opus 5.5 and Fable 5.1 thinking does not turn off (sources 1.6, sources 1.7).
- **Claude's answer** the board receives as a stream: so it is seen when the first text of the answer came — counted from the sending of the attempt that answered. GPT is without a stream, and it does not have this time.

**Tasks** — 11 constant ones. The arrows above the board go over them in a circle: from the first task "←" leads to the last.
- **Tasks 1–5** — from the bank of the measurement of the course of writing, `eval/bank.json` (section 3.1): «Реши уравнение 3x + 1 = 7», «Реши уравнение 2(x − 3) = 10», «Вычисли 17 + 49 − 14 × 2», «Вычисли (47 + 19) : 6» and the task about posts along a path 20 m long.
- **Tasks 6–11** — with free writing, from the bank `eval/free-bank.json` (T52; measurements 3.1):
  - on a number line — a grasshopper jumps from point 3, and «задумали число», which is found from the end;
  - with a drawing — a row of houses, where the house with a flag is fourth from the left and sixth from the right, and a scheme of segments: the stamps of two brothers;
  - with counting at the side — how many digits there are in the page numbers from 1 to 75, and how much albums and notebooks cost.

The page receives only the statements; the answers and the reference solutions stay on the board's server. A test of `cmd/tutor-eval` holds each task of the board equal to its copy in one of the two banks.

**How to run it** — in the devcontainer's terminal:

```
just board
```

The recipe asks for the keys for eval — Anthropic, then OpenAI — and does not show them on the screen, so that a key does not get into the shell's history; Enter skips a model. A key that already stands in `TUTOR_ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` or `TUTOR_OPENAI_API_KEY` it does not ask for. Then the recipe calls `go run ./cmd/tutor-eval board` from the repository's root.

The board is ready when the terminal writes «The board is at http://127.0.0.1:8080»: it listens on this address, and VS Code opens it in the browser on the author's computer. If port 8080 is busy on the computer, VS Code forwards it under another number — for example, `localhost:36615` — and the board answers there too; the number is in the Ports tab of VS Code. While the board is not running, the page at this address loads without end: VS Code forwards the port, but there is no one to answer on it. Flags go after `just board`, for example `just board -max-usd 10`: `-addr` — another local address; `-dir` — the directory of the pictures and the log, by default `samples/board`; `-max-usd` — the spending limit per launch, by default $5. The board refuses a non-local address: it spends the keys for eval. Without the Anthropic key the board has no Claude models, without the OpenAI key no GPT.

**Cost** — each model has its own, per million input and output tokens: Haiku 5.5 — $0.10 and $0.50, Sonnet 5.5 — $2 and $10, Opus 5.5 — $4 and $20, Fable 5.1 — $10 and $50 (sources 1.3; T40). In the first series Opus 5.5 with `high` answered for about $0.01 (R20); the board shows the exact cost of each answer and the mean by pair. The worst case of one answer is 4,000 input tokens and 16,000 output tokens: from $0.0084 for Haiku 5.5 to $0.84 for Fable 5.1. The checks go one at a time, and a check that in the worst case would go beyond `-max-usd` the board does not start; it counts the worst case by the ticked models. A call cut off by the clock or by a closed page brings no bill, and the board counts it into the spending by the worst case. It counts the same way an answer cut off in the middle of the stream: the input tokens have already come, but their total has not yet.
- **Without analysis** the visible answer is 16–51 tokens instead of about 150–250, by the series of T45 (measurements 2.1). For Haiku 5.5 without thinking a check costs about $0.0002, so the board shows the cost with five decimal places. For Opus 5.5 · `high` — about $0.008 and 2.5 s.
