The path is as in the cycle of concepts 9.1 spec, and each capability has its own prompt and eval set. A separate reading holds the learner's errors more exactly. But there are two calls, and the question arises how many readers an essay has (R68 spec).
- **Decided** (R53, 2026-11-01): two calls, and one reader reads the essay. How the assessment is built is section 3.5. The measurement on essays of adult volunteers (П-16) checks this path and measures one call beside it, and if one call turns out better, the question is reopened by a new decision. The edit of the card and the clarification of R68 spec are carried by rows В58 and В59 «в реестр spec». *2026-11-01 (T100; R58): `spec` recorded both — R75 spec and the second 2026-11-01 amendment to R68 spec.*
- **Task:** T46 — done (R53). The data — the measurement of essays of E4, T82–T84 (R28), and the extended measurement, T92 and T95 (R50).

### 3.6. Measures for minors

**2026-11-01** (R54): the item of the card architecture 3.6 spec — the measures for minors that the providers require — is decided by subsection 2.2. There are two providers, OpenAI and Anthropic: Google's models are closed on the platform (R47 spec). Their terms were checked again on 2026-11-01, the quotes and dates — sources 1.11. The retelling is ours, not a legal one: whether these measures suffice, a lawyer and `spec` decide (П-15).

| Measure | Who names it | Where it is carried out | How |
|---|---|---|---|
| disclosure that the learner is talking with AI, not with a human | Anthropic — "must", and the Usage Policy — "at a minimum at the beginning of each chat session or in the product interface"; OpenAI — "age-appropriate disclosures to minors about AI tools" | `app` | to all learners, by the recommendation of П-15 (architecture 3.1 spec) |
| how to use AI safely and responsibly | Anthropic — "Educational resources and guidance for minors"; OpenAI — the same measure as the disclosure | `app` | beside the disclosure; what is in these materials, the detailing of `app` decides |
| parental consent | OpenAI — Services Agreement, para. 3.3(c) | `api` | the records of the account owner's consent (architecture 3.5 spec); verifiable consent under COPPA — П-1 |
| age verification | Anthropic — "Age verification systems"; OpenAI — "Where required or otherwise appropriate", "age assurance systems" | `api` and the account owner | the account owner states the age, and the platform stores the age category (R06 spec; privacy 3.3 spec). How to confirm that the account owner is an adult is П-6, and a teenager's account in the first slice is under an adult (П-13). `tutor` gets the age category with the request |
| content filters | OpenAI — "age-appropriate content filters"; Anthropic — "Content moderation and filtering" | `tutor` | without a separate moderation call (R54). At the output, the content is held by the safety rules in `tutor`'s prompts and the item about safety in the eval sets. The checks of the words in `api` — the leak by `module-kit`'s code, the form of the hint and the feedback rule by `tutor`'s code (R70 spec) — hold the form and the length, and do not look at the content. At the input there is no filter of our own: the work goes to the model as it is, and only the provider's classifiers see it. The prohibition of OpenAI's Usage Policies, current and new, — "exposing minors to age-inappropriate content" — is held by the prompt and eval |
| monitoring and reporting | OpenAI — "reasonable monitoring and reporting mechanisms, including escalation paths for high-risk interactions"; Anthropic — "Monitoring and reporting mechanisms" | only the provider (R54) | the platform builds no monitoring of its own: `tutor` and `api` flag nothing and store nothing. On the platform's side this measure is not met — row В60 «в реестр spec» |
| COPPA and a statement of compliance with it | both; Anthropic — "clearly stated on the organization’s website" | the platform and a lawyer | not code. Consent and the basis of processing — П-1, the statement on the site — with the lawyer's answer |
| zero data retention for under 13 or under the age of digital consent | OpenAI — without it, "should not" | the deployment: the agreement with OpenAI | only "subject to prior approval by OpenAI". Until it exists, OpenAI is closed for these learners — our conclusion from "should not". It is a deployment fact for the admission function (T05; П-3) |
| flagship models | OpenAI — a best practice | the list of admitted models of `tutor` | an argument in choosing a model, not a rule: a model is admitted only by eval (R07 spec) |
| a child system prompt | Anthropic — "may provide" | `tutor`'s prompts | no published one was found. When Anthropic gives it, it will enter the safety rules for children |
| a user identifier for monitoring | OpenAI — `safety_identifier`, "recommended but not required" | not sent | `tutor`'s request types have no identifiers (R15 spec) |

- **The provider's monitoring** — what it gives in practice:
  - at Anthropic, the Trust & Safety automation works under zero data retention too: it may keep what it flagged for up to 2 years, and Anthropic, by its own words, checks its API for violations of the Usage Policy;
  - at OpenAI, the abuse logs by default keep content for up to 30 days. But for under 13 or under the age of digital consent, OpenAI says not to process data without zero data retention, and with it the content drops out of these logs. Then the customer itself is responsible for the moderation and reporting that the law requires;
  - there is ZDR with Private Safety Processing: an automatic check without storage at OpenAI. But it writes its records to the customer's own storage, for 30 days, and the platform's server does not store works (R09 spec). So for younger learners OpenAI's abuse logs have no content — except pictures and files suspected of CSAM —, and the page does not exclude the classifiers' outputs from them. Whether this is enough as monitoring is a question to the lawyer in row В61;
  - OpenAI checks pictures for CSAM under any storage mode.
- **What `tutor` does by code:**
  - the safety rules for children — in all prompts, for all learners. They are not switched on by age category: then the provider would learn that the learner is a minor, and privacy 5.1 spec does not name the age category in what goes to AI;
  - the item about safety — in the eval set of each capability that writes words to the learner, beside the item about gender (R70 spec). A model whose words stray from the task or do not suit the age, when the learner's work holds something alarming or unsuitable, does not pass admission; *2026-11-08 (T118; R85): for the hint the item about safety is the sheets of the set of words with working at the side of five kinds, sadness, a rude word, a fight or a threat, personal data and a request that is an instruction, and the judge of the words, a model checked on control phrases. It judges each hint — whether it is about the task, whether it suits the age, whether it picks up the working. The code counts the echo of the working. Section 3.4, "The set of words". The feedback by a rubric has the same item — T125;*
  - to the provider — no identifiers. It flags nothing and stores nothing.
- **A measure that requires data that the platform does not collect** (privacy 1 spec): the data are not collected. If the measure is not met without them, the provider is closed for that age category, and this is an input of the admission function (T05). There are two such measures now. Confirmation of age beyond the account owner's statement OpenAI asks for "Where required or otherwise appropriate", so where the law requires it, it is mandatory (П-6). `safety_identifier` is not mandatory.
- **What this means for admission** (T05; Т-20) — how the function holds it is section 3.9 (R57):
  - the deployment facts — whether a DPA is signed, whether zero data retention is approved at OpenAI for under 13 and under the age of digital consent (П-3) — are in the configuration;
  - parental consent is checked by `api`, before the attempt;
  - monitoring on the platform's side is not met. Under the strict option of П-15 — "platform AI for under 18 only at providers whose measures are met" — platform AI is closed to children until there is monitoring of our own, and the first slice is for children (R41 spec). This is row В60.
- **Own key** (П-4): the organization facing the provider is then the key's owner, and the measures fall on them. The recommendation of О-4 is an own key only for 18+.

*2026-11-10 (T129; R90): `spec` decided the measures for minors, R121–R123 spec, and П-3, П-4 and П-15 are closed. The platform keeps the measures as a list by provider, with the status of each. To the provider's monitoring it adds its own: an adult's report, "report a problem", at the tutor's answer, and an escalation path at the operator — this is `app` and `api` (R123 spec), and `tutor` still flags nothing and stores nothing. `tutor`'s share:*
- *the routes under zero data retention — Responses with `store: false` and the picture in the request body at OpenAI, Messages without the Files API and Batch at Anthropic (R121 spec). The adapters' tests hold the request path, `store: false` and the picture `data:` in the body;*
- *Anthropic's models that need storage of 30 days — Fable 5 and 5.1, Mythos 5 and 5.1, — are outside the list of the admitted for learners' works. `TestAdmitted` does not let Fable 5.1 into the list, the only one of them that the adapter calls;*
- *not a single call judges the learner's feelings (R123 spec): the program gives the verdict, not AI (R03 spec). The reading prompt says to add no words of its own and not to judge the learner — "no judgement of the learner", — the hint prompt says to speak only about the task, and the judge of the words counts words about the learner's feelings as "not about the task";*
- *the safety system prompt for minors "by Anthropic's article": the article does not publish it. "Guidelines for Organizations Serving Minors" (support.claude.com/en/articles/9307344, updated 2026-03-16, read 2026-11-10) says: "we may provide a child-safety system prompt, which organizations serving minors should implement", — and the text itself is neither there nor in the linked "Child safety guidance for developers" (15591275, updated 2026-06-26, read 2026-11-10). There is nothing to check `tutor`'s prompts against — row В75 «В реестр spec». When the prompt exists, it will enter as a new version of the prompts, with eval, in T127 and a repeat measurement of reading: an edit of the prompt removes the admission of reading until a run returns it (R80).*

*2026-11-11 (T130; R91): `spec` answered В75 with the 2026-11-10 amendment to R123 spec, by option (b): the measure "filters" is met by the safety rules for minors in all of `tutor`'s prompts and by the item about safety in the eval sets, and the system prompt by Anthropic's article enters it only if Anthropic gives it. The card architecture 3.6 spec says the same. This changes neither the prompts nor the eval sets of `tutor`: the row "a child system prompt" in the table above and the item about it in the note of 2026-11-10 stay as they were — Anthropic's prompt, if it comes, will enter as a new version of the prompts, with eval (R80). The same tag checked the platform against the laws of US states (R140 spec): Texas, Bus. & Com. Code §552.052 and §552.057, prohibits AI built to push toward harm to oneself or to others or toward a crime, and AI that depicts a child in a sexual conversation, — and privacy 3.4 spec assigns this to the same safety rules of `tutor` and to not a single call judging the learner's feelings, with no new work for `tutor`.*

**How it was decided** — the former subsection 2.2, moved here word for word on 2026-11-01 (T06; R59):

- **The point:** which measures for minors the AI providers require — age verification, filters, monitoring, disclosure that the learner is talking with AI, — and which of them `tutor` carries out, and which `api` and `app`. This is an item of the card (architecture 3.6 spec). The question itself is П-15 from the register of `spec`; it is decided by epic E4 «Приватность до первого ребёнка» in `spec`'s RUN.md. So here `tutor`'s share is decided, and the list of measures and what the task finds out about П-15 go «в реестр spec».
- **What is known:**
  - what the providers say about minors is privacy 5.3 spec, checked on 2026-10-18, and the row of Google Cloud on 2026-10-22; the sources — sources 2.1, 2.2 and 2.4 spec. The terms change, so before each decision about an AI route they are checked again (privacy 5.3 spec):
    - OpenAI: for under 18 — additional measures: filters by age, monitoring, disclosure that it is AI. For personal data of children under 13 or under the age of digital consent zero data retention is needed, and OpenAI turns it on only by prior approval;
    - Anthropic: serving minors through the API is allowed with protective measures, among them age verification, filters and monitoring; COPPA must be complied with, and it must be disclosed that the user is talking with AI, not with a human;
    - Google: Google's models are closed on the platform — both the Gemini API in the AI Studio variant and Vertex AI. The Service Specific Terms of Google Cloud, changed on 2026-10-12, in §20(d) prohibit generative AI in a service "directed towards or is likely to be accessed by individuals under the age of 18" (sources 1.1), and privacy 5.3 spec says so by R47 spec. A separate deployment only for adults is a question to a lawyer (П-3);
  - the recommendation of П-15 is a list of measures for each provider, with source and date; the disclosure that it is AI `app` shows to all learners (architecture 3.1 spec). Another option of П-15 is platform AI for under 18 only at providers whose measures are met;
  - the account owner states the age, and the platform stores the age category, the country and the date the age was stated (R06 spec; privacy 3.3 spec). How to confirm that the account owner is an adult is П-6; a teenager's account is П-13, its recommendation — under an adult until 18;
  - `tutor` stores nothing, and the logs are without content: neither works, nor what was read, nor the text of hints (architecture 1 spec; R09 spec). How the monitoring that the providers require fits with this is not decided. *2026-11-01 (R54): monitoring is only at the provider, section 3.6;*
  - with an own key Anthropic's measures fall on the key's owner, that is, on the family. Whether this is so, and whether the platform's measures suffice, is a question to a lawyer (privacy 5.3 spec; П-4);
  - processor agreements and zero data retention at each provider — before the first learners' data (П-3);
  - **checked again** on 2026-11-01 (T02): what was found beyond privacy 5.3 spec is section 3.6, and the quotes are sources 1.11.
- **Options:**
  - monitoring: a flag without content — the model flags alarming work with a code, `api` writes an event and tells the account owner, and the work stays on the device; storing flagged works for review by a human, with an amendment to R09 spec; only the provider's monitoring;
  - filters: without a separate call — rules in the prompts, program checks of the words and the item about safety in the eval sets; the provider's moderation of the tutor's words.
- **Decided** (R54, 2026-11-01): monitoring is only at the provider, against the recommendation "a flag without content"; the filter is without a separate call. Which measures are carried out where is section 3.6. The measure of monitoring is not met on the platform's side, and whether the provider's monitoring is enough, a lawyer and `spec` decide — rows В60 and В61 «в реестр spec».
- **Task:** T02 — done (R54).

### 3.7. The spending limit of an own key and the usage of a call

**2026-11-01** (R55): the item of the card architecture 3.6 spec — the spending limit of an own key — is decided by subsection 2.3. The providers' terms were checked the same day, the quotes — sources 1.12. There is no product code yet: the epic after T06 builds it, and here is what it builds.

- **The limit is at the provider.** The key's owner sets a monthly spending limit at the provider itself (concepts 14 spec: the key's owner sets the limit):
  - at Anthropic — on a workspace, and the key is issued in the same workspace. A key without a workspace chooses one by a header of each request (sources 1.12), and the limit of one workspace does not hold it. A spending limit cannot be set on the default workspace, and the limit of the whole organization is on the Billing page. Only the organization's admins create workspaces, and whether the owner of a personal account can, the pages do not say;
  - at OpenAI — the project's hard limit, with the switch "Enforce a hard limit", and a key of this project, or the limit of the whole organization. A spending alert does not stop requests.

  The platform holds no limit of its own and accumulates no usage of an own key — neither `tutor`, nor `api`, nor the device. Hence:
  - the usage from all devices and from other programs on the same key is counted together: it is the key owner's bill at the provider (privacy 5.2 spec). A leaked key of a workspace or a project also spends no more than their limit;
  - the limit is only monthly: the documentation of both providers names the limits of the organization and of the workspace or project, and names no limit per key or per day. A monthly limit per user at Anthropic exists only in the Claude Code workspace, and keys cannot be issued in it by hand;
  - whether a limit is set, the platform cannot check. Reading the usage and the limits the documentation describes only in the admin APIs: at Anthropic they need Admin API credentials, and a workspace key will not do, at OpenAI the examples use an Admin API key (sources 1.12). That an ordinary key cannot read them is, at OpenAI, our conclusion. Without a limit of their own, the owner is held only by the tier's cap — $500 a month for Anthropic's Start tier, $100 for OpenAI's Free tier — and by the prepaid balance. Anthropic's Evaluation tier has lower caps, and its page does not name them;
  - at OpenAI the limit does not trigger at once, and the usage may slightly exceed it;
  - slots with a verdict are read by two readers (R68 spec), so such a check spends twice as much on reading. An essay is read by one reader (R53).
- **A refusal for the limit is an outcome of `tutor`'s own.** A refusal of the provider because of the limit, the balance or the payment `tutor` gives as the outcome "limit reached", separately from an AI failure. Repeating such a call is useless until the owner raises the limit, tops up the balance, or a new month begins. Both SDKs by default retry a 429 answer twice themselves, before the adapter even sees the error. So the adapter does not let the SDK retry the refusals of the table below — by a request middleware that looks at the code of the refusal. The number of retries for the whole client does not do for this: without retries an ordinary 429 of a rate limit, 408, 409 and 5xx would at once become an AI failure.

  | Provider | Answer | What it is |
  |---|---|---|
  | Anthropic | 400 `invalid_request_error`, the text starts with "You have reached your specified API usage limits" or "You have reached your specified workspace API usage limits" | the limit of the organization or of the workspace that the owner set |
  | Anthropic | 429 `rate_limit_error`, `error.details.error_code` — `enforced_spend_limit_reached`, without `retry-after` | the tier's cap, until 00:00 UTC on the first day of the next month |
  | Anthropic | the text "Your credit balance is too low to access the Anthropic API" | the balance is exhausted. The page on the subscription's credits does not name the status: check it by the API's answer once there is code |
  | Anthropic | 402 `billing_error` | something is wrong with the payment. It is not a limit, but it too only the owner removes, so the outcome is the same, with the cause "payment" |
  | OpenAI | 429, `error.code` — `project_spend_limit_exceeded` or `organization_spend_limit_exceeded` | the limit of the project or of the organization that the owner set |
  | OpenAI | 429, `error.code` — `organization_usage_limit_exceeded` | the tier's cap |
  | OpenAI | 429, `error.code` — `credit_balance_exhausted` | the balance is exhausted |

  The outcome carries the cause: the limit that the owner set, the tier's cap, the balance or the payment. Only the key's owner can remove each, and they must be told which one: when the payment is refused, raising the limit is useless. The outcome is one on both AI routes, and what it means, `api` decides. On an own key it is a reason to tell the key's owner, and on platform AI a signal to the operator: the refusal is to the platform's key.
- **Two readers.** If the provider refused even one reader of handwriting for the limit, the reading gives the outcome "limit reached" as a whole. It does not give the observations of one reader: the pair holds the verdict (R68 spec). But the call of the reader that answered is paid for, and its record stays in the usage message.
- **What the learner sees.** A limit reached is not the learner's error (R04 spec):
  - at reading — the verdict "unchecked" and a neutral phrase (concepts 9.1 spec);
  - at the analysis and the hint — the fallback text of the rung: the model's question did not come, and then the learner gets the fallback text (concepts 10 spec; R57 spec). It needs no AI. How this fits with the neutral phrase at an AI failure (architecture 4.1 spec), the detailing of `api` decides;
  - at assessment by the rubric — a neutral phrase, and the criteria stay without an assessment, as at a failure of the call (section 3.5).

  The key's owner learns that the limit at the provider is reached. What to show them and where, `api` and `app` decide — row В62 «в реестр spec».
- **The usage message** — one per call of the library, the same for platform AI and an own key (architecture 3.5, 3.6 spec):
  - it has a record for each call of a model: reading handwriting has two, one per reader (R68 spec), and reading an essay one (R53);
  - in the record — the provider and the model, as the provider named it in the answer. Also the input tokens without cache, the cache writes, the cache reads and the output, and of the output tokens, how many went to thinking. The input tokens add up from these three kinds, with no overlap. The providers count them differently: at Anthropic `input_tokens` does not include the cache, and at OpenAI the cache tokens are part of `input_tokens`, and the adapter subtracts them (sources 1.12, sources 1.14); Thinking at both providers is paid as output and is included in the output tokens (sources 1.12);
  - whether the count is exact. An answer cut off before the tokens were counted, and a call interrupted before the count, are counted by the worst case, as in eval: the largest input that the call kind names, and the request's `MaxOutputTokens` (`provider.Billed`; `runner.Step.Worst`);
  - an estimate in dollars — by `tutor`'s price table. It has for each admitted model the prices of input, of cache writes for each retention period, of cache reads and of output, the long-context prices where there are any, and the date they were read. The table comes out with a release of `tutor`, and the prices in it are checked before each release. It is an estimate: the provider issues the bill;
  - the usage comes with an answer without the needed JSON too — a refusal of the model or an answer that ran out of tokens (`provider.ErrNoAnswer`): such a call is paid. The very call that was refused for the limit has no record;
  - the message has neither content nor identifiers, and it may be written to logs (R09 spec);
  - by it `api` counts the platform AI quotas (architecture 3.5 spec). On an own key `api` does not accumulate it: the limit is at the provider.
- **What the epic of code builds** after T06:
  - the tokens of cache writes by retention period and of cache reads in `provider.Response`: now it has only input, output and thinking;
  - the type of the usage message in the package `tutor` and the price table with cache and date. It grows from the adapters' prices (`provider.Model`), and does not stand as a second copy beside them;
  - the outcome "limit reached": the adapters recognize the refusals of the table above and do not let the SDK retry them. Tests — one case per row of it.

*2026-11-11 (T120; R94): built:*
- *`provider.Response` carries the input tokens without cache, the cache writes by retention period, `CacheWrites`, and the cache reads, `CacheReadTokens`. The Claude adapter takes them from `usage` as they are, and a write that `usage` does not split by period it counts as five-minute — Anthropic's default period. The GPT adapter subtracts from `input_tokens` both the cache reads and the cache writes, and a write without a period it counts as thirty-minute;*
- *the prices — `provider.Prices`: input, output, cache reads, writes for each period, the long-prompt prices and the date read; `Prices.Cost` estimates an answer and tells whether the prices cover all its tokens. For Haiku 5.5 a prompt longer than 100,000 tokens, cache counted, goes by its own prices; for `gpt-6-astra` the prices beyond 272,000 tokens have not been read, and there is no estimate there. Claude's prices are in `provider/claude/catalog`, their date is `catalog.PricesRead`, the sources — sources 1.14;*
- *`Usage` — the usage record — carries the cache and the estimate in dollars, `USD`, and `Priced` — whether the prices cover it; prices without a date read cover nothing. A call whose tokens are not its whole bill is estimated by the worst case: the largest input of the kind, or more if that much went to the cache, and the request's `MaxOutputTokens`. Cache tokens are counted even when there is no input without cache: the usage record stays even for a call whose prompt was read entirely from the cache;*
- *the price table — `Tutor.Prices`: each model of the list of the admitted whose adapter gave a config, once, with its adapter's prices — not as a second copy;*
- *the price of a call — `Tutor.Price` by capability and route: the expected and the worst, by the models of the chosen entry that the call asks — both readers of the pair, and for the words the first model. The prices are taken from the adapter without a key, and the library does not touch an own key for the sake of a price. The expected tokens are constants in the code from the measurements: reading — Opus 5.5 · `high` 1,354 of input and 177 of output, Haiku 5.5 · `medium` 1,354 and 502 (T126, 180 calls each); words — Opus 5.5 · `high` 2,255 and 358 (T119, 300 calls). The largest input of the kind is the limit of the request's text, 24,000 bytes of instructions and text together, and the largest crop that the library takes, ⌈4096 / 28⌉² = 21,609 tokens. A request longer than the limit the library rejects before the call: a token takes at least a byte of text, so there are no more tokens in the text than bytes — our conclusion —, and the request of the words takes about 5,400 bytes. The worst price is each input token at the higher of the input and cache-write prices, and the largest output. A model without a measurement has no expected price, and `api` takes the value of its configuration (В13 `api`);* *2026-11-13 (T138; R106): the hint without the work, `CallHint`, has no expected tokens — T127 measured the hint with the work —, so its price is the worst alone, by the bound of the text without a picture, and `api` takes the expected from its own config (В13 api);*
- *`MaxOutputTokens` — 16,000 for reading and for the words, the same bound as the eval runner's: a bound narrower than the runner's would give the platform a call that eval did not measure (R94).*

*2026-11-11 (T121; R95): built:*
- *the adapters recognize the refusals of the table above by the provider's answer — the status code, the text and the error code — and give them as the errors `provider.LimitError`, with a cause, and `provider.ErrKeyRefused`. A key refusal is a 401 at both providers: the key is invalid, revoked or expired (R84 spec). A 401 of Anthropic whose text begins with "CORS requests are not allowed for this Organization" is not the key but a request from a browser, and it is an AI failure. A 403 "no rights" is an outcome of the key check, T122;*
- *the middleware `provider.NoRetry` reads the start of the body of each response with an error and sets the header `x-should-retry: false` on a refusal: both SDKs heed it and do not retry such a response, and the ordinary 429, 408, 409 and 5xx they retry, as before;*
- *for a pair the outcome takes the heaviest refusal: the limit of even one reader makes the whole of it "limit reached", then a key refusal, then an AI failure. The usage of the reader that answered stays in the usage message, and a refused call has no tokens and no record;*
- *the text of the answer is not a contract: if the provider changes the words, the refusal becomes an AI failure until the table and its tests catch up. The sources are checked again before each release of `tutor`, as the prices are (sources 1.14, sources 1.15).*

*2026-11-11 (T122; R96): the check of an own key is `Tutor.CheckKey`. `api` calls it when the owner connects a key, and does not go to the providers round `tutor` (R84 spec):*
- *the route is the own key, with the profile's age category. The key is checked for every model the library will call with it: the models that `Choose` chooses for each capability of a call, both readers of a pair (*2026-11-13, R106: those that `ChooseCall` chooses for each call kind, the hint without the work among them*). The request is without the learner's content and free, the adapter's `provider.Model.Check`. At Anthropic, a count of the tokens of one word for the model, `/v1/messages/count_tokens`: "Token counting is free to use". At OpenAI, the description of the model, `GET /v1/models/gpt-6-astra`: it writes no tokens, and OpenAI charges for tokens, so it is free — our inference; the pricing page names no price for it (sources 1.16);*
- *the outcomes are the same as those of `spec`'s contract `OwnKeyCheck`: "accepted"; "invalid" — a 401, the key is invalid, revoked or expired; "the key has no rights" — a 403 at a model; "provider closed" — the admission function does not open the provider to the profile's own key, or the provider has no adapter, and there was no call; "the provider did not answer". A 404 of a model is not the key's fault: perhaps the provider no longer has the model — and it is "did not answer". Of the models' outcomes the worst is taken: an invalid key, then no rights, then did not answer. A refusal for a limit at the check means the key is accepted: the limit will show on the calls. A refusal is not retried (`provider.NoRetry`, R95), and a panic of the adapter is not a panic of `api`;*
- *the key lives only in the memory of the request: it is neither in the error nor in the log, and the tests hold this.*

*2026-11-13 (T139; R107): each record of the usage message carries its model's time, so that the log of `api` shows which reader holds a pair and how long the words take (Д222, DEBT.md of `spec`):*
- *`Usage.Latency` — from when the library asked the adapter to when the answer or the failure came back, the SDK's retries counted in, and `Usage.Retries` — how many times the SDK sent the request again, so that a retried call does not pass for a slow model. The library times it around `provider.Model.Ask`, the readers of a pair each in its own goroutine: the span the eval runner times as `latency_ms`, so eval and `api` measure one thing (R70 spec) — but for the runner's first call of a model, which also builds its client, without the network. A call refused for the limit has no record, so no time either;*
- *`Usage.FirstToken` — when the first text of the answer came, counted from the attempt that answered, as the adapter measures it (`provider.Response.FirstToken`). The Claude adapter streams and measures it, and gives zero when no text came — a refusal, an answer that broke off while thinking; the GPT adapter does not measure it, and its records carry zero;*
- *what eval measured of it: the reading pair — measurements 3.13, the hint with the work on prompt version 3 — measurements 3.16.*

**How it was decided** — the former subsection 2.3, moved here word for word 2026-11-01 (T06; R59):

- **The point:** how the owner of an own key limits how much the platform spends from their key: where the limit is set, who counts and checks it, and what `tutor` reports about each call for it. It is an item of the card (architecture 3.6 spec). The key lies on the device, and `api` counts the platform AI quotas, so what the limit requires of `app` and `api` goes «в реестр spec».
- **What is known:**
  - the limit of platform AI is a daily quota of the tutor's help, as in the prototype; on an own key, the key's owner sets the limit (concepts 14 spec);
  - `tutor` reports the usage of each call, and `api` counts the platform AI quotas by it (architecture 3.5, 3.6 spec). How the limit of an own key is counted, `spec` does not say;
  - the key is stored on the device and comes with the request; in `api` and `tutor` it lives only in the memory of the request (architecture 4.3 spec; privacy 5.2 spec). The recommendation of Т-9 is on the device, and an adult's key is bound to their profile: for a child's profile `api` does not accept it;
  - the calls go in the name of the key's owner: the provider sees their account and links each request to them, and the provider's terms are between the provider and the key's owner (privacy 5.2 spec);
  - who an own key is for is О-4 and П-4; the recommendation of О-4 is only for 18+. Among the risks of an own key is that it leaks (product 12 spec);
  - an exhausted limit is not the learner's error: the verdict "unchecked" and a neutral phrase (concepts 9.1 spec; R04 spec);
  - whether the providers have spending limits of their own per key, and how much a call costs, `spec` does not say. These are external facts: the task takes them from the providers' documentation, with a quote and the date read, into [sources.md](sources.md). *2026-11-01 (T03): checked — sources 1.12. At both providers the spending limit is monthly: per organization and per workspace of Anthropic, other than the default workspace, or per organization and project of OpenAI. A limit per key or per day the documentation does not name.*
- **Options:**
  - where the limit is:
    - at the provider — the monthly limit of an Anthropic workspace or an OpenAI project. Whoever issues the bill counts it, though at OpenAI it does not trigger at once. It is shared by all the devices and programs on the key and holds a leaked key too, and the platform stores nothing. But it is only monthly, and whether it is set the platform cannot check;
    - at the provider and also in `api`, behind the profile of the key's owner: the owner sets a limit in dollars per day or month, and `api` accumulates the usage by the reports of `tutor` and refuses by itself. The devices are counted together, but these are new data of `api` that privacy 4 spec does not name: the counter, and the price table on which the limit rests;
    - at the provider and also on the device, beside the key (Т-9): the device accumulates the usage by the answers of `api`. The server stores nothing new, but the usage of several devices does not add up;
  - what the limit is in: at the provider, in dollars per month. At the platform, in dollars by `tutor`'s price table, in calls or in tokens, and the calls and tokens of different models cost differently;
  - what `tutor` reports about the usage: the model, the tokens and an estimate in dollars by its price table — or only the model and the tokens, and then `api` would know the models' prices.
- **Decided** (R55, 2026-11-01): the limit is at the provider, and the platform keeps none of its own. A provider's refusal for a limit `tutor` gives as a separate outcome. The usage message is one for both AI routes: the model, the tokens and an estimate in dollars. In detail — section 3.7. What this requires of `api`, `app` and concepts 14 spec is row В62 «в реестр spec».
- **Task:** T03 — done (R55).

### 3.8. Voice

**2026-11-01** (R56): the item of the card architecture 3.6 spec — voice (Т-13) — is decided by subsection 2.4 in part: the boundary of voice between `tutor` and the device, and its rules. The route of voice waits for stage 3 (product 11 spec), and subsection 2.4 stays open. There are no external facts here: the facts are from `spec`'s documents, and the rules of voice are derived from them. There is no code of voice, and there will be none before stage 3.

- **The boundary.** Voice is the tutor's voicing and the learner's voice input (concepts 10 spec):

  | Part | Where | How | When |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | voicing by the device's speech synthesis | `app` | the same text that passed the checks of `api`, by one synthesis for the whole app and with one set of rules for voices (R36 spec). By the recommendation of Т-13, only local voices, whose attribute `localService` is true (architecture 8 spec). `tutor` gives only the text | when `app` decides its item "voicing, at first by the device's speech synthesis" (architecture 3.1 spec). To all learners: it is output, not data collection (privacy 6 spec) |
  | voicing by an AI provider | `tutor`, the capability "voice" | synthesis of the same checked text, with admission, as for any call of a model. `app` does not call AI providers (architecture 3.1 spec) | stage 3 — if the device's speech synthesis is not enough |
  | speech to speech: the model itself answers the learner by voice | nowhere | closed for the tutor's words (R56) | — |
  | recognition of the learner's speech | `tutor`, the capability "voice" | `app` records the sound and sends it to `api`, and `api` to `tutor`. The sound lives only in the memory of the request (privacy 4 spec; architecture 3.5 spec). What is recognized is an observation, and `module-kit` gives the verdict on it (R03 spec) | stage 3: the first slice has no voice input (product 10 spec) |
  | pronunciation assessment | `tutor`, the capability "voice" | a rubric: its result is feedback, not a verdict (concepts 9.2 spec; R04 spec). The feedback rule R70 spec was made for essays, and whether its bounds and its quote rule suit feedback on pronunciation is decided at stage 3 — row В63 | stage 3, together with the step "pronounce" (concepts 7 spec) |
  | the persona's voice | with the device's speech synthesis, `app`; with voicing by a provider, a call of `tutor` | the persona changes the tone and the voice, but not the content (concepts 10 spec). What the presentation style consists of is decided by `app` and `tutor`, and what a module says about it is О-23 | with voicing |

- **The tutor's words are only checked text.** What the learner will hear first passes everything that text passes: the leak check and the form check (architecture 3.5 spec; R70 spec). A hint that did not pass them is replaced by a fallback text (concepts 10 spec), and what the learner sees in place of feedback that did not pass the feedback rule is decided by the detailing of `api` (R70 spec). Hence:
  - speech to speech is closed for the tutor's words. A model that itself answers the learner by voice would give them the words before the check, and R54 spec requires giving them whole, once the whole text is checked. And it would itself decide what to say, rather than fit words to the rung the program chose (R03 spec). That is the argument of Т-13 (R05 spec), and also of R54 spec and R70 spec;
  - the model writes no separate text for speech: a number written in words the key search does not find — only leak phrases catch it, if the author gave them (concepts 10 spec). If the synthesis needs a notation — `3/4` — written in words, the code of `app` does it, after the checks;
  - R54 spec allows a stream only to a reading call, whose answer the program reads. Recognition may stream by the same argument — its answer the program reads too —, but this is `tutor`'s inference, and row В63 carries it. Whether a real-time channel is then needed is Т-25: it is returned to together with voice.
- **Voice input is like reading the work.** Answers by voice are the learner's work too (concepts 8 spec), and their check rules are the same — this is our inference from the rules of reading:
  - doubt about what was recognized is a request to repeat, not "not yet", like "unreadable" for handwriting (R04 spec). An AI failure is a neutral phrase;
  - the learner sees what was recognized, as they see what was read (R05 spec);
  - the sound goes to the model without identifiers, the pseudonym and the adult's data (privacy 5.1 spec). But the recording may hold anything the learner says — as a picture may show their name (privacy 1 spec);
  - recognition on the device is an option of stage 3. If it is chosen, the sound does not go to the network, and the model is `tutor`'s code, like a reading model on the device: weights with a digest in a release of `tutor`, eval and admission (R66 spec). Recognition by the means of the browser or the system will not do: its model is not from a release of `tutor` and has not passed admission, and if it sends the sound to the network, the sound goes to a third-party service past `tutor` (R66 spec; architecture 3.1 spec).
- **What voice requires of admission** — an input of T05 (Т-20):
  - the capability "voice" has no admitted models, so the function closes it to everyone until a model passes the eval set of voice (R07 spec; concepts 14 spec). So before stage 3 the function needs no new inputs;
  - voice input is only for learners 18 and older, until the lawyer has answered on П-5 (privacy 6 spec; О-6);
  - if voice input is allowed to children, then by the recommendation of П-5 it is without storage and at a provider with zero data retention. Whether a provider's zero data retention covers sound is a deployment fact; it is checked at stage 3;
  - voicing itself does not depend on the age category: it is output, not data collection (privacy 6 spec). But voicing by a provider passes the provider's admission by category, as any call does: the tutor's words may hold the learner's work — feedback quotes the essay (R07 spec; section 3.5). And while "voice" is one capability, the second part of the admission table closes it to minors too (section 3.9);
  - the rules are the same for platform AI and an own key (Т-13).

  How the function deals with voice input, R57 proposes to `spec` as the second part of the admission table, "capability × category" (section 3.9; row В64). While "voice" is one capability, input cannot be told from voicing by the function's inputs: whether to split it is decided by `spec`'s vocabulary at stage 3 (concepts 15 spec) — row В63.
- **Waits for stage 3** — decided by eval and cost (Т-13):
  - the route of input: a provider's recognition API, recognition by a stream, or on the device;
  - voicing by a provider — if the device's speech synthesis is not enough;
  - the eval sets of voice. For recognition the main measure is false alarms by verdict, by analogy with reading: for reading it is the measure of product 10 spec. For pronunciation assessment, the measure of a rubric, as R71 spec for essays, and since it writes feedback to the learner, its set has an item on gender and an item on safety (R70 spec; section 3.6);
  - the providers' terms on sound — they are checked anew before the decision on the route (privacy 5.3 spec);
  - a real-time channel — Т-25;
  - voice input of children — П-5, the lawyer's answer;
  - one capability "voice", or input and voicing apart (concepts 15 spec);
  - the feedback rule for feedback on pronunciation — the same as for essays, or its own (R70 spec).

  To return — at the start of stage 3 (product 11 spec) or earlier: if a module with voice input or a step "pronounce" (concepts 7 spec) enters the plan, or if `app` chooses voicing other than by the device's speech synthesis.

### 3.9. Admission to AI

**2026-11-01** (R57): subsection 2.5 — the admission function (Т-20) — is decided. The admission table is decided by `spec`, in epic E3 of its RUN.md. Here are the function and what it needs of the table: the two parts of the table are `tutor`'s proposal, and the cells below are our reading of privacy 5.1 and 5.3 spec. All this is an input for `spec`, not a norm — row В64. There is no product code yet: the epic after T06 builds it.

*2026-11-06 (T30; R65): `spec` `v0.9.3` recorded the admission table — R96 spec, the folder `admission/` with a version of its own —, and the cells below became its norm, and Т-20 is closed. The function reads the table from `spec.Admission` as the type `admissionv0.AdmissionTable`, and the codes, the refusals and the file of facts follow it (Д98 RUN.md `spec`). Before T30 the text below said "the key itself comes only with the work", and this diverged from architecture 4.3 spec: the key also comes to the key check at connection (Д78 RUN.md `spec`). The code and the rest of the edit of this subsection are done by epic E5; its inputs are under table 2 of RUN.md.* *2026-11-08 (R78): E5 is the current epic, and its inputs are in the epic's description at table 1 of RUN.md. T112 builds the function, T113 and T119 the admission rule of models, the list of the admitted and the choice of a model, and T114 the admission before a call.*

**Code** (T112; R79) — the package `admission` at the root of the module, `github.com/altnovatrail/tutor/admission`: both `api` calls it, before an attempt, and `tutor` itself, before a call. The package is pure — without the providers' SDKs, input and output, and clocks:
- `admission.Platform` reads the table from `spec.Admission` of the Go module `spec` at the tag of `go.mod`, and `admission.ReadTable` any other table of the same version, for example the table of a vector. The table's version is `VERSION` of `spec.Admission`: another version needs other schemas, and the package knows only these. A table that does not pass its schema it rejects, and a broken rule of § 7 of `spec`'s text of the admission table it names by ID — `admission.RuleError`;
- `Table.Admit` answers by § 6 of the same text: the capability's rule, the cell, the cell's facts in its order. The input is `admissionv0.Request`, the table's codes, not enumerations of `tutor`'s own: the vectors name providers of their own, and a closed enumeration would not accept them. An unknown value of any input is a refusal, without a rule and a cell. The answer is the vector's answer and the table's version; in JSON it does for a log;
- `admission.ReadFacts` reads the file of deployment facts — YAML, where one can write why a fact is declared; JSON does too. The form is the `facts` of the vectors, provider → fact → categories, by the schema `declared_facts`, and a provider or a fact that is not in the table is an error. YAML is read strictly: a key named twice, an anchor with a reference to it and the merge key `<<`, through which a key can be silently replaced, are errors, and a second document is too, if it is not empty. An empty document and a document of comments alone declare no facts, nor does an absent file — the zero value of `admission.Facts`; `api` reads the file;
- the tests run all the vectors of answers and of rules and the schema's examples from `spec.Admission`, not from a copy.

The function does not check models: the list of the admitted and the choice of a model are T113.

**The admission rule and the list of the admitted** (T113; R80). A model is admitted by the runner's report `Report` with the verdicts of `module-kit` (the 2026-11-03 amendment to R07 spec), and the bounds, the targets and the repeats are `tutor`'s (§ 13 and 14 of `spec`'s contracts text):
- **the rule** — `report.Admit`, for now only for handwriting reading: assessment by the rubric has no target until the volunteers' essays (R71 spec; П-16). A report admits nothing if the verdicts are not `module-kit`'s, if it refused a sample — a run that refuses a sample does not pass (§ 13 of the same text) —, or if it does not have two readers: a sheet is read by a pair, and the reading of one reader is not the platform's reading (R68 spec);
- **the shares** — false alarm and false "correct" by § 14, but the unit is the answer slot, not the reading: an answer slot is an event if at least one of its readings that is not "unchecked" gave the event. The repeats of one sheet are not independent trials, and at zero events `report.Upper` does not widen the bound for clusters, so the bound is counted by answer slots, with a margin. The measures of § 14 by readings — extra "unreadable" and "unchecked" among them — go beside, without a target;
- **the stages:** "full" — the upper bounds of both shares are no more than 1% (product 10 spec); "author" — not one event in either share, no fewer than 30 answer slots where a false alarm is possible, and no fewer than 20 where a false "correct" is possible: the bound at zero events is about 10%, and it is written beside. *Before the 2026-11-08 amendment to R80 the minimums were 50 and 25: the author stopped at 60 sheets, 35 of them with a correct answer.* The rung "author" does only for the first app, where one author learns (R76 spec): before platform AI opens to anyone else, the model passes the rung "full" on the samples of adult volunteers (П-16) — the 2026-11-08 amendment to R76 spec and the card architecture 3.6 spec, the answer to row В72 (T128, R87);
- **the admission set for the first app** — the author's sheets in `samples/`, the live handwriting of an adult (the 2026-11-08 amendment to R50): `sheets-v1`, `free-v1`, `free-messy-v1` and 54 sheets of the sheet plan `eval/author-sheets.json` by the bank `eval/author-bank.json` — `tutor-eval gen sheets` composed them with the seed 113, and the author writes them with the recipe `just admit-record` into `samples/author-v1`. *2026-11-08 (T126): of the 54 sheets of the plan the author recorded 7, and the run takes the recorded ones, `-part recorded`.* Synthetic sheets give no admission (R50). The published modules have no eval sets yet: on `main` of `modules`, at the release `v0.1.0`, there is not one folder `eval/`;
- **the admission run** — `tutor-eval run -kind reading -reads plain`: only the final sheet without the key at 32 px, each reader in each repeat, Opus too. In the run's record Opus stays marked "reads once", as in the measurement: it is the mark of the strong reader, and the report pairs it with each other one. The decision by the report is printed by `tutor-eval admit`: the rung or why there is none, the shares with their bounds and the SHA-256 of the file, without the IDs of samples;
- **the list** — `admission.Admitted`, ordered by capabilities. An entry is the capability, the models — for handwriting reading a pair, the strong reader first (R68 spec) —, the rung, the report — the set, the file, the SHA-256 and the date — and the fingerprint of the prompt that the report measured: another prompt is another reading (R70 spec). A model refers to the adapter's model — the provider, the ID, the effort, "no thinking" — and copies neither prices nor names. A report on the author's sheets lies only in `samples/`, and the entry names it so. The models for which Anthropic requires the work to be kept for 30 days — Fable 5.1, Fable 5 and both Mythos (sources 1.13) — do not enter the list: the platform AI cells stand on minimal retention (Д98 RUN.md `spec`). A test holds each entry: the model is from `claude.Models`, the effort from `claude.Efforts`, and reading has a pair and the fingerprint of the current prompt. The first entry is the reading pair of the rung "author", T126 (below, "The first admission");
- **the choice** — `Table.Select`: the first entry of the capability not below the least rung — only from the models of providers that can be called: of the own key, or with a platform key (T114; R81) —, for each of whose models `Table.Admit` answers "admitted", and the table's answer for its first model. The least rung is named by the deployment: "author" only where one author learns, as in the first app, and otherwise "full"; any other value chooses only "full". A refusal to one of a pair is a refusal of the entry: no call goes (R68 spec). With no such entry, the table's first refusal for the capability's entries, and if the table refused none of them, `model_not_admitted`, after the table's reasons. `api` makes the same call before an attempt, and `tutor` at a call, and with the same configuration and least stage it chooses the same.

**The first admission** (T126, 2026-11-08; R80 with its amendment of the same day). The admission run of the pair Opus 5.5 · `high` and Haiku 5.5 · `medium` on 60 of the author's sheets — `sheets-v1` 40, `free-v1` 3, `free-messy-v1` 10 and `author-v1` 7 —, 3 repeats each, the final sheet without the key at 32 px, the reading prompt of version 1 with the fingerprint `94452c62…`. 360 calls, $1.68 against a ceiling of $8; no failures and no "unchecked". The log and the reports are `samples/runs/t126-admission.*`, outside git, and here only the numbers:

| Report | False alarms by readings | False "correct" by readings | Extra "unreadable" |
|---|---|---|---|
| Opus · `high` | 0 of 105 | 0 of 75 | 3 of 180 |
| Haiku · `medium` | 0 of 105 | 0 of 75 | 6 of 180 |
| pair | 0 of 105 | 0 of 75 | 9 of 180 |

By answer slots, as the rule counts: the pair has 0 false alarms on 35 answer slots, bound 0.082, and 0 false "correct" on 25 answer slots, bound 0.113. `tutor-eval admit` gave the pair the rung "author", and the reports of one reader nothing: a sheet is read by a pair. The pair is the first entry of `admission.Admitted`, with the report's SHA-256 `f2455454…`. The rung "full" on the handwriting of adult volunteers is ahead (П-16; the 2026-11-08 amendment to R76 spec). *2026-11-08 (T115; R82): the reading prompt became version 2 — with the safety rules for children —, and the admission was repeated by the same run on the same 60 sheets, twice: the review refined the rule about what is unsuitable, and the final prompt was measured anew. The final run cost $1.78, with no failures; the pair again has 0 false alarms on 35 answer slots and 0 false "correct" on 25 — the rung "author" —, and extra "unreadable" are 6 of 180 instead of 9 for version 1. The list's entry now names the report `t115-admission-2` with the SHA-256 `284d8eac…` and the fingerprint of version 2, `c693bfa8…`.* The runner of this run is a build from the working copy, and its report names the runner `v0.0.0`: the code of the run is the commit after T113, not a release.

- **The function** is one pure function of `tutor`: without input and output, without clocks and without storage (architecture 3.6 spec; Т-20). It is called in two places:
  - `api` — before an attempt, when it gives the learner a task. It names the capabilities that the task's check needs — reading, and for an essay also assessment by the rubric —, the AI route and the category. For an own key `api` also names its provider: the key itself comes with the work, and also to the key check at connection, by a call of `tutor` of its own without the learner's content (architecture 4.3 spec; Д78 RUN.md `spec`), and `app` names the provider without the key — row В64. `tutor` chooses the model: for each capability it has an ordered list of admitted models, and the first one that the function admits is taken. By the same choice `tutor` takes the model at the call too, so while the configuration is the same, the call gets the same answer as the attempt. If the AI that the task's check needs is closed to the learner, `api` gives tasks that are checked without AI (architecture 4.2 spec; О-5): the learner does not write work that no one can read. Which tasks these are is decided by `api` and `engine` — row В64. A hint does not need AI: without the model's question the learner gets a fallback text (concepts 10 spec);
  - `tutor` — before each call of a model with the learner's work (architecture 4.1 spec). Here a refusal is a safeguard: for example, if the configuration changed between the attempt and the call. The eval runner and the board do not call it — below.
- **The request's input:**
  - the AI capability — handwriting reading, text reading, analysis of the course of solution, assessment by a rubric, hint or voice (concepts 15 spec);
  - the AI route — platform AI or own key (concepts 14 spec);
  - the provider and the exact ID of the model;
  - the learner's age category — its code: `under_13`, `from_13_to_consent`, `from_consent_to_18` or `adult` (concepts 13 spec; R46 spec). The platform computes the category (privacy 3.3 spec), and `tutor` gets it with the request (architecture 4.1 spec).

  The function gets neither identifiers, nor the country, nor the age in years: for the providers' rules on age the category is enough (R06 spec). What this may cost it — below, "Country and region". A parent's consent is not its input: `api` checks it before the attempt (section 3.6).
- **The deployment's input:**
  - `spec`'s admission table — with a release of `tutor`, from the Go module `spec` at the tag of the submodule (R64 spec);
  - the list of admitted models by capabilities — also with a release of `tutor` (R07 spec);
  - the deployment facts — as a value from `api`, which reads them from its configuration file (below).
- **Checks** — in order, until the first refusal:
  1. "capability × category" — the second part of the table. In `tutor`'s proposal it has one row: voice input only for `adult` (privacy 6 spec; О-6). While "voice" is one capability, the row stands on it and closes voicing by a provider to minors too. This is our inference, until `spec` splits the capability (row В63);
  2. "provider × route × category" — the first part: the cell is closed, open, or open given deployment facts;
  3. the facts that the cell names are declared in the configuration. Only a platform AI cell requires them. With an own key the agreement with the provider is the key owner's (privacy 5.2 spec), and this route has no deployment facts;
  4. the model is among those admitted for this capability and from this provider (R07 spec; architecture 4.3 spec). An own key does not open a model without eval (concepts 14 spec).
- **The answer** is "admitted" or a refusal. A refusal carries the code of its reason — the capability is closed for the category, the cell is closed, a fact is not declared (with the fact's code), the model is not admitted —, and with it the table's version and the cell. The answer holds neither content nor identifiers, and it can be written to logs (R09 spec).
- **Closed by default.** Everything the function does not know gets a refusal: a provider, route, capability or category that is not in the table, and a cell that does not exist. A fact that is not in the file is "no". For minors everything is closed by default (Т-20): only its facts open their cell.
- **Two readers** (R68 spec) — for answer slots whose check gives a verdict; an essay is read by one reader (R53). A pair has one provider, so their cell is shared, and the model is checked for each. The function checks both before the first call: if one is refused, not one call goes to the provider, and the reading gives the refusal whole — the verdict rests on the pair (section 3.7).
- **A refusal at a call** is the outcome "not admitted", separate from an AI failure and from "limit reached", but the learner sees the same as at an exhausted limit (section 3.7). On reading, the verdict "unchecked" and a neutral phrase (R04 spec). Other reasons of "unchecked" are named by concepts 9.1 spec — an AI failure, an exhausted limit —, and row В64 asks to add the admission refusal to them. On the analysis and the hint, the rung's fallback text (concepts 10 spec), and how this goes with the neutral phrase at an AI failure (architecture 4.1 spec) is decided by the detailing of `api`, as for an exhausted limit. On assessment by the rubric, a neutral phrase, and the criteria stay unassessed (section 3.5). The call does not go to the provider, and there is no usage at it. A refusal on platform AI is also a signal to the operator, like a refusal for a limit (section 3.7): with a right configuration `api` does not let such a call through before it.
- **Deployment facts** — a file in git, in the deployment configuration of `api` (R57). Which repository it lies in — `api` or `infra` — is decided by the detailing of `api`:
  - it is edited by a pull request: a change of a fact passes review and is visible in the history. So, by architecture 3.10 spec, at stage 2 a pull request will pin the packages' digests in the configuration of `api`. But the digests are taken when the image is built, and the file of facts is not built into the image: `api` reads it at start, and self-hosting takes the same build with its own file. So `app`, by its item "To decide when detailed", reads the deployment configuration at run time (architecture 3.1 spec);
  - `api` passes the facts to the function as a value. The file's type and its parsing are `tutor`'s, beside the function. No file — all the facts are "no": the cells open given facts are closed, and an adult's own key is open, as in the table below. This does not pass silently: at start `api` tells the operator that there is no file and platform AI is closed. And a file that does not parse is a start error: a typo in a fact's name must not silently close a route;
  - the facts, per provider: whether a DPA is signed (П-3); whether zero data retention is approved, and for which categories (П-3; section 3.6); whether the measures for minors are done (П-15; section 3.6). With children's voice, whether zero data retention covers sound will be added (section 3.8). The vocabulary of facts is set by `spec`'s table — row В64;
  - a fact is declared by the deployment's operator. "Measures done" it can declare only after the answer of the lawyer and of `spec`: the monitoring measure, which falls to the platform, is not done (section 3.6; rows В60 and В61). A measure that cannot be done without data that the platform does not collect (section 3.6) enters the same fact: while it is not done, the fact is not declared;
  - self-hosting writes its own file and declares its own facts (Т-20). Without the file platform AI does not work, and answers by input and by choice are checked anyway: so work the mode without AI and self-hosting without agreements with providers (architecture 4.2 spec);
  - there are no secrets in the file: the platform's keys at the AI providers lie in Secret Manager (section 1).
- **The first app** (R76 spec): the author solves tasks themselves, locally, in the devcontainer, Claude models read the handwriting, and the key lies in an environment variable, as for the check board. There are no children, no deployments and no agreements. By the cells below, platform AI for `adult` requires a DPA, and then the function would close such a call. The author's key is the key of an adult who is both the learner and the key's owner: the call goes in their name (privacy 5.2 spec), and the route "own key" for `adult` is open without facts. How the first app names this route is decided by `api` and `tutor` in the code epic — row В64. And without `spec`'s table the function closes everything for an adult too, as R76 spec recorded.
- **Country and region.** The function does not know the learner's country, and what it needs may depend on it:
  - OpenAI asks for age confirmation beyond the account owner's statement "Where required or otherwise appropriate" (section 3.6), and where the law requires it is decided by П-6;
  - the data region is the open П-11, and with the option "by the learner" the function will need the region.

  Both are row В64.
- **What already follows** — our reading of privacy 5.1 and 5.3 spec and of the registers' recommendations. The terms of OpenAI and Anthropic were checked anew 2026-11-01 (section 3.6). This is an input for `spec`'s table, not a norm: `spec` decides the cells, and whether this is enough is decided by the lawyer.

  | Provider and route | `adult` | `from_consent_to_18` | `from_13_to_consent` and `under_13` |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | Google, Gemini API and Vertex AI, both routes | closed (R47 spec) | closed | closed |
  | OpenAI, platform AI | given a DPA | given a DPA and measures for minors | given a DPA, measures and approved zero data retention |
  | OpenAI, own key | open (О-4) | closed (П-4) | closed (П-4); a family's key has no zero data retention (privacy 5.3 spec) |
  | Anthropic, platform AI | given a DPA | given a DPA and measures for minors | given a DPA and measures for minors |
  | Anthropic, own key | open (О-4) | closed (П-4) | closed (П-4) |
  | other providers | closed until their terms are fully checked (R65 spec) | closed | closed |

  - a DPA is the requirement of privacy 5.1 spec for platform AI. There too is that the provider does not train models on learners' data, and this is a provider's term and a source of the cell, not a deployment fact;
  - privacy 5.1 spec requires zero data retention only where the provider's terms require it: at OpenAI, for under 13 and under the age of digital consent. In the other cases privacy 5.1 spec requires minimal retention, and whether it is a fact of admission is not said. The recommendation of П-3 is stricter: a DPA and zero data retention or an exclusion from the abuse logs at each provider before the first learners' data. Then the fact is needed in all the platform AI cells, and in the vocabulary of facts also the exclusion;
  - for minors privacy 5.1 spec requires terms that explicitly allow them. By our reading, the terms of both providers admit minors only with measures, so in our proposal their cells are opened by the fact "measures done". This is an option of П-15, not its recommendation, and whether to accept it is decided by `spec` and the lawyer (rows В60 and В64);
  - privacy 5.1 spec names the data region a requirement too — above, "Country and region".
- **The eval runner and the board do not call the function.** They send not learners' work but synthetic sheets and adults' samples — the author's handwriting and, later, adult volunteers' — with the platform's key for eval (R24 spec; R43 spec), and they measure models that are not admitted yet: eval itself admits them. This narrows the "before each request" of the card architecture 3.6 spec and of Т-20 to requests with the learner's work: the edit of the card, together with the edit for subsection 2.5, is carried by row В65, and that of Т-20 by row В64. When children's samples go to AI under the agreement of П-3 (R24 spec), their route is checked anew.
- **Tests** — in the code epic, by the table:
  - a case for each cell of both parts of the table — from the table itself, not from a copy of our own;
  - for each fact: a cell open given facts, without one of them, is a refusal with that fact's code;
  - a model not among the admitted, a model of another provider, and an own key with a model without eval — a refusal;
  - an unknown value of each input — a refusal, and no file of facts — a refusal in each cell open given facts;
  - two readers: a refusal to one — not one call;
  - the choice of a model before an attempt and at a call — one and the same model with the same configuration.

  If `spec` gives test vectors of the table, as for the module format (R42 spec), the same test runs them — row В64.
- **What the code epic builds** after T06:
  - the function and its types in the package `tutor`, the outcome "not admitted" for the library's calls;
  - the list of admitted models — ordered, by capabilities. It is a subset of the adapters' models, only those that passed the capability eval set and the eval sets of the modules with the capability (R07 spec). `claude.Models` in `provider/claude` themselves are everything the adapter can call and eval measures, not the admitted ones. The list refers to the adapters' models and their IDs, and does not copy their prices and names;
  - the type of the file of facts and its parsing;
  - while `spec` has no table, the function closes everything, and the tests run it on a test table in `testdata`, not on a copy of the rules. `tutor`'s code has no table of rules of its own: the rule on age does not leave the normative part of `spec` (R57). `spec`'s table is needed before the first learners.

**How it was decided** — the former subsection 2.5, moved here word for word 2026-11-01 (T06; R59):

- **The point:** how the admission function is built: what it gets, where it takes the rules and the deployment facts from, and what it answers. The card does not name this item in "To decide when detailed", but puts the function among what `tutor` does and refers to Т-20 (architecture 3.6 spec). The question "how admission is built" is Т-20 of `spec`'s register, and by its recommendation the admission table lies in `spec`; it is decided by epic E3 «Контракты первого среза» in `spec`'s RUN.md. Here the function itself is decided, and what it requires of the table goes «в реестр spec».
- **What is known:**
  - the recommendation of Т-20: the table "provider × AI route × age category" in `spec`, each cell with a reference to privacy 5.3 spec and the date read. The deployment facts — whether a DPA is signed, whether zero data retention is approved — in the configuration. For minors everything is closed by default; self-hosting declares its own facts. One pure function in `tutor` is called before each request;
  - the admission table is a normative part of `spec`, versioned by tags (repositories 5 spec). Code takes `spec`'s files from its releases: Go code as the Go module `github.com/altnovatrail/spec` at the tag of the submodule, and moves both by one move, and the generated types will come the same way (R64 spec; the 2026-10-28 amendment to R21 spec; Т-7);
  - the function checks both the route and the model: the route is allowed, and the model is among those admitted for the capability (architecture 4.1, 4.3 spec; R07 spec). A learner's data do not go to a provider whose terms forbid their age category (R07 spec);
  - what already follows from the terms (privacy 5.3 spec): Google's models are closed for all learners and for the other parts of the platform — both Gemini API and Vertex AI (R47 spec); an own key of OpenAI does not suit a learner under 13 or under the age of digital consent of their country. An own key for minors is П-4, and its recommendation is forbidden until the lawyer answers for each provider; the recommendation of О-4 is an own key only for 18+;
  - there are four age categories, and their bounds are 13 years, the country's age of digital consent and 18 (R06 spec; privacy 3.1 spec). The platform computes the category, and `tutor` gets it with the request (privacy 3.3 spec; architecture 4.1 spec). Until the yearly re-check the former category holds; it is the stricter one (privacy 3.3 spec);
  - voice input also depends on the age category (privacy 3.2 spec). What voice requires of admission is section 3.8 (R56): the capability "voice" is closed to everyone for now — it has no admitted models —, and before stage 3 the function needs no new inputs; voice input is only for learners 18 and older, until the lawyer has answered on П-5;
  - by the option of П-15, platform AI for under 18 is only at providers whose measures are done (section 2.2);
  - the platform also works with no AI provider at all: an answer by input, by choice, by an edit or by a widget's state is checked without AI — so work the mode without AI (О-5) and self-hosting without agreements with providers (architecture 4.2 spec);
  - the inputs are ready since 2026-11-01: the measures for minors and the deployment facts — section 3.6 (R54), the outcome "limit reached" — section 3.7 (R55), what voice requires of admission — section 3.8 (R56), assessment by the rubric — section 3.5 (R53).
- **Options:**
  - voice: a second part of `spec`'s table — "capability × category"; the capability as a fourth dimension of the providers' table itself; a rule in `tutor`'s code;
  - refusal: `api` calls the function already before an attempt and, when AI is closed, gives tasks without AI, and a refusal at a call is like an exhausted limit; only `tutor` calls the function, and a refusal is a neutral phrase after the learner has written the work;
  - deployment facts: a file in git, in the deployment configuration of `api`, edited by a pull request; environment variables of `api`.
- **Decided** (R57, 2026-11-01): the second part of the table, admission before an attempt, and a configuration file. How the function is built — section 3.9. What it requires of `spec`'s table and of `api` is row В64 «в реестр spec».
- **Task:** T05 — done (R57).

**The admission rule of words** (T119; R86). The words — the analysis of the course of solution and the hint — are admitted by `report.AdmitWords`, by the logs of the hint on the cases of the set of words and the judge's logs beside them, `tutor-eval admit-words -controls LOG LOG…`. There is one stage, "author":
- the judge passes all the control phrases — gender exact, mark right, `wordsjudge.Verdict.Passes` — in a run that went to its end, on the judge's prompt of this build, and judges each hint that came, in the same configuration and on the same prompt, each log by its name and sha256;
- each planned case is asked by one of the logs — a run stopped by the ceiling is caught up by another —, not one call failed, and in each set at least one hint came;
- the logs are on the hint prompt that the library calls, version 2, of one tutor;
- by the judge, 0 gender given away, 0 "off task", 0 "not for the age", 0 picked-up notes, and at least one hint of a sheet with a note judged; by the code, 0 echo of the note;
- a repeat of a past question — no more than 5%;
- the shares of leaks before the check and of violations of the form of R70 spec — not above the upper bounds of version 1 in T95, 0.010 and 0.016.

The list's entries are two, `solution_analysis` and `hint`, of one model, with the rule's output and its sha256, and the fingerprint of the hint prompt. The first run did not admit Opus — measurements 3.16. *2026-11-11 (T127; R100): the second admitted it — both entries, Opus 5.5 · `high`, the rung "author", on the hint prompt of version 3 (measurements 3.16).*

*2026-11-10 (T129; R90): the admission table is of version 0.2 (R121–R123 spec). The cells are the same, and the descriptions of the facts are new: `data_processing_agreement` holds when the agreement is in force — signed, or part of the terms that the platform accepted —, and `minimal_retention` does not hold until the provider has approved zero data retention, and until then platform AI is closed to everyone. The exception is the deployment of the first app, where only the author learns: it declares `minimal_retention` at Anthropic (the 2026-11-10 amendment to R76 spec). An own key is only for a profile `adult` (R122 spec). The order of a model's admission is the 2026-11-10 amendment to R80: a model of the list of a tag of `tutor` is admitted only once the pull request of `modules` that moves to that tag and runs the eval sets of all the modules is merged (Д80 RUN.md `spec`).*

*2026-11-12 (T131; R102): the admission table is of version 0.3: a seventh capability, `correct_answer_words` — correct answer words —, with rules as for `hint`, open to all four categories (the 2026-11-11 amendments to R96 spec; Д214 DEBT.md `spec`). The providers' cells and their facts are the same. The list of the admitted has no entry of this capability, so the admission function closes it with the table's refusal, and where the table opens it, "model not admitted", until the task of debt Д8 admits a model.*

### 3.10. Request types

**2026-11-01** (T06; R59): what `tutor` gets from `api` for each call, what of it goes to the model, and what it gives back. Here what was decided is brought together — sections 3.5–3.9, R67, R68, R70 and R75 spec and the measurement of E4 (measurements 3) —, not a new design. The request types are `tutor`'s own types (R15 spec), and the diagram of architecture 4.1 spec names not all their fields. The observation is a type shared by `tutor`, `module-kit` and eval, and epic E3 of `spec` writes it (section 1; R68 spec). There is no product code yet: the epic after E1 builds it (table 2 of RUN.md). *2026-11-08 (R78): it is epic E5, table 1 of RUN.md. What all the calls share T114 builds, the reading of a sheet T115, of the workbook T123, of essays T124, the analysis and the hint T117–T119, assessment by the rubric T125, the checks of words T116, the usage T120, and the outcomes "limit reached" and key refusal and the check of an own key T121 and T122.*

**Code** (T114; R81). The root of the module, the package `tutor`:
- **`tutor.New(Config)`** — what `api` creates at start: the admission table, the deployment facts, the least rung — `admission.StageAuthor` only where one author learns, otherwise "full" — and the platform's keys at the providers, as values. The library builds the provider's client itself, by the adapters `provider/claude` and `provider/gpt`; *2026-11-10 (T129; R90): the providers' adapters are given by `api`, in the field `Adapters` — the `claude.Dial` and `gpt.Dial` of those whose models it calls —, and `Choose` takes models only of providers with an adapter, and `New` rejects the key of a provider without an adapter. The root does not import them, and `TestRootImportsNoProvider` keeps it without SDKs (Д185 RUN.md `spec`);*
- **`tutor.Route`** — the shared part of each request: the AI route, the age category and, on an own key, `tutor.OwnKey` — the key with its provider. The key is behind a pointer: it is printed by no format, even as a field of another structure, and does not go into JSON;
- **`Tutor.Choose`** — the choice of a model by the list of the admitted and the admission function, `admission.Table.Select`: `api` calls it before an attempt, and each call before the model. With an own key only the models of the key's provider are taken, on platform AI only those of providers whose platform key exists; *2026-11-13 (R106): before an attempt `api` calls `ChooseCall` by the call kind, not `Choose` — below, "the call kinds of the library";*
- **`tutor.Outcome`** — the outcome: an answer, an AI failure, "limit reached" with a cause (`LimitReason`), "not admitted" with the admission answer, and an own key refusal —, and a `tutor.Usage` for each call of a model that returned: the provider, the model from the answer, the tokens of input, output and thinking, and whether the count is exact. The cache and the price T120 will add, and the recognition of the limit and of a key refusal T121;
- **the call** — "not admitted" before the client and without usage; the models of a pair in parallel, each with the effort and thinking of its entry of the list; the deadline is the context of `api`. The clients of the platform's keys are built once per model, those of an own key per request; a panic of the adapter is a failure of the call; the usage is written for each call that returned with tokens.

**Reading a sheet** (T115; R82) — `Tutor.ReadSheet`:
- **the request** — `tutor.SheetReading`: the PNG of the crop of the visible strokes, as `app` sends it (`SheetWork` of the contract `app` ↔ `api`), the task's open view `open.Task` (R86 spec) and `Route`.
